# U0817 | National - economy: Union Budget and Economic Survey highlights, RBI policy, key data releases
- **Rows:** G-6 - National - economy: Union Budget and Economic Survey highlights, RBI policy, key data releases; S4-7 - National - economy: Union Budget and Economic Survey highlights, RBI policy, key data
- **Exams:** G1-Prelims + G2-Screening | **Tier:** LOW, 1 pp (~480 words) | **PYQs:** APPSC 12, other exams 11, last APPSC year 2024, latest papers 0
- **Keywords:**
  1. *Payment systems and platforms -> launching organisation + features (RuPay, UPI, BHIM, e-RUPI, NPCI platforms) [I+II]
  2. *Banking developments - RBI penalties on banks, mergers, overseas subsidiary closures, new licences, digital currency pilots [I]
  3. *Key data releases -> compiling agency + frequency + base year (IIP, CPI, WPI, GDP estimates, PLFS) [I]
  4. *Growth projections -> agency (IMF, World Bank, ADB, RBI, rating agencies) for India [I]
  5. *Savings and insurance - EPF interest rate, small savings rates, insurance products launched -> company [I]
  6. *Currency events - demonetisation, new note series -> announcing authority [I]
  7. *External sector data - top trade partners, crude oil suppliers, largest sources of grants/aid, FDI source countries [I]
  8. Union Budget and Economic Survey - fiscal deficit target, capital expenditure, tax changes, survey theme, Chief Economic Adviser [I]
  9. RBI Monetary Policy - repo, reverse repo, SDF, CRR, SLR changes, MPC members [I]
  10. *Economic Survey - publishing ministry, Chief Economic Adviser, new concepts and indices introduced (Universal Basic Income, Chakravyuha challenge, Bare Necessities Index) [II]
  11. *Key data releases -> publishing agency + frequency (IIP, CPI, WPI, GDP, PLFS, core sector) [II]
  12. *Union Budget - new funds and schemes announced (infrastructure development funds), fiscal deficit target, tax changes [II]
  13. *Banking and financial sector actions -> institution (RBI penalties, new portals, overseas subsidiary closures, insurance products) [II]
  14. *Landmark economic decisions - announcement date and scope (demonetisation, GST rollout) [II]
  15. *Savings and social-security rates - EPFO interest rate, small savings rate decisions [II]
  16. RBI monetary policy - policy rates, MPC decisions, inflation and growth projections [II]
  17. GST Council decisions - rate changes, collections data, meeting number [II]

### RBI raises repo rate 25 bps to 5.50%
## 1. Decision (7 October 2026)
- Repo: 5.25% → 5.50% (+25 bps); MPC unanimous; chaired by Governor Sanjay Malhotra
- Stance: "Neutral" → "Calibrated Tightening"
- ⚠ First hike since February 2023 (after cuts in 2025)
| Rate | Level |
| Repo | 5.50% |
| SDF | 5.25% |
| MSF / Bank Rate | 5.75% |
## 2. Background
- MPC: 6 members under RBI Act 1934 — 3 RBI + 3 Centre; Governor has casting vote
- Inflation target: CPI 4% ± 2% — set by Centre in consultation with RBI
- Sec. 45ZB, RBI Act creates the MPC; Sec. 45ZA — inflation target (4% ± 2%, set every 5 years)
- Sec. 45ZN — failure to meet it = 3 consecutive quarters outside the band (8 Sep 2026)
- ⚠ Trap: older app notes say "3 months"
### GST collections September 2026 — ₹2.03 lakh crore
## 1. Key facts
- Gross GST: ₹2,03,521 crore (+14.7% YoY)
- Domestic ₹1,37,996 crore; imports ₹65,525 crore
- Refunds: ₹27,001 crore; net GST: ₹1,76,520 crore (+18.1%)
- Aug 2026: ₹1,99,853 crore gross (+14.8%) (2 Sep 2026)
- South: Telangana ₹5,327 crore, 18% — highest September growth in south; Karnataka 16%, Kerala 11%, AP 6%, TN −5%, Lakshadweep −21%, Puducherry −31%
## 2. Background
- GST from 1 July 2017; 101st Amendment Act 2016; GST Council — Article 279A, chaired by Union FM; CGST, SGST/UTGST, IGST
### 57th GST Council meeting — key reform recommendations
## 1. Recommendations
- Remove arrest provisions under GST
- Prosecution threshold ₹1 crore → ₹5 crore
- Simpler registration/compliance; faster refunds; common standards for notices and investigations
- Simpler registration for small e-commerce sellers
## 2. Background
- 57th meeting: Scheduled for 12 Sep 2026, New Delhi, after a gap of more than a year (30 Aug 2026)
- GST Council: Article 279A; 101st Amendment 2016; chaired by Union FM
- Decisions need three-fourths of weighted votes; Centre 1/3, States 2/3; quorum half of members
- Offences, prosecution, arrest in CGST Act 2017; GST is destination-based

### PM-DHARA scheme — Green Energy Corridor Phase-III, 135 GW, 50 GWh BESS
## 1. Cabinet approval
- Approved: 30 September 2026 by Union Cabinet
- Evacuation: up to 135 GW renewable energy via Intra-State Transmission System; completion by FY 2032–33
## 2. Outlay
| Component | Amount |
| 50 GWh BESS | ₹50,000 crore |
| Central Financial Support | ₹54,082 crore |
- ⚠ First time a dedicated battery-storage component in GEC
## 3. Implementation
- Overall implementing agencies: State Transmission Utilities
## 4. Context
- GEC Phase-II in 7 States: Gujarat, Himachal Pradesh, Karnataka, Kerala, Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh — 7,863 ckm lines, 24,388 MVA
- FY 2025–26: ~6,900 GWh clean power restricted
- Apr–Jun 2026: record peak demand 270 GW; ~8,133 GWh solar curtailed
- Targets: RE 295 GW → 500 GW by 2030; 900 GW non-fossil by 2035
## 5. Scheme name
- PM-DHARA: PM–Developing Harmonized and Accelerated Renewable-energy Access — the ₹1,86,405 crore scheme approved by Cabinet; InSTS component is GEC-III
### Bharat Maritime Insurance Pool (BMIP)
## 1. Key facts
- What: India's first collective domestic platform for insuring large maritime risks
- Approved: 18 April 2026; launched: 12 May 2026 (operationalised May 2026)
- Capacity: ₹13,906.50 crore (~USD 1.5 billion); sovereign guarantee: ₹12,980 crore (~USD 1.4 billion)
- Duration: 10 years, extendable to max 15 years
- Administrator: General Insurance Corporation of India (GIC Re)
- Claims: up to USD 100 million from reserves/reinsurance; sovereign guarantee only after reserves exhausted
- Covers: Hull & Machinery, Cargo War, Protection & Indemnity (P&I), War-perils
- ⚠ Eligibility not only Indian-flagged ships — also vessels owned/managed by Indian entities and cargo to/from India
## 2. Achievements (by 7 Sept 2026)
- 3,000 Cargo War, 92 Hull War-risk, 3 P&I policies
- First policy (12 May 2026): New India Assurance → Hoger Offshore and Marine Pvt Ltd (H&M War-risk)
- First P&I policy (30 July 2026): to Shipping Corporation of India by New India Assurance
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say first P&I policy → Shipping Corporation of India on 31 Jul 2026
## 3. Background
- International Group of P&I Clubs: 13 clubs insure ~90% of world's large ships
- Maritime trade: ~95% by value, 70% by volume
- Ports: 12 major, 217 non-major; cargo ~1,668 MMT in 2025–26
- Coastline: ~11,098 km; EEZ 2.4 million sq km; inland waterways >14,500 km
- Fleet (mid-2026): 1,609 ships, 14.33 million GT (~36% growth since 2015)
- P&I premium outflow: ~USD 45–60 million/year
### SME Growth Fund — ₹10,000 crore
## 1. Key facts
- Approved: Union Cabinet — Government commitment of ₹10,000 crore
- Announced in: Union Budget 2026–27
- Form: Alternative Investment Fund (AIF) making direct equity investments — patient growth equity
- Target: viable, scalable small and medium enterprises, mainly manufacturing; clusters in Tier-II/III cities
- ⚠ Not for micro enterprises; Budget also gave ₹2,000 crore extra to Self-Reliant India Fund for micro enterprises
## 2. MSME classification (from April 2025)
| Enterprise | Max investment | Max turnover |
| Small | ₹25 crore | ₹100 crore |
| Medium | ₹125 crore | ₹500 crore |
## 3. MSME data
- Economic Survey 2025–26: MSMEs = 31.1% of GDP, 35.4% of manufacturing output, 48.58% of exports
- Udyam + Udyam Assist (6 Oct 2026): ~9.78 crore registrations; 43.28 crore jobs; women-owned 37.6%
- Trading 4.08 crore, Services 3.77 crore, Manufacturing 1.93 crore
- Other schemes: CGTMSE, PMEGP, PM Vishwakarma, ESDP & ASPIRE, RAMP, ZED, MSME SAMBANDH, National SC-ST Hub
## 4. Approval date and AIF background
- Cabinet approval on 6 October 2026
- AIF: regulated by SEBI under SEBI (AIF) Regulations, 2012; Category I, II, III
- Micro: investment up to ₹2.5 crore, turnover up to ₹10 crore

## 1. Forecasts

- SBI Research: raised forecast 6.6% → 7.3% — on Q1 growth of 7.8% (vs 6.9% a year earlier)
| Agency | 2026–27 projection |
| DBS Bank | 7.3% |
| SBI Research | 7.3% |
| S&P Global | 7.0% |
## 2. Background
- DBS: HQ Singapore; est. 1968 (Development Bank of Singapore)
- SBI: July 1, 1955, Mumbai; successor of Imperial Bank of India
- Base effect: high base makes current growth look lower, low base higher
### UPI Merchant Discount Rate framework
## 1. Key facts
- NPCI MDR framework effective October 15, 2026
- NPCI set 0.4% MDR on P2M payments above ₹2,000 for specified merchant payments (16 Sep 2026); UPI stays free for consumers
- Flat ₹5 above ₹2,000 also covers insurance; cap ₹300 at ₹75,000+
- Sec 10A, Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007: Zero-MDR on UPI since Jan 2020
- Taxation and Other Laws (Amendment) Bill, 2026: let the government notify chargeable UPI transactions (6 Aug 2026)
- Follows govt exemption of UPI payments up to ₹2,000 and RuPay debit cards from bank charges
| Category | MDR |
| Merchant payment ≤ ₹2,000 | Nil |
| Merchant payment > ₹2,000 | 0.4% |
| Essential sectors (railways, telecom, fuel, electricity, education, agri inputs...) ≥ ₹2,000 | Flat ₹5 |
| P2P / P2PM / AutoPay | Nil |
- P2PM (micro-merchants up to ₹1 lakh/month) — moved to P2M if over limit for 3 consecutive months
- MDR split: payer bank 40%, payee bank 30%, UPI app (TPAP) 20%, PSP 10%
- 5% of MDR collections to a small-merchant adoption fund
## 2. Data
- P2P ~37% + merchant ≤ ₹2,000 60.5% = 97.5% volume free; P2M > ₹2,000 = 2.5% volume but ~two-thirds of merchant value
- 2025–26: 24,000 crore+ UPI txns worth ₹314 lakh crore
- Yes Bank — payer bank in 50%+ txns (ICICI ~18.3%), payee ~55% (Axis ~19%); PhonePe ~46%, Google Pay ~32%
## 3. Background — NPCI
- In-principle approval by RBI DPSS 24 September 2009; took NFS from IDRBT Hyderabad on 14 December 2009
- Initiative of RBI and IBA; not-for-profit — Section 8, Companies Act 2013 (earlier Section 25, 1956); under PSS Act 2007
- NPCI: Set up 2008; promoted by RBI + Indian Banks' Association (IBA); a Section 8 (not-for-profit) company
- Products: UPI, IMPS, RuPay, BHIM, BBPS, NETC FASTag, NCMC; also Bharat BillPay
- NPCI's fast-payment platform = "Vajra" (asked 2018)
- RuPay: Launched 2012 by NPCI (asked 2018, 2019)
- Systems: UPI, RuPay, IMPS, NACH, AePS, APBS, NFS, NETC FASTag
### UPI launched in Uzbekistan
## 1. News
- NPCI International Payments Ltd (NIPL) tied up with Uzbekistan's National Interbank Processing Center (NIPC) — announced during PM Modi's visit
- Indians scan UZQR codes at merchants and pay from Indian bank accounts — UPI linked to Uzbekistan's domestic QR system
- Uzbekistan = 11th country to accept UPI via NPCI International Payments Ltd (NIPL) (1 Sep 2026); Bhutan = the first
## 2. Background
- UPI: 2016, by NPCI, regulated by RBI; NIPL — NPCI subsidiary taking UPI/RuPay abroad
- Uzbekistan: capital Tashkent; currency Som; President Shavkat Mirziyoyev; ⚠ one of the world's two doubly landlocked countries (with Liechtenstein)
### ECLGS 5.0 — Emergency Credit Line Guarantee Scheme
## 1. Key facts
- Approved: 2026; facilitates additional credit up to ₹2.55 lakh crore
- Implemented by: National Credit Guarantee Trustee Company (NCGTC) via Member Lending Institutions
- Valid till: March 31, 2027 or until ₹2.55 lakh crore guarantees, whichever earlier
- Eligibility: working capital with MLIs as on March 31, 2026; overdue ≤ 60 days
- Access: Jan Samarth Portal; ⚠ guarantee is not a loan waiver
| Feature | MSMEs / non-MSMEs | Scheduled passenger airlines |
| Guarantee cover | 100% MSME / 90% non-MSME | 90% |
| Extra credit | 20% of peak working capital (Q4 FY 2025–26) | Up to 100% of eligible base |
| Cap | ₹100 crore per borrower | ₹1,500 crore (promoter equity beyond ₹1,000 crore) |
| Interest | Benchmark + 0.75 pp, ceiling 9%; NBFC max 13% | Lender's board policy |
| Tenure | 5 years incl. 1-year moratorium | 7 years incl. 2-year moratorium |
| Guarantee fee | Nil | Nil |
- Bank benchmark: EBLR for MSMEs, MCLR for non-MSMEs
- Overlap adjusted with Credit Guarantee Scheme for Exporters (CGSE)
## 2. Evolution
| Phase | Coverage |
| 1.0 (2020) | MSMEs, Mudra — Aatmanirbhar Bharat package (COVID) |
| 4.0 | Hospitals, oxygen facilities |
| 5.0 | External disruptions — MSMEs, non-MSMEs, airlines |
- Earlier phases ended in 2023
### USD 1 billion from IFC for MSME credit
## 1. News
- Union Finance Ministry mobilised USD 1 billion from the International Finance Corporation (IFC) (September 2026) — long-term credit to small businesses via Indian banks/FIs
- Supplements MSME credit under ECLGS (launched 2020, Atmanirbhar Bharat package)
## 2. Background
- IFC: est. 1956, HQ Washington, D.C. — World Bank Group's private-sector arm
- World Bank Group (5): IBRD, IDA, IFC, MIGA, ICSID

### Samudra Manthan — National Offshore Exploration Scheme
## 1. Scheme
- Approved: August 2026 by Union Cabinet; Central Sector Scheme of Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
- Outlay: ₹84,084 crore up to 2030-31 — ⚠ oil and gas, not seabed minerals (differs from Sagar Manthan vessel)
| Component | Allocation |
| Offshore seismic data | ₹28,534 crore |
| Common offshore infrastructure | ₹10,000 crore |
| Manufacturing and services zones | ₹2,000 crore |
- Support: up to 50% of drilling cost or ₹675 crore/well, whichever lower
- Targets: 600 MT+ reserve accretion; output 62 → 80 mtoe/yr; resource base 1,600 → 2,200 mtoe
### Decentralised Grain Storage Plan in the Cooperative Sector
## 1. Key facts
- Pilot launched: 31 May 2023 — "world's largest grain storage plan" in cooperative sector via PACS
- Implementing agency: National Cooperative Development Corporation (NCDC)
- By July 2026: 1,012 PACS identified; godowns completed in 313 PACS; 1.80 LMT+ capacity
- Pilot: 11 PACS in 11 States, 9,750 MT (Maharashtra, UP, Gujarat, Rajasthan, MP, Uttarakhand, TN, Telangana, Assam, Karnataka, Tripura)
- PACS selected by: District Cooperative Development Committees under SOP "Margdarshika"; leased land > 20 years
- Silos: 50 / 100 / 200 MT; standards of WDRA
## 2. Schemes converged
| Scheme | Support |
| AIF (Agriculture Infrastructure Fund) | Financing + 3% interest subvention |
| AMI (Agricultural Marketing Infrastructure) | Back-ended capital subsidy — raised 25% → 33.33% |
| SMAM | Machinery, Custom Hiring Centres |
| PMFME | Micro food-processing units |
- Margin money: cut 20% → 10%; NABARD refinance — effective interest as low as 1%; NABARD = AMI subsidy-channelising agency
- FCI allowed assured hiring of godowns
- Case study: Nerpingalai PACS, Amravati (Maharashtra) — 3,000 MT warehouse; ~300 farmers; ~32,000 bags of soybean
## 3. Background
- Foodgrain output 2025-26 (3rd Advance Estimates): 376.563 million tonnes — up 18.8 MT (5.3%) from 357.732 MT
- NFSA 2013 covers ~80 crore people
### Incentive Scheme for Promotion of Domestic PNG Connections
## 1. Scheme
- Launched: August 18, 2026; in effect: September 1, 2026; 2 tranches over 6 months
- Incentive: 200 SCM of domestic APM gas per incremental active, billed household PNG connection above target
- Payback on a connection: ~10 years → ~3 years
## 2. Status and related steps

## 1. Recommendations

- Simpler registration/compliance; faster refunds; common standards for notices and investigations
- Simpler registration for small e-commerce sellers

### UPI Merchant Discount Rate framework
## 1. Key facts
- NPCI MDR framework effective October 15, 2026
- NPCI set 0.4% MDR on P2M payments above ₹2,000 for specified merchant payments (16 Sep 2026); UPI stays free for consumers
- Flat ₹5 above ₹2,000 also covers insurance; cap ₹300 at ₹75,000+
- Sec 10A, Payment and Settlement Systems Act, 2007: Zero-MDR on UPI since Jan 2020
- Taxation and Other Laws (Amendment) Bill, 2026: let the government notify chargeable UPI transactions (6 Aug 2026)
- Follows govt exemption of UPI payments up to ₹2,000 and RuPay debit cards from bank charges
| Category | MDR |
| Merchant payment ≤ ₹2,000 | Nil |
| Merchant payment > ₹2,000 | 0.4% |
| Essential sectors (railways, telecom, fuel, electricity, education, agri inputs...) ≥ ₹2,000 | Flat ₹5 |
| P2P / P2PM / AutoPay | Nil |
- P2PM (micro-merchants up to ₹1 lakh/month) — moved to P2M if over limit for 3 consecutive months
- MDR split: payer bank 40%, payee bank 30%, UPI app (TPAP) 20%, PSP 10%
- 5% of MDR collections to a small-merchant adoption fund
## 2. Data
- P2P ~37% + merchant ≤ ₹2,000 60.5% = 97.5% volume free; P2M > ₹2,000 = 2.5% volume but ~two-thirds of merchant value
- 2025–26: 24,000 crore+ UPI txns worth ₹314 lakh crore
- Yes Bank — payer bank in 50%+ txns (ICICI ~18.3%), payee ~55% (Axis ~19%); PhonePe ~46%, Google Pay ~32%
## 3. Background — NPCI
- In-principle approval by RBI DPSS 24 September 2009; took NFS from IDRBT Hyderabad on 14 December 2009
- Initiative of RBI and IBA; not-for-profit — Section 8, Companies Act 2013 (earlier Section 25, 1956); under PSS Act 2007
- NPCI: Set up 2008; promoted by RBI + Indian Banks' Association (IBA); a Section 8 (not-for-profit) company
- Products: UPI, IMPS, RuPay, BHIM, BBPS, NETC FASTag, NCMC; also Bharat BillPay
- NPCI's fast-payment platform = "Vajra" (asked 2018)
- RuPay: Launched 2012 by NPCI (asked 2018, 2019)
- Systems: UPI, RuPay, IMPS, NACH, AePS, APBS, NFS, NETC FASTag
### UPI launched in Uzbekistan
## 1. News
- NPCI International Payments Ltd (NIPL) tied up with Uzbekistan's National Interbank Processing Center (NIPC) — announced during PM Modi's visit
- Indians scan UZQR codes at merchants and pay from Indian bank accounts — UPI linked to Uzbekistan's domestic QR system
- Uzbekistan = 11th country to accept UPI via NPCI International Payments Ltd (NIPL) (1 Sep 2026); Bhutan = the first
## 2. Background
- UPI: 2016, by NPCI, regulated by RBI; NIPL — NPCI subsidiary taking UPI/RuPay abroad
- Uzbekistan: capital Tashkent; currency Som; President Shavkat Mirziyoyev; ⚠ one of the world's two doubly landlocked countries (with Liechtenstein)
### ECLGS 5.0 — Emergency Credit Line Guarantee Scheme
## 1. Key facts
- Approved: 2026; facilitates additional credit up to ₹2.55 lakh crore
- Implemented by: National Credit Guarantee Trustee Company (NCGTC) via Member Lending Institutions
- Valid till: March 31, 2027 or until ₹2.55 lakh crore guarantees, whichever earlier
- Eligibility: working capital with MLIs as on March 31, 2026; overdue ≤ 60 days
- Access: Jan Samarth Portal; ⚠ guarantee is not a loan waiver
| Feature | MSMEs / non-MSMEs | Scheduled passenger airlines |
| Guarantee cover | 100% MSME / 90% non-MSME | 90% |
| Extra credit | 20% of peak working capital (Q4 FY 2025–26) | Up to 100% of eligible base |
| Cap | ₹100 crore per borrower | ₹1,500 crore (promoter equity beyond ₹1,000 crore) |
| Interest | Benchmark + 0.75 pp, ceiling 9%; NBFC max 13% | Lender's board policy |
| Tenure | 5 years incl. 1-year moratorium | 7 years incl. 2-year moratorium |
| Guarantee fee | Nil | Nil |
- Bank benchmark: EBLR for MSMEs, MCLR for non-MSMEs
- Overlap adjusted with Credit Guarantee Scheme for Exporters (CGSE)
## 2. Evolution
| Phase | Coverage |
| 1.0 (2020) | MSMEs, Mudra — Aatmanirbhar Bharat package (COVID) |
| 4.0 | Hospitals, oxygen facilities |
| 5.0 | External disruptions — MSMEs, non-MSMEs, airlines |
- Earlier phases ended in 2023

### Jute — Golden Fibre sector
## 1. Data (FY 2025-26)
- Exports: 22,700 MT raw jute (₹225.11 crore); 1.71 lakh MT goods (₹3,155.27 crore)
- Jute + mesta: 88.02 → 94.03 lakh bales (2024-25 → 2025-26, 3rd AE)
- 3.70 lakh workers; 40 lakh farm families; 120 composite mills (Dec 2025), West Bengal 89
- Domestic consumption: 12.05 lakh MT (~94%)
- Top states: West Bengal → Bihar → Assam → Odisha → Jharkhand
## 2. Policy
- MSP 2026-27: ₹5,925/quintal — 61.8% over cost; ~147% over ₹2,400 (2014-15)
- Jute Packaging Materials Act, 1987: 100% foodgrains, ⚠ 20% sugar
- Jute Corporation of India (JCI): sole MSP agency, ~110 purchase centres (incl. Andhra Pradesh)
- NJDP: ₹485.58 crore, 2021-22 to 2025-26, by National Jute Board — components JUTE-ICARE (2015-16) and Jute Diversification Scheme
- JUTE-SMART: e-portal, Nov 2016 — 303 lakh bales of B.Twill bags, value ₹1 lakh crore+, 20+ state agencies
- CWG 2026: Indian athletes wore jute-viscose apparel — fabric by NJB, designed by NIFT New Delhi
- Grow More Jute Campaign: September 1949; after Partition ~80% jute area went to East Pakistan
### Bamboo economy and National Bamboo Mission
- Indian Forest Act, 1927, 2017 amendment — bamboo on non-forest land is no longer a "tree"; bamboo in notified forests still controlled
- Bamboo — subfamily Bambusoideae of Poaceae (grass family)
### India Cine Hub — single-window film permissions

- Film Bazaar — held alongside IFFI Goa

- Madhya Pradesh — Most Film Friendly State at 68th National Film Awards

### India's solar growth and PM Surya Sarovar Yojana
## 1. PM Surya Sarovar Yojana
- Approved: 31 July 2026
- Target: 5,000 MW of floating solar PV projects with co-located Energy Storage Systems
- Minimum storage: 2 hours / 10,000 MWh
- Outlay: ₹5,070 crore; period FY 2026–27 to FY 2030–31
- Sites: reservoirs and industrial ponds; all States and UTs eligible
- Floating solar potential: about 102.18 GWp — assessment by National Institute of Solar Energy (NISE)
- Expected benefit: cuts about 10 million tonnes CO₂ a year; 16,000–17,000 full-time-equivalent jobs
## 2. Solar capacity
- Growth: about 3 GW (2014) to 162.15 GW by 30 June 2026
| Segment | Capacity |
| Large solar projects | 118.79 GW |
- Major solar States: Rajasthan, Gujarat, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Maharashtra
- FY 2025–26: 44.61 GW solar added; distributed solar 16.3 GW (36%) — 7.6 GW PM-KUSUM, 8.7 GW rooftop
## 3. Chronology
| Year | Development |
| 2010 | National Solar Mission launched |
| 2014 | About 3 GW; Solar Parks Scheme (December 2014) |
| 2015 | Solar target revised to 100 GW |
| 2017 | Tariff fell to ₹2.44/unit at Bhadla (from about ₹18/unit in 2010) |
| 2021 | Panchamrit goals at COP26 |
| 2024 | PM Surya Ghar launched |
| 2025 | India became second-largest solar growth market |
| 2026 | 162.15 GW |
## 4. Schemes and infrastructure
- Panchamrit: 500 GW non-fossil capacity by 2030; net-zero by 2070
- RPO target 2026–27: 35.95%
- Solar Parks Scheme: target 20 GW, raised to 40 GW; by February 2026 — 54 parks, 39,188 MW sanctioned
- Andhra Pradesh: 5 solar parks, 4,200 MW sanctioned
- Bhadla (Rajasthan) 2,245 MW; Pavagada (Karnataka); Rewa (MP)
- Green Energy Corridor: evacuated 24,567.8 MW; plan about 44 GW across 10 States
- PLI for High-Efficiency Solar PV Modules: ₹24,000 crore — Tranche I ₹4,500 crore (2021), Tranche II ₹19,500 crore (2022); awards for 48,337 MW; about ₹52,900 crore investment and 44,400 jobs (Sept 2025)
- ALMM (Approved List of Models and Manufacturers): introduced 2019 — quality framework for PV modules and cells
- PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana: launched 13 February 2024; ₹75,021 crore; 1 crore households; up to 300 units free a month — ⚠ world's largest domestic rooftop solar programme
- PM-KUSUM (2019): Component A — plants on barren land; B — standalone solar pumps; C — solarising grid pumps
- 11.49 lakh off-grid pumps; 15.89 lakh pumps via feeder solarisation; 1,726 MW decentralised; 21.77 lakh farmers
## 5. Background
- International Solar Alliance: co-founded by India and France at COP21, Paris (2015); 8th ISA Assembly — New Delhi, October 2025, 125+ countries
- One Sun One World One Grid: proposed by India in 2018; Green Grids Initiative–OSOWOG launched with the UK at COP26
## 6. Update (31 July 2026)
- Solar capacity: 164.59 GW on 31 July 2026 (vs 2.8 GW in 2014); added 37+ GW in 2025
- PM Surya Ghar outcomes: 51.58 lakh households; ₹28,024 crore subsidy; ~19 lakh zero-bill homes; 14.8 GW rooftop
## 7. Cabinet approval details (CA-360)
- PM Surya Sarovar: MNRE; projects sanctioned 2026–27 to 2030–31, support until 2032–33; ₹1 crore CFA per MW; benefits — less land, lower evaporation, better panel efficiency
### Critical minerals and India's mineral security
## 1. Basics
- India has identified 30 critical minerals — lithium, cobalt, nickel, graphite, copper, rare earths
- Top-3 refiners' share (Cu, Li, Ni, Co, graphite, REE): 82% (2020) to 86% (2024)
- China: leading refiner in 19 of 20 strategic minerals (~70%); rare earths >90%, graphite >90%, cobalt ~75%, lithium chemicals ~70%; rare earth export controls in 2025
- EU Critical Raw Materials Act (2030): 10% extraction, 40% processing, 25% recycling, max 65% dependence on one country
- India net-zero demand: ~169 million tonnes by 2070 (51% above current-policy path)
## 2. India's resources
| Mineral | Potential |
| Cobalt | ~44.9 MT |
| Copper | ~163.9 MT |
| Graphite | ~211.6 MT |
| Nickel | ~189 MT |
| Lithium | Karnataka, Jammu & Kashmir |
- Rare earth States: Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, Odisha, Kerala (monazite sands)
## 3. Policy
- National Critical Mineral Mission: 1,200 exploration projects by 2030–31; at least 15 minerals produced; 50 overseas assets
- MMDR Act 1957, amended 2023 — Centre can auction critical minerals
- Budget 2026–27 rare earth corridors: Odisha, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu
- India–US critical minerals and rare earths framework: signed May 2026
- SDG links: 7, 9, 13
## 4. MMDR Amendment Act 2026 — uniform mineral taxation
- New Section 9D: States cannot levy tax/cess on mineral rights or mineral-bearing land except under Centre-prescribed conditions; Section 13 amended for rules
- Why: ~14 levies on mining; some State levies up to 20%; mineral imports ~₹10.12 lakh crore (2025–26); iron ore exports ~₹15,136 crore
- State revenue: ~90% of mining revenue; up 354% since 2014 (₹7 lakh crore+); ~₹1.14 lakh crore in 2025–26
- Auctions (since 2015): 723 blocks in 17 States — Rajasthan 140, MP 127, Odisha 76; record 212 in 2025–26 (36 operational); coal 141 auctioned
- 2025–26 output: iron ore 313 MT; limestone 484 MT; major mineral value up 26.8%
- India's rank: 2nd limestone, 3rd zinc, 4th iron ore, 5th bauxite
- NCMM: approved January 2025, ₹16,300 crore to 2030–31 (₹2,600 crore budgetary); NMEDT 777 projects (255 critical)
- Recycling scheme: ₹1,500 crore, October 2025; Critical Mineral Processing Parks in AP, Gujarat, Odisha, Maharashtra
- MAHA mission: 9 CoEs, ₹210 crore; NMEDT contribution raised to 3%; 51 private exploration agencies; GSI 457 projects (FS 2025–26)
### PM MITRA mega textile parks
## 1. Scheme
- Full form: PM Mega Integrated Textile Region and Apparel Parks — Ministry of Textiles
- Parks: 7; outlay ₹4,445 crore for 2021-22 to 2027-28
- Status: MoUs with all 7 States; 100% land; DPRs ₹13,537 crore; investment interest ₹68,590 crore
- Jobs per park: ~3 lakh (1 lakh direct, 2 lakh indirect)
- Competitiveness Incentive Support: ₹300 crore per park; up to 3% of turnover
- Land use: manufacturing 50%, utilities 20%, commercial 10%
- 5F Vision: Farm → Fibre → Factory → Fashion → Foreign

### India world's top ship recycling nation
## 1. Numbers
- Rank: 1st in 2025 (per UNCTAD) — share 30.1% (2024) to 35.4% (2025)
- Volume: 1.86 to 2.99 million gross tons (~60% growth)
- ⚠ Met Maritime India Vision 2030 target 5 years early
- Alang plan: capacity to ~9 million LDT
## 2. Alang-Sosiya (Gujarat)
- ~98% of India's ship recycling; ~6 million GT capacity; ~3.5 MMT steel a year
## 3. Law
- Ship Recycling Act 2019 — National Authority: Directorate General of Shipping; Rules 2021; Regulations 2026
- Hong Kong Convention: binding from 26 June 2025
- ₹53.5 crore helped 115 yards comply; 35 Alang yards applied for EU list; Shree Ram Vessel Scrap and Y.S. Investments in draft 16th European List
- Ship-breaking Credit Note (Shipbuilding Financial Assistance Scheme 2.0): credit = 40% of scrap value; covers up to 5% of a new Indian-built ship
## 4. Ex-INS Viraat
- Served Royal Navy 27 years, Indian Navy 30 years; decommissioned March 2017; reached Alang in September 2020
### India's energy journey: capacity and security
## 1. Capacity (July 2026)
| Source | Capacity |
| Total installed | ~552 GW (vs 249 GW in 2014) |
| Solar | 164.59 GW |
| Wind | 58.14 GW |
- India's ranks: 3rd in renewable capacity; 4th in wind; 4th-largest crude refiner (258.1 MTPA, 22 refineries); 2nd-largest coal producer and consumer
## 2. Coal
- Production: 609.18 MT (FY15) to 1,040+ MT (FY26); reserves ~4,00,715 MT
- Coal meets ~55% primary energy, 70%+ electricity
- Coal/lignite gasification scheme: ₹37,500 crore, May 2026; target 100 MT gasification by 2030
## 3. Wind and hydro
- FY26 wind addition: 6.05 GW — highest ever; ~28 GW under implementation; 70–80% indigenisation
- Global Wind Day: 15 June; Global Wind Day 2026 Conference in Goa — theme "Wind Energy: From Ambition to Acceleration"
- India–UK Offshore Wind Taskforce: February 2026 (Vision 2035); India–Denmark pact 2019, renewed May 2025
- Small Hydro Power Development Scheme: approved March 2026; ₹2,584.60 crore; ~1,500 MW (FY27–FY31)
## 4. Hydrogen, nuclear, geothermal
- National Green Hydrogen Mission: January 2023, ₹19,744 crore, 5 MMT by 2030; ~8,000 TPA commissioned by Feb 2026
- July 2026: 12 pilots, 70 hydrogen vehicles, 21 routes; ⚠ first indigenous hydrogen train on Jind–Sonipat section
- Nuclear Energy Mission: 100 GW by 2047; 22.38 GW by 2031-32; ₹20,000 crore for SMRs — at least 5 by 2033
- PFBR Kalpakkam (500 MWe): first criticality 6 April 2026
- SHANTI Act 2025 (Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India): private participation under licence; statutory status to AERB
- Laws: Atomic Energy Act 1962 (replaced 1948); Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act 2010 — no-fault liability
- National Policy on Geothermal Energy: 15 September 2025; GSI potential ~10,600 MW from 381 hot springs
## 5. Fuel access
- LPG coverage: 55.9% (2014) to 107.2% (2026); consumers 14.51 crore to 33.18 crore; PMUY refills ~49.21 crore (FY26)
- Samudra Manthan National Offshore Exploration Scheme: approved 31 July 2026, ₹84,084 crore
## 6. SHANTI Act draft rules
- DAE released draft rules: operators keep insurance/financial security until spent fuel leaves pool; liability limit reviewed every 5 years; foreign reactor design must be operational somewhere; single licence for construction to decommissioning
- In-principle approval possible before site/technology clearance — lets applicants start supplier talks, land acquisition
- DAE: est. 1954, Mumbai, under PM; AERB: est. 1983
- Samudra Manthan: Central Sector Scheme of MoPNG, up to 2030–31; to help identify ~600 MMT oil-equivalent reserves; minister Hardeep Singh Puri
### Natural farming push
## 1. Concept
- Natural farming (Prakritik Kheti): chemical-free, on-farm biomass, local cattle-based inputs
- PM appealed for a shift to natural farming amid fertiliser and El Niño risks
| Input / practice | Meaning |
| Bijamrutha | Seed treatment — dung, urine, lime, soil |
| Acchadana | Mulching |
| Waaphasa | Air–moisture balance in root zone |
## 2. Policy
- National Mission on Natural Farming; PM-PRANAM — States cut chemical fertiliser use
- Andhra Pradesh natural farming won the Food Planet Prize 2026
## 3. Regenerative agriculture and scheme data
- Natural farming clusters (Mar 2026): 8.80 lakh ha, 18.19 lakh farmers
- Per Drop More Crop: since 2015–16; ~109 lakh ha by May 2026; subsidy 55% small/marginal, 45% others
- Soil Health Cards: 25.89 crore generated; scheme 2015; 12 parameters; every 2 years
- Agri decadal growth FY16–FY25: 4.45%
- NMNF (2024): ₹4,000 per acre per year for 2 years (max 1 acre); 10,000 Bio-input Resource Centres; 2 CRPs per cluster
- Rainfed Area Development: since 2014–15; ~20 ha clusters; ₹30,000 per family; ₹343.86 crore (2025–26)
- Agroforestry component under PM-RKVY (2023–24): 2025–26 — ₹78.31 crore, 27 States, 176.59 lakh saplings
- Natural farming inputs: Beejamrut, Jeevamrut, GhanJeevamrut, Neemastra, Dashparni
- SDG link: SDG 13, Target 13.1
### India's coconut sector
## 1. India's position
| Indicator | Value |
| Production rank | 1st (31.24% of world) |
| Area rank | 3rd (17.90%) |
| Production 2024–25 | 20,301.22 million nuts |
| Area | 2.19 million ha |
- States: Kerala — largest area (~7.54 lakh ha); Tamil Nadu — highest production; ⚠ Andhra Pradesh — highest productivity
- Exports 2025–26: ₹7,038.35 crore (62% rise; vs ₹25.3 crore in 2001–02); largest export volume — activated carbon
## 2. Coconut Development Board
- Est.: 12 January 1981; Ministry of Agriculture; HQ Kochi; 22 States/UTs; ₹147.21 crore (2025–26)
- Export Promotion Council for coconut products (except husk and fibre)
## 4. Schemes
- Coconut Promotion Scheme: Union Budget 2026–27 — part of ₹350 crore for high-value agriculture (coconut, cashew, cocoa)
- PICCS: ₹42,000/ha; 2025–26 — 18,800 ha, 48,696 farmers, 266 clusters
- Friends of Coconut Tree (climbers), Cocomitra, Kera Kaushal Kendra, Kera Suraksha Insurance; Neera — unfermented zero-alcohol sap
## 5. Background
- Cocos nucifera, family Arecaceae; rainfall 1,000–3,000 mm; ~27°C; called Kalpavriksha
## 6. Extra (CA-360)
- Exports 2025–26: US$794.70 million; previous year ₹4,349.03 crore
- Livelihoods: ~3 crore people depend on coconut, incl. ~1 crore farmers
- Kerala output: ~5,149.87 million nuts; productivity after AP — West Bengal, then Tamil Nadu
- 2025–26: 2,175 ha new area; rejuvenation on 1,672 ha
### Solar irrigation and PM-KUSUM 2.0 design
## 1. Context
- PM-KUSUM installed 2.5 million+ solar pumps in five years; PM-KUSUM 2.0 being prepared
- Gujarat Suryashakti Kisan Yojana: ~100 feeders solarised; feed-in tariff ~₹7/unit; farmers earned ~₹21,900 a year; each offsets ~12.3 t CO₂
- Bangladesh fee-for-service model: solar 20–30% cheaper than diesel, no extra water use
- Groundwater irrigation emissions: 45–62 million t CO₂ a year; farm power subsidies ₹1 lakh crore+ a year; 25 million+ farm pumps
- Water-saving schemes: Punjab "Pani Bachao, Paisa Kamao"; Haryana "Mera Pani Meri Virasat"
### Makhana sector and National Makhana Board
## 1. Basics
- Makhana: *Euryale ferox* — aquatic crop; edible part is the seed (fox nut)
- States: Bihar, West Bengal, Assam; Bihar ~3/4 of India's output and 80–85% of global supply (Kosi basin — Supaul, Saharsa, Madhepura; Mithila; Seemanchal)
- Output: 0.6 lakh MT+; ~40% exported; price ~₹500/kg (2020–22) to ~₹1,250/kg (2025)
## 2. Board and scheme
- National Makhana Board: announced Budget 2025–26; launched in Bihar on 15 September 2025
- Central Sector Scheme: ₹476.03 crore for 2025–26 to 2030–31
- DGFT gave separate HSN codes in 2025
- Exports 2025–26: 7,264.89 MT, ₹192.96 crore — USA 40%, Canada 20%, UAE 17%, UK 10% (top 3 = 77%); highest unit price Germany ($26/kg)
- National Research Centre for Makhana (NRCM): varieties, makhana-cum-fish farming, popping machines
### UPI completes 10 years
## 1. Growth
- Ten years: on 25 August 2026; developed by NPCI
- July 2026: 741 live banks; 2,365.8 crore transactions; ₹29.87 lakh crore
- 2024: ~49% of global real-time payment volume; IMF: world's largest real-time payment system by volume
- 2023–24: ~70% of India's digital payment transactions
## 2. Timeline
| Year | Event |
| April 2016 | Pilot with 21 banks; public rollout August 2016 |
| December 2016 | BHIM app |
| 2017 | Dynamic QR |
| 2018 | UPI 2.0 — mandates, overdraft linking, invoice in inbox |
| 2019 | IPO payments (SEBI); 1 billion monthly transactions (October 2019) |
| 2020–22 | AutoPay, UPI 123PAY (feature phones), UPI Lite, credit cards; 5 billion monthly (March 2022) |
| 2023 | Credit Line on UPI |
- ⚠ Bhutan — first international deployment
- Limits: tax payments ₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh; UPI 123PAY ₹10,000; UPI Lite wallet ₹5,000 (₹1,000 per txn)
- UPI Circle: secondary user pays from primary's account; biometric on-device and Aadhaar face authentication
## 3. Global footprint (Aug 2026) — 11 countries
- Bhutan, Nepal, Singapore, UAE, France, Sri Lanka, Mauritius, Qatar, Cambodia, Greece, Maldives
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say Uzbekistan = 11th country to accept UPI (1 Sep 2026)
- Cambodia: June 2026 — NIPL with ACLEDA Bank; Greece: June 2026 — Eurobank remittances; Maldives: 30 July 2026 — link with Favara system
## 4. Extra
- Launched: 25 August 2016; regulated by RBI; NPCI est. 2008, HQ Mumbai (promoted by RBI and Indian Banks' Association)
- ⚠ Sources differ: other notes say UPI was launched on 11 April 2016 by NPCI
- Uses a VPA; built on IMPS; turned 10 in 2026; 24,161.69 crore transactions in 2025-26
- BHIM (2016): Run by NPCI BHIM Services Ltd; BHIM 3.0 in 2025

- e-Navik: 24x7 grievance-redressal portal for seafarers — supports Maritime Labour Convention 2006 (ILO; in force 2013; India ratified 2015)

- Incentive: CGD entities get 200 SCM of low-cost APM gas per billed domestic connection above the threshold, in two instalments over six months
- India's domestic PNG connections: ~1.74 crore

| Rate (Aug 2026) | Value |

| SDF | 5.00% |
| MSF / Bank Rate | 5.50% |
| CRR | 3% of NDTL (cut by 100 bps) |
| Stance | Neutral since Jun 2025 |

| GDP growth | 6.6% | 6.7% |

- Outlook: Aug minutes hint at a hike; Aug CPI of 4.8% raised the odds of an October 2026 increase (20 Aug & 16 Sep 2026)

### India's first soil texture map (ICAR–NBSS&LUP)
## 1. Map
- ⚠ India's first hectare-level digital Soil Texture Map — also called first village-level soil-resource map
- By: ICAR–National Bureau of Soil Survey and Land Use Planning, Nagpur (est. 1976)
- Soil texture: share of sand, silt, clay — affects water-holding, drainage, crop choice
### World's first IVF calf of Lakhimi cattle (Assam)
## 1. Breakthrough
- ⚠ World's first IVF female calf of the indigenous Lakhimi breed — Assam Veterinary and Fishery University

### Nine years of GST
## 1. What happened
- GST completed nine years on July 1, 2026 (in force since July 1, 2017)
- Registered taxpayers: 66.5 lakh (2017) → 1.65 crore (May 2026)
- 2025: Taxpayer base 1.51 crore
- Gross GST collections: ₹7.4 lakh crore (2017–18) → ₹22.27 lakh crore (2025–26)
## 2. Evolution
| Year | Step |
| 2003 | Kelkar Task Force on Indirect Taxes recommended GST |
| 2006 | Union Budget proposed national GST |
| 2014 | 122nd Constitutional Amendment Bill introduced |
| 2016 | Constitution (101st Amendment) Act, 2016 |
| 2017 | GST in force July 1, 2017 — subsumed 17 taxes and 13 cesses |
## 3. Constitutional framework
| Article | Provision |
| 246A | Concurrent GST law-making power to Parliament and States; Parliament exclusive for inter-State |
| 269A | GST on inter-State supplies — levied by Union, apportioned |
| 279A | GST Council constituted by the President |
- ⚠ GST is a destination-based consumption tax (not origin-based)
- Outside GST: alcoholic liquor for human consumption; petroleum crude, petrol, HSD, natural gas, ATF (until Council recommends); Basic Customs Duty, stamp duty, electricity duty, motor vehicle tax
## 4. Next-Generation GST reforms
- Approved: 56th GST Council meeting; in effect September 2025
- Slabs: mainly 5% and 18%; 40% for luxury and sin goods
- Registration threshold (goods): ₹20 lakh → ₹40 lakh
- Composition Scheme limit: ₹1.5 crore
- QRMP: turnover up to ₹5 crore — quarterly returns, monthly payment
## 5. June 2026 collections (CA360)
- Gross GST June 2026: ₹1,94,812 crore — up 13.9% from ₹1,71,105 crore (June 2025)
- Imports GST: ₹60,038 crore (+34.6%); domestic: ₹1,34,774 crore (+6.5%)
- GST Council (2016, Art. 279A) chaired by Nirmala Sitharaman; implemented by CBIC
### Rising inflation — WPI near 10%
## 1. What happened
- WPI inflation: close to 10% in June (near zero/negative until December) — driven by fuel and power, manufactured products, food supply shocks
- Nature: cost-push / supply-shock, not demand-driven (structural approach of Michal Kalecki)
## 2. Price indices (2026 revisions)
| Index | Detail |
| CPI | Base year 2024; used by MPC |
| WPI | Base revised to 2022–23 (June 2026); compiled by Office of Economic Adviser, DPIIT; to be phased out in five years |
| Output PPI (OPPI) | New, base 2022–23, June 2026 |
| Input PPI (trial) | Base 2022–23 |
| Service PPI | Quarterly — banking, insurance, pension, securities, railways, air passenger, telecom |
- ⚠ Food weight higher in CPI than WPI; WPI excludes services
- WPI: 9.78% Jul 2026 (TH, 15 Aug 2026)
- IIP deflator: Now the new Output Producer Price Index (OPPI) instead of WPI
- ⚠ CPI base = 2024; GDP, IIP, WPI and Core base = 2022-23; WPI and Core = Office of Economic Adviser, not NSO
## 3. MPC background
- RBI Act amended May 2016 — Flexible Inflation Targeting; target set by GoI every five years
- MPC: Section 45ZB; 6 members (Governor chairs, DG, one RBI officer, 3 govt nominees); external members 4 years; quorum 4; Governor has casting vote; meets at least 4 times/year
### Rural credit ecosystem and NABARD 45th Foundation Day
## 1. What happened
- NABARD (est. 1982) — 45th Foundation Day on 12 July 2026
- NABARD RECSS (May 2026): 77.2% rural households higher consumption; 51% only formal credit; 27%+ both
- Ground Level Credit target FY 2025–26: ₹32.50 lakh crore (₹5 lakh crore for animal husbandry, dairy, fisheries)
## 2. Evolution
| Year | Milestone |
| 1955 | National Agricultural Credit Long-term Operations Fund; SBI established |
| 1969 | 14 banks nationalised |
| 1982 | NABARD |
| 1992 | SHG-Bank Linkage Programme |
| 1998 | Kisan Credit Card |
| 2014 | PMJDY (28 August 2014) |
| 2015 | MUDRA |
| 2022 | Jan Samarth Portal (June 2022), e-KCC |
## 3. Institutions
- SCBs: Second Schedule of RBI Act 1934; about 120; rural branches 41,464 (2014) → 56,193 (July 2025)
- RRBs: RRB Act 1976; 28 RRBs, 22,000+ branches, 700 districts
- Small Finance Banks: after Budget 2014–15; 11 operational
## 4. Schemes
- DAY-NRLM: NRLM 2010 (restructured SGSY); renamed 29 March 2016; not in Delhi and Chandigarh; 10.05 crore women in 90.90 lakh SHGs; 50,548 Bank Sakhis; ₹13.28 lakh crore loans
- New coop societies: plan (2023) for 2 lakh in five years; 32,836 registered by 20 January 2026; 61,842 of 79,630 PACS on ERP (10 March 2026)
- MISS: loans at 7%; 1.5% subvention to lenders; 3% prompt repayment incentive → effective 4%; Budget 2025–26 raised KCC MISS limit ₹3 lakh → ₹5 lakh; collateral-free limit ₹1.6 lakh → ₹2 lakh (from Jan 2025)
- KCC extended to dairy, fisheries, animal husbandry in 2019; e-KCC sanction in about two days
- PM Dhan Dhanya Krishi Yojana: approved July 2025; 100 low-performing districts; 36 schemes, 11 Ministries; top districts — Banka (Bihar), Mahoba (UP), Charaideo (Assam), Kishanganj, Tikamgarh
- Jan Dhan Darshak App: 99.92% villages have banking outlet within 5 km (6 March 2025)

### UDAN and Modified UDAN
## 1. Modified UDAN
- Period: FY 2026–27 to FY 2035–36; outlay ₹28,840 crore
- Components: 100 airports from unserved airstrips; 200 modern helipads; O&M support; VGF; HAL Dhruv helicopters and HAL Dornier aircraft
## 2. UDAN background
- UDAN: Ude Desh ka Aam Nagrik — Regional Connectivity Scheme; launched October 2016 under National Civil Aviation Policy, 2016
- First flight: Shimla–Delhi, April 2017
- Fare cap: about ₹2,500 for one-hour / ~500 km flight; funded via RCS levy; lowest-subsidy bidding
- Achievements: 679 routes; 95 airports/heliports/water aerodromes; 1.68 crore+ passengers
- Operational airports: 74 (2014) → 165 (July 2026); India 3rd-largest domestic aviation market
- UDAN Yatri Cafés, Flybrary
- Greenfield airports (PYQ): Donyi Polo, Kushinagar (not Vijayawada)
## 3. Launch details (CA360)
- Launched by PM Modi on July 4, 2026 from Jodhpur, Rajasthan
- 100 airports: ₹12,159 crore; VGF: about ₹10,043 crore
- Civil Aviation Minister: Kinjarapu Ram Mohan Naidu; AAI est. 1995
### White Gold — India's cotton story
## 1. Key facts
- ⚠ India — only country growing all four cotton species; 1st in area, 2nd in production and consumption
- Area: 114.84 lakh ha — about 38% of world area; 62% rain-fed
- Production: 297.24 lakh bales (2024–25); 290.91 lakh bales (2025–26 P); consumption 328 lakh bales; 1 bale = 170 kg
- Exports 2024–25: about 18 lakh bales (0.31 MMT, 3.37% of world); cotton exports US$ 11.49 bn (FY25)
- Farmers: about 6 million; 40–50 million in processing and trade
- Ahmedabad: "Manchester of India"
## 2. Species and staple
| Species | Name |
| G. Arboreum | Asian cotton |
| G. Herbaceum | Asian cotton |
| G. Barbadense | Egyptian cotton |
| G. Hirsutum | American upland — about 90% of hybrid output; all Bt hybrids |
- Bt cotton: genes from Bacillus thuringiensis; against bollworm; commercial in India 2002
- Extra-Long Staple: 32.5 mm and above; Long staple 27.5–32 mm; Short 20 mm and below
- Zones: North — Punjab, Haryana, Rajasthan; Central — Gujarat, Maharashtra, MP; South — Telangana, Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka
## 3. Schemes
- MSP: recommended by CACP; cotton year October–September; CCI nodal agency — 571 centres (2025–26), 152 districts, 11 States
- Mission for Cotton Productivity: 2025–26, five years, ₹5,659.22 crore; 24 lakh ha, 140 districts, 14 States, 32 lakh farmers; target 297 → 498 lakh bales by 2031; 5F vision — Farm → Fibre → Factory → Fashion → Foreign
- Special Project on Cotton under NFSM: since 2023–24; 8 States; HDPS (~40% yield gain), closer spacing (32%+)
- Kapas Kisan App: MSP slot booking; 41 lakh+ farmers
- Kasturi Cotton Bharat: Ministry of Textiles, CCI, TEXPROCIL; long staple 28 mm+, ELS 35 mm+; QR + blockchain traceability; NABL testing
- Cottonseed: about 18% oil; "heart oil" — about 50% PUFA
- 2025–26 production: 290.91 lakh bales = 4.95 million tonnes; about 60 lakh farmers, 4–5 crore in value chain
- CCI: est. 1970, Mumbai; cotton designated "White Gold" in a July 2026 government information paper
### Compressed Biogas and energy security
## 1. Context
- Biogas: mainly methane (50–80%) + CO2 (20–50%); produced by anaerobic digestion; upgraded CBG used like CNG
## 2. Schemes
- SATAT (Sustainable Alternative Towards Affordable Transportation): launched 2018; target 5,000 CBG plants by 2023; only 132 completed by June 3, 2026
- GOBARdhan (Galvanising Organic Bio-Agro Resources Dhan): up to ₹50 lakh per district for community plants; ₹564 crore for biomass machinery; ₹994 crore for pipelines to gas grid
- CBG Blending Obligation: approved 2023 by National Biofuels Coordination Committee — 1% in FY26 rising to 5% by FY29, for CNG (transport) and PNG (domestic)
- ⚠ Blending starts at 1%, not 5%
- Ethanol blending: 1.5% (2014) → 20% by December 2025 — five years ahead of 2030 target
## 3. Global lessons
- Europe, China, USA: about 90% of global biogas
- Germany: Renewable Energy Sources Act, 2000 — maize feedstock boom, later capped
- Denmark: target only biomethane in gas grid by 2030; manure/residues, no food crops
- Economic Survey 2026: maize yield 2.56 t/ha (FY16) → 3.78 t/ha (FY25); maize-ethanol price CAGR 11.7% (FY22–FY25)
### E20 petrol — no plan beyond 20%
## 1. What happened
- Centre defended E20 (petrol with 20% ethanol) — tested, higher octane, cleaner combustion
- ⚠ No proposal at present to raise blending beyond 20% — future move depends on scientific studies and consultations
- Feedstock: maize, damaged grain, biomass
### Heat stress hits milk production — Haryana study
## 1. What happened
- Study: published in Scientific Reports — climate change cutting milk yield in Haryana (trans-Gangetic plains)
- Data: 2004–2019, 1,148 villages; 4.66 million cross-bred cattle, 2.86 million indigenous cattle, 35.56 million buffaloes
- Threshold: temperature above 38°C with humidity above 70% — worst in July–August; winter impact negligible
- Resilient breeds: Sahiwal, Hariana
- Heat stress can cut yield by 20–30%
- Current loss: about 3.2 million tonnes of milk, worth ₹2,661 crore; could reach 15 million tonnes by 2050s
- 80 million smallholder dairy farmers
## 2. Milk production — India
| Indicator | 2014-15 | 2024-25 | Growth |
| Milk production | 146.3 MMT | 247.87 MMT | 69.41% |
- ⚠ India is the world's largest milk producer
## 3. Breeds
| Breed | Type | Origin / note |
| Gir | Indigenous | Gir forests, Kathiawar, Gujarat; 1200–1800 kg/lactation |
| Red Sindhi | Indigenous | Karachi / Hyderabad (now Pakistan) |
| Sahiwal | Indigenous | Montgomery (now Pakistan); 1400–2500 kg |
| Jersey | Exotic | Jersey Island, UK — smallest dairy breed; about 4.5% fat |
| Holstein Friesian | Exotic | Friesland, Netherlands — largest dairy breed; 6000–7000 kg |
| Brown Swiss | Exotic | Switzerland; Karan Swiss = Brown Swiss × Sahiwal at NDRI Karnal |
| Red Dane | Exotic | Denmark |
| Ayrshire | Exotic | Scotland — Dunlop / Cunningham cattle |
| Guernsey | Exotic | Guernsey Island near France — golden milk (beta-carotene) |
## 4. Rashtriya Gokul Mission
- Launched: 2014 — Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying
- Sex-sorted semen: female calves with up to 90% accuracy; indigenous technology cut cost ₹800 → ₹250/dose
- Accelerated Breed Improvement Programme: incentive up to 50% of cost on assured pregnancy
- IVF: for Gir, Kankrej; indigenous IVF media cut embryo cost ₹5000 → ₹2000
- Breeds covered: all registered by NBAGR — Gir, Sahiwal, Rathi, Tharparkar, Hariana, Kankrej, Ongole, Deoni, Nagauri, Red Sindhi
### Life insurers fund government borrowing
## 1. Key facts
- Life insurers hold nearly one-fourth of outstanding Central Government dated securities (RBI, IRDAI data)
- LIC: holds about 19% of Central G-Secs; ₹20.2 lakh crore in Central G-Secs; ₹32.3 lakh crore+ in Govt and Govt-guaranteed securities; 63% of non-linked policyholder funds in sovereign securities
- ⚠ LIC = single largest institutional holder of GoI debt; designated Domestic Systemically Important Insurer (D-SII) by IRDAI
- G-Sec maturities: 10 to 40 years — suit Asset-Liability Matching; insurers are patient, counter-cyclical capital
## 2. Insurance penetration
| Measure | Value |
| Pandemic peak | 3.2% of GDP |
| FY25 | 2.7% of GDP |
| Global average | about 3.0% |
- Initiatives: Insurance for All by 2047; Bima Sugam (digital marketplace); Bima Vahak (women distribution); Bima Vistaar (affordable product)

- Tariff (MFN): 20% → 60% (2020) → 70% (2023); parts of electronic toys (HS 95030091) 20% in Budget 2025–26
- GST on toys: 12% → 5%
- Toycathon: 2021; first e-Toycathon: 2025

- Manak Manthan: April 2026, BIS, on IS 9873 Part 1:2025

### Tourism potential and FAITH demands
## 1. Key numbers
- Tourism share: about 7% of GDP, 9%+ of employment
- International tourist arrivals: 20.57 million (2024) vs 17.91 million (2019) — +14.82%
- Domestic tourist visits: 18.6 billion (2014–2024) vs 6.8 billion (2004–2013)
- Vision@2047: 100 million international visitors by 2047
- WTTC: India's T&T contribution USD 523 billion by 2034 — 4th-largest tourism economy
- Premium hotel occupancy: 72–74% (2025–26); India 2nd-largest hotel investment market in Asia-Pacific (USD 340 million)
- Medical tourism: USD 8.7 bn (2025) → USD 16 bn by 2030; five regional medical hubs proposed
- Sustainable tourism market: USD 44.3 mn (2025) → USD 258 mn by 2035 (CAGR 19.3%)
- Swadesh Darshan: 76 projects, about ₹5,290 crore, 75 completed
- Budget 2026–27: 4,000 electric buses for Purvodaya States
## 2. FAITH
- FAITH: Federation of Associations in Indian Tourism and Hospitality — apex body
- Demands: uniform industry status in all States; 50 destinations in mission mode; Brand Bharat campaign; liberal visas; tax rationalisation (rooms above ₹7,500/night attract 18% GST); single-window clearance
### Markets, payments and loans — July 2026
## 1. Items
- UPI in Greece: launched via NPCI International Payments Ltd (NIPL) – Eurobank partnership; NPCI est. 2008, NIPL 2020
- Nifty500 Ahimsa Index: launched by NSE Indices on July 10, 2026 — companies following non-harmful (Ahimsa) practices; classed Green/Orange/Red; only Green included; free-float market-cap weighting; base date April 1, 2016, base value 1000; NSE est. 1992
- ADB loan: USD 230 million (~₹1,920 crore) for Chennai water and sanitation (AMRUT 2.0, Urban Challenge Fund); ADB — 1966, Manila, 69 members, President Masato Kanda
- Greece: capital Athens; PM Kyriakos Mitsotakis; President Konstantinos Tasoulas

### Green hydrogen roadmap and National Green Hydrogen Mission
## 1. NGHM
- Nodal ministry: MNRE; launched 2023
- Components: SIGHT (Strategic Interventions for Green Hydrogen Transition), SHIP (Strategic Hydrogen Innovation Partnership) for R&D
- 2030 targets: 5 MMT/yr green H2; ₹1 lakh crore import savings; 6 lakh jobs; 50 MMT CO2 avoided/yr; ₹8 lakh crore investment
## 2. Types of hydrogen
| Type | Method | Source |
| Green | Electrolysis | Renewable energy |
| Grey | Steam Methane Reforming | Natural gas |
| Blue | SMR + carbon capture | Natural gas |
| Brown | Gasification | Coal |
## 3. Key numbers
- Steel: Ministry of Steel allotted ₹455 crore for green H2 pilots till FY 2029–30 (replaces coking coal in DRI process)
- Fertiliser: India 2nd-largest consumer, 3rd-largest producer of fertilisers; SECI 10-year contracts — 7,24,000 tonnes green ammonia/yr to 13 plants — saves ~$2.5 billion over a decade
- Green Hydrogen Hub ports: Deendayal (Kandla), V.O. Chidambaranar (Tuticorin), Paradip
- Transport pilots: 37 hydrogen buses/trucks, 10 routes, 9 refuelling stations
- AP link: NTPC green hydrogen hub at Pudimadaka, Andhra Pradesh
- Cost (2026): green ₹397–560/kg vs grey ₹150–200/kg; water 9–11 litres per kg H2
- Delivered cost: midstream + end-use = 70–85% of final cost; SME window for 10 kW–2 MW electrolysers suggested
### BHAVYA Portal — 100 industrial parks
## 1. Scheme
- BHAVYA: Bharat Audyogik Vikas Yojana — 100 investment-ready industrial parks in six years
- Outlay: ₹33,660 crore (Union Cabinet)
- Portal launched by: Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal, New Delhi; developed by NICDC
- Model: challenge-based competitive selection; Centre-State — States give land, Centre infrastructure; NICDC–State 51:49
- Guidelines: released by DPIIT in May 2026
## 2. Park sizes
| Region | Size |
| Hilly / small UTs / NE States | 25 acres |
| Mid-sized States | 100–500 acres |
| Near cities | up to 1,000 acres |
- Testing partners: BIS, Export Inspection Agency, FSSAI
- International enclaves: Japan, Singapore, Korea, Switzerland
## 3. Background
- NICDC: under DPIIT, Ministry of Commerce and Industry; 20 projects in 13 States; plug-and-play greenfield industrial smart cities
### Mission for Cotton Productivity
## 1. Mission
- Approved by: Union Cabinet; outlay ₹5,659 crore; period 2026–27 to 2030–31
- Target: lint productivity 441 kg/ha (TE 2025–26) → 755 kg/ha by 2031
## 2. Bt cotton history
- 2002: commercial Bt cotton approved by GEAC — gene Cry1Ac against bollworm
- 2006: Bollgard II (two stacked genes); Bollgard III / RR Flex not commercialised
| Indicator | 2002–03 | 2013–14 |
| Production | 13.6 mn bales | 39.8 mn bales (+193%) |
| Year | Price control step |
| 2006 | Andhra Pradesh capped seed price at ₹750/packet |
| 2015 | Cotton Seed Price Control Order — trait fee cut 74% |
| 2020 | Trait fee eliminated |
## 3. Productivity gap (TE 2025–26)
| Country | Lint yield |
| India | 441 kg/ha |
- ⚠ India has the largest cotton area but low yield; production falling ~2%/yr since 2014–15; 2025–26 output ~29 mn bales; now imports ~4 mn bales
- PYQ: black cotton soil from fissure volcanic rock; cotton needs 210 frost-free days, 50–100 cm rain

### RBI special FCNR(B) deposit swap facility
## 1. RBI measure
- Banks may mobilise: fresh 3–5 year FCNR(B) deposits till September 2026 (eligible till 30 September 2026)
- Swap window: open till 16 October 2026 at concessional rate — RBI absorbs hedging cost; swap at FBIL Reference Rate, at par, once a week, multiples of $1 million
- Expected inflow: $50–70 billion; banks may need to raise rates by 100 bps (US dollar rates above 4%)
## 2. Data (FY26)
- FCNR(B) inflows: $7.1 bn (FY25) → $946 mn (FY26) — fell 86%; outstanding $33.8 bn
- Total NRI inflows: $16.16 bn → $14.41 bn; outstanding $165.65 bn
- NRE: +$7.94 bn, outstanding $98.56 bn; NRO: +$5.53 bn, outstanding $33.33 bn
## 3. Background
| Account | Currency | Purpose |
| FCNR(B) | Foreign currency | NRI fixed deposits in foreign currency; interest tax-exempt for non-residents |
| NRE | Rupee | Park foreign earnings in India |
| NRO | Rupee | Manage income earned in India |
- Eligible: NRIs, OCIs, PIOs
- FBIL: Financial Benchmarks India Pvt Ltd — benchmark & forex reference rates

### Ethanol programme — sustainability rethink
## 1. Status
- E20 target achieved; Roadmap 2020–25 advanced E20 from 2030 → 2025; National Policy on Biofuels, 2018
- E20 demand: 10–11 billion litres; installed capacity ~20 billion litres (Nov 2025); ~500 distilleries
- Gains: forex saved ~₹1.84 lakh crore; crude cut 302 lakh MT; farmers ₹1.58 lakh crore; CO2 avoided 909 lakh MT
- 2G bamboo plant: Golaghat, Assam
## 2. Concerns
- Maize: poultry uses ~60%; price ₹26–30/kg; distillery shortfall 17 MT; India turned maize importer
- Blends: E10, E20 (20% ethanol), E85 (51–83%, needs flex-fuel vehicle), E100
- E85 facts: ~₹20/litre cheaper; ~61% lifecycle GHG cut; ethanol RON ~108
- 1G: sugarcane, molasses, maize, rice; 2G: rice/wheat straw, bagasse, bamboo
- PYQ: biofuel feedstocks — cassava, damaged wheat, rotten potatoes, sugar beet (⚠ not groundnut, horse gram)

## 1. Fiscal and RBI
- Fiscal deficit 2025–26: target 4.4% of GDP met — actual ₹15.19 lakh crore (97.5% of RE ₹15.58 lakh crore); receipts ₹33.02 lakh crore; expenditure ₹49.05 lakh crore (revenue ₹38.36, capital ₹10.69 lakh crore); FRBM Act 2003
- RBI Annual Report 2025–26: balance sheet +20.6% to ₹91.97 lakh crore; ₹1.09 lakh crore to Contingency Fund; gold holdings +63.8%; domestic investments +44.9%, foreign investments +7.9%; bank frauds value ₹48,021 crore, cases 23,722 → 10,114; (RBI Act Section 53); Contingency Fund — for financial and monetary risks
- Repo rate: unchanged at 5.25% — third consecutive pause (Governor Sanjay Malhotra); MPC — constituted 2016, 6 members (3 RBI + 3 Govt nominees); RBI est. 1935, nationalised 1949, HQ Mumbai
- GST (May 2026): gross ~₹1.94 lakh crore (+3.2% from ₹1.88 lakh crore); GST Council — Article 279A; GST from 1 July 2017 (101st Amendment, 2016)
- CGST ₹37,397 crore, SGST ₹45,143 crore, IGST ₹51,990 crore; taxable supply — goods +26.9%, services +22.2%; refunds ₹27,281 crore; CBIC HQ New Delhi (Ministry of Finance)
## 2. Payments
- UPI May 2026: record 2,320 crore transactions, ₹29.90 lakh crore (+24% volume, +19% value); April — 2,235 crore, ₹29.03 lakh crore; average ticket ₹1,848 (2021) → ₹1,313 (2025)
- UPI in Cambodia: via NIPL + ACLEDA Bank — phase 1: Indian tourists pay by QR at ~4.5 million merchants; next phase: Cambodian tourists can use UPI in India (Cambodia — capital Phnom Penh, currency Riel, King Norodom Sihamoni, PM Hun Manet)
- UPI in Seychelles: MoU between NIPL and Central Bank of Seychelles during PM Modi's visit — UPI by end of 2026
- UPI available in (CA-360 list, 8): Singapore, France, Nepal, Sri Lanka, Mauritius, Bhutan, UAE, Cambodia; NIPL = NPCI subsidiary for international expansion of UPI and RuPay
- NPCI: est. 2008, Mumbai — products UPI, RuPay, IMPS, Bharat BillPay, FASTag; UPI launched 2016
## 3. Investment, trade, statistics
- FDI 2014–15 to 2025–26: USD 843 bn (+169% over previous 12 years); FY26: USD 94.53 bn; 90%+ via automatic route (DPIIT)
- Seafood exports 2025–26: record 19.72 lakh t, ₹73,890.46 crore (USD 8.45 bn); target USD 30 bn by 2031; top buyers USA, China; frozen shrimp ₹49,000 crore+, then frozen fish ₹5,658 crore+ (MPEDA est. 1972, Kochi, Ministry of Commerce); EEZ up to 200 nautical miles
- Horticulture 2nd Advance Estimates 2025–26: 3,777.76 lakh t from 301.51 lakh ha (production +1.90%, area +0.05%); fruits 1,214.75 lakh t (+3.25%), vegetables 2,210 lakh t (+1.47%); potato 598.89, tomato 214.61, onion 307.37 lakh t (MIDH 2005)
- WPI base year: 2011–12 → 2022–23; items 697 → 957; ⚠ solar, wind, nuclear power added to electricity for the first time; paves way for Producer Price Index (PPI); WPI released by DPIIT (Ministry of Commerce and Industry)
- Services Production Index: new index, base 2024–25; first experimental estimates 14 July 2026, then monthly on the 29th with ~60-day lag — like IIP (mining, manufacturing, electricity) for services; MoSPI (MoS (IC) Rao Inderjit Singh)
- World Bank loan: USD 1.5 bn under Development Policy Financing (DPF) — tax, trade and investment reforms, ease of doing business, women's employment, finance for entrepreneurs; supports Viksit Bharat @2047 (WB President Ajay Banga)
- World Food India 2026 Southern Regional Conference: Visakhapatnam, 9–10 July 2026 — ⚠ first-ever regional editions
- India–France trade: USD 15.81 bn (2025–26), target USD 32 bn in 5 years

## 1. Facts

- OMCs: Indian Oil, Bharat Petroleum, Hindustan Petroleum

### India's solar growth and PM Surya Sarovar Yojana
## 1. PM Surya Sarovar Yojana
- Approved: 31 July 2026
- Target: 5,000 MW of floating solar PV projects with co-located Energy Storage Systems
- Minimum storage: 2 hours / 10,000 MWh
- Outlay: ₹5,070 crore; period FY 2026–27 to FY 2030–31
- Sites: reservoirs and industrial ponds; all States and UTs eligible
- Floating solar potential: about 102.18 GWp — assessment by National Institute of Solar Energy (NISE)
- Expected benefit: cuts about 10 million tonnes CO₂ a year; 16,000–17,000 full-time-equivalent jobs
## 2. Solar capacity
- Growth: about 3 GW (2014) to 162.15 GW by 30 June 2026
| Segment | Capacity |
| Large solar projects | 118.79 GW |
- Major solar States: Rajasthan, Gujarat, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu, Andhra Pradesh, Maharashtra
- FY 2025–26: 44.61 GW solar added; distributed solar 16.3 GW (36%) — 7.6 GW PM-KUSUM, 8.7 GW rooftop
## 3. Chronology
| Year | Development |
| 2010 | National Solar Mission launched |
| 2014 | About 3 GW; Solar Parks Scheme (December 2014) |
| 2015 | Solar target revised to 100 GW |
| 2017 | Tariff fell to ₹2.44/unit at Bhadla (from about ₹18/unit in 2010) |
| 2021 | Panchamrit goals at COP26 |
| 2024 | PM Surya Ghar launched |
| 2025 | India became second-largest solar growth market |
| 2026 | 162.15 GW |
## 4. Schemes and infrastructure
- Panchamrit: 500 GW non-fossil capacity by 2030; net-zero by 2070
- RPO target 2026–27: 35.95%
- Solar Parks Scheme: target 20 GW, raised to 40 GW; by February 2026 — 54 parks, 39,188 MW sanctioned
- Andhra Pradesh: 5 solar parks, 4,200 MW sanctioned
- Bhadla (Rajasthan) 2,245 MW; Pavagada (Karnataka); Rewa (MP)
- Green Energy Corridor: evacuated 24,567.8 MW; plan about 44 GW across 10 States
- PLI for High-Efficiency Solar PV Modules: ₹24,000 crore — Tranche I ₹4,500 crore (2021), Tranche II ₹19,500 crore (2022); awards for 48,337 MW; about ₹52,900 crore investment and 44,400 jobs (Sept 2025)
- ALMM (Approved List of Models and Manufacturers): introduced 2019 — quality framework for PV modules and cells
- PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana: launched 13 February 2024; ₹75,021 crore; 1 crore households; up to 300 units free a month — ⚠ world's largest domestic rooftop solar programme
- PM-KUSUM (2019): Component A — plants on barren land; B — standalone solar pumps; C — solarising grid pumps
- 11.49 lakh off-grid pumps; 15.89 lakh pumps via feeder solarisation; 1,726 MW decentralised; 21.77 lakh farmers
## 5. Background
- International Solar Alliance: co-founded by India and France at COP21, Paris (2015); 8th ISA Assembly — New Delhi, October 2025, 125+ countries
- One Sun One World One Grid: proposed by India in 2018; Green Grids Initiative–OSOWOG launched with the UK at COP26
## 6. Update (31 July 2026)
- Solar capacity: 164.59 GW on 31 July 2026 (vs 2.8 GW in 2014); added 37+ GW in 2025
- PM Surya Ghar outcomes: 51.58 lakh households; ₹28,024 crore subsidy; ~19 lakh zero-bill homes; 14.8 GW rooftop
## 7. Cabinet approval details (CA-360)
- PM Surya Sarovar: MNRE; projects sanctioned 2026–27 to 2030–31, support until 2032–33; ₹1 crore CFA per MW; benefits — less land, lower evaporation, better panel efficiency
### Critical minerals and India's mineral security
## 1. Basics
- India has identified 30 critical minerals — lithium, cobalt, nickel, graphite, copper, rare earths
- Top-3 refiners' share (Cu, Li, Ni, Co, graphite, REE): 82% (2020) to 86% (2024)
- China: leading refiner in 19 of 20 strategic minerals (~70%); rare earths >90%, graphite >90%, cobalt ~75%, lithium chemicals ~70%; rare earth export controls in 2025
- EU Critical Raw Materials Act (2030): 10% extraction, 40% processing, 25% recycling, max 65% dependence on one country
- India net-zero demand: ~169 million tonnes by 2070 (51% above current-policy path)
## 2. India's resources
| Mineral | Potential |
| Cobalt | ~44.9 MT |
| Copper | ~163.9 MT |
| Graphite | ~211.6 MT |
| Nickel | ~189 MT |
| Lithium | Karnataka, Jammu & Kashmir |
- Rare earth States: Andhra Pradesh, Karnataka, Odisha, Kerala (monazite sands)
## 3. Policy
- National Critical Mineral Mission: 1,200 exploration projects by 2030–31; at least 15 minerals produced; 50 overseas assets
- MMDR Act 1957, amended 2023 — Centre can auction critical minerals
- Budget 2026–27 rare earth corridors: Odisha, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu
- India–US critical minerals and rare earths framework: signed May 2026
- SDG links: 7, 9, 13
## 4. MMDR Amendment Act 2026 — uniform mineral taxation
- New Section 9D: States cannot levy tax/cess on mineral rights or mineral-bearing land except under Centre-prescribed conditions; Section 13 amended for rules
- Why: ~14 levies on mining; some State levies up to 20%; mineral imports ~₹10.12 lakh crore (2025–26); iron ore exports ~₹15,136 crore
- State revenue: ~90% of mining revenue; up 354% since 2014 (₹7 lakh crore+); ~₹1.14 lakh crore in 2025–26
- Auctions (since 2015): 723 blocks in 17 States — Rajasthan 140, MP 127, Odisha 76; record 212 in 2025–26 (36 operational); coal 141 auctioned
- 2025–26 output: iron ore 313 MT; limestone 484 MT; major mineral value up 26.8%
- India's rank: 2nd limestone, 3rd zinc, 4th iron ore, 5th bauxite
- NCMM: approved January 2025, ₹16,300 crore to 2030–31 (₹2,600 crore budgetary); NMEDT 777 projects (255 critical)
- Recycling scheme: ₹1,500 crore, October 2025; Critical Mineral Processing Parks in AP, Gujarat, Odisha, Maharashtra
- MAHA mission: 9 CoEs, ₹210 crore; NMEDT contribution raised to 3%; 51 private exploration agencies; GSI 457 projects (FS 2025–26)

### India's energy journey: capacity and security
## 1. Capacity (July 2026)
| Source | Capacity |
| Total installed | ~552 GW (vs 249 GW in 2014) |
| Solar | 164.59 GW |
| Wind | 58.14 GW |
- India's ranks: 3rd in renewable capacity; 4th in wind; 4th-largest crude refiner (258.1 MTPA, 22 refineries); 2nd-largest coal producer and consumer
## 2. Coal
- Production: 609.18 MT (FY15) to 1,040+ MT (FY26); reserves ~4,00,715 MT
- Coal meets ~55% primary energy, 70%+ electricity
- Coal/lignite gasification scheme: ₹37,500 crore, May 2026; target 100 MT gasification by 2030
## 3. Wind and hydro
- FY26 wind addition: 6.05 GW — highest ever; ~28 GW under implementation; 70–80% indigenisation
- Global Wind Day: 15 June; Global Wind Day 2026 Conference in Goa — theme "Wind Energy: From Ambition to Acceleration"
- India–UK Offshore Wind Taskforce: February 2026 (Vision 2035); India–Denmark pact 2019, renewed May 2025
- Small Hydro Power Development Scheme: approved March 2026; ₹2,584.60 crore; ~1,500 MW (FY27–FY31)
## 4. Hydrogen, nuclear, geothermal
- National Green Hydrogen Mission: January 2023, ₹19,744 crore, 5 MMT by 2030; ~8,000 TPA commissioned by Feb 2026
- July 2026: 12 pilots, 70 hydrogen vehicles, 21 routes; ⚠ first indigenous hydrogen train on Jind–Sonipat section
- Nuclear Energy Mission: 100 GW by 2047; 22.38 GW by 2031-32; ₹20,000 crore for SMRs — at least 5 by 2033
- PFBR Kalpakkam (500 MWe): first criticality 6 April 2026
- SHANTI Act 2025 (Sustainable Harnessing and Advancement of Nuclear Energy for Transforming India): private participation under licence; statutory status to AERB
- Laws: Atomic Energy Act 1962 (replaced 1948); Civil Liability for Nuclear Damage Act 2010 — no-fault liability
- National Policy on Geothermal Energy: 15 September 2025; GSI potential ~10,600 MW from 381 hot springs
## 5. Fuel access
- LPG coverage: 55.9% (2014) to 107.2% (2026); consumers 14.51 crore to 33.18 crore; PMUY refills ~49.21 crore (FY26)
- Samudra Manthan National Offshore Exploration Scheme: approved 31 July 2026, ₹84,084 crore
## 6. SHANTI Act draft rules
- DAE released draft rules: operators keep insurance/financial security until spent fuel leaves pool; liability limit reviewed every 5 years; foreign reactor design must be operational somewhere; single licence for construction to decommissioning
- In-principle approval possible before site/technology clearance — lets applicants start supplier talks, land acquisition
- DAE: est. 1954, Mumbai, under PM; AERB: est. 1983
- Samudra Manthan: Central Sector Scheme of MoPNG, up to 2030–31; to help identify ~600 MMT oil-equivalent reserves; minister Hardeep Singh Puri
### Natural farming push
- PM appealed for a shift to natural farming because of fertiliser and El Niño risks
### Solar irrigation and PM-KUSUM 2.0 design
## 1. Context
- PM-KUSUM installed 2.5 million+ solar pumps in five years; PM-KUSUM 2.0 being prepared
- Gujarat Suryashakti Kisan Yojana: ~100 feeders solarised; feed-in tariff ~₹7/unit; farmers earned ~₹21,900 a year; each offsets ~12.3 t CO₂
- Bangladesh fee-for-service model: solar 20–30% cheaper than diesel, no extra water use
- Groundwater irrigation emissions: 45–62 million t CO₂ a year; farm power subsidies ₹1 lakh crore+ a year; 25 million+ farm pumps
- Water-saving schemes: Punjab "Pani Bachao, Paisa Kamao"; Haryana "Mera Pani Meri Virasat"
### Makhana sector and National Makhana Board
## 1. Basics
- Makhana: *Euryale ferox* — aquatic crop; edible part is the seed (fox nut)
- States: Bihar, West Bengal, Assam; Bihar ~3/4 of India's output and 80–85% of global supply (Kosi basin — Supaul, Saharsa, Madhepura; Mithila; Seemanchal)
- Output: 0.6 lakh MT+; ~40% exported; price ~₹500/kg (2020–22) to ~₹1,250/kg (2025)
## 2. Board and scheme
- National Makhana Board: announced Budget 2025–26; launched in Bihar on 15 September 2025
- Central Sector Scheme: ₹476.03 crore for 2025–26 to 2030–31
- DGFT gave separate HSN codes in 2025
- Exports 2025–26: 7,264.89 MT, ₹192.96 crore — USA 40%, Canada 20%, UAE 17%, UK 10% (top 3 = 77%); highest unit price Germany ($26/kg)
- National Research Centre for Makhana (NRCM): varieties, makhana-cum-fish farming, popping machines
### UPI completes 10 years
## 1. Growth
- Ten years: on 25 August 2026; developed by NPCI
- July 2026: 741 live banks; 2,365.8 crore transactions; ₹29.87 lakh crore
- 2024: ~49% of global real-time payment volume; IMF: world's largest real-time payment system by volume
- 2023–24: ~70% of India's digital payment transactions

| 2017 | Dynamic QR |

| 2019 | IPO payments (SEBI); 1 billion monthly transactions (October 2019) |
| 2020–22 | AutoPay, UPI 123PAY (feature phones), UPI Lite, credit cards; 5 billion monthly (March 2022) |
| 2023 | Credit Line on UPI |
- ⚠ Bhutan — first international deployment
- Limits: tax payments ₹1 lakh to ₹5 lakh; UPI 123PAY ₹10,000; UPI Lite wallet ₹5,000 (₹1,000 per txn)
- UPI Circle: secondary user pays from primary's account; biometric on-device and Aadhaar face authentication

### RBI keeps repo rate at 5.25%
## 1. Policy
- Repo rate: unchanged at 5.25%; Governor Sanjay Malhotra
- 2026 meetings (Governor Sanjay Malhotra): Held at 5.25% in Feb, Apr, Jun and Aug 2026 (4th hold in a row, unanimous)
| Rate (Aug 2026) | Value |
| Repo | 5.25% (cut by 25 bps in Dec 2025) |
| SDF | 5.00% |
| MSF / Bank Rate | 5.50% |
| CRR | 3% of NDTL (cut by 100 bps) |
| Stance | Neutral since Jun 2025 |

| Core inflation | 4.7% | 4.3% |
| GDP growth | 6.6% | 6.7% |

- Current account surplus: US$2.8 billion (April–May 2026); FDI: US$30.7 billion in Q1
- Outlook: Aug minutes hint at a hike; Aug CPI of 4.8% raised the odds of an October 2026 increase (20 Aug & 16 Sep 2026)
### Rising inflation — WPI near 10%
## 1. What happened
- WPI inflation: close to 10% in June (near zero/negative until December) — driven by fuel and power, manufactured products, food supply shocks
- Nature: cost-push / supply-shock, not demand-driven (structural approach of Michal Kalecki)
## 2. Price indices (2026 revisions)
| Index | Detail |
| CPI | Base year 2024; used by MPC |
| WPI | Base revised to 2022–23 (June 2026); compiled by Office of Economic Adviser, DPIIT; to be phased out in five years |
| Output PPI (OPPI) | New, base 2022–23, June 2026 |
| Input PPI (trial) | Base 2022–23 |
| Service PPI | Quarterly — banking, insurance, pension, securities, railways, air passenger, telecom |
- ⚠ Food weight higher in CPI than WPI; WPI excludes services
- WPI: 9.78% Jul 2026 (TH, 15 Aug 2026)
- IIP deflator: Now the new Output Producer Price Index (OPPI) instead of WPI
- ⚠ CPI base = 2024; GDP, IIP, WPI and Core base = 2022-23; WPI and Core = Office of Economic Adviser, not NSO
## 3. MPC background
- RBI Act amended May 2016 — Flexible Inflation Targeting; target set by GoI every five years
- MPC: Section 45ZB; 6 members (Governor chairs, DG, one RBI officer, 3 govt nominees); external members 4 years; quorum 4; Governor has casting vote; meets at least 4 times/year
### Rural credit ecosystem and NABARD 45th Foundation Day
## 1. What happened
- NABARD (est. 1982) — 45th Foundation Day on 12 July 2026
- NABARD RECSS (May 2026): 77.2% rural households higher consumption; 51% only formal credit; 27%+ both
- Ground Level Credit target FY 2025–26: ₹32.50 lakh crore (₹5 lakh crore for animal husbandry, dairy, fisheries)
## 2. Evolution
| Year | Milestone |
| 1955 | National Agricultural Credit Long-term Operations Fund; SBI established |
| 1969 | 14 banks nationalised |
| 1982 | NABARD |
| 1992 | SHG-Bank Linkage Programme |
| 1998 | Kisan Credit Card |
| 2014 | PMJDY (28 August 2014) |
| 2015 | MUDRA |
| 2022 | Jan Samarth Portal (June 2022), e-KCC |
## 3. Institutions
- SCBs: Second Schedule of RBI Act 1934; about 120; rural branches 41,464 (2014) → 56,193 (July 2025)
- RRBs: RRB Act 1976; 28 RRBs, 22,000+ branches, 700 districts
- Small Finance Banks: after Budget 2014–15; 11 operational
## 4. Schemes
- DAY-NRLM: NRLM 2010 (restructured SGSY); renamed 29 March 2016; not in Delhi and Chandigarh; 10.05 crore women in 90.90 lakh SHGs; 50,548 Bank Sakhis; ₹13.28 lakh crore loans
- New coop societies: plan (2023) for 2 lakh in five years; 32,836 registered by 20 January 2026; 61,842 of 79,630 PACS on ERP (10 March 2026)
- MISS: loans at 7%; 1.5% subvention to lenders; 3% prompt repayment incentive → effective 4%; Budget 2025–26 raised KCC MISS limit ₹3 lakh → ₹5 lakh; collateral-free limit ₹1.6 lakh → ₹2 lakh (from Jan 2025)
- KCC extended to dairy, fisheries, animal husbandry in 2019; e-KCC sanction in about two days
- PM Dhan Dhanya Krishi Yojana: approved July 2025; 100 low-performing districts; 36 schemes, 11 Ministries; top districts — Banka (Bihar), Mahoba (UP), Charaideo (Assam), Kishanganj, Tikamgarh
- Jan Dhan Darshak App: 99.92% villages have banking outlet within 5 km (6 March 2025)

- ⚠ India expected to remain the fastest-growing major economy
