### UDAN and Modified UDAN
## 1. Modified UDAN
- Period: FY 2026–27 to FY 2035–36; outlay ₹28,840 crore
- Components: 100 airports from unserved airstrips; 200 modern helipads; O&M support; VGF; HAL Dhruv helicopters and HAL Dornier aircraft
## 2. UDAN background
- UDAN: Ude Desh ka Aam Nagrik — Regional Connectivity Scheme; launched October 2016 under National Civil Aviation Policy, 2016
- First flight: Shimla–Delhi, April 2017
- Fare cap: about ₹2,500 for one-hour / ~500 km flight; funded via RCS levy; lowest-subsidy bidding
- Achievements: 679 routes; 95 airports/heliports/water aerodromes; 1.68 crore+ passengers
- Operational airports: 74 (2014) → 165 (July 2026); India 3rd-largest domestic aviation market
- UDAN Yatri Cafés, Flybrary
- Greenfield airports (PYQ): Donyi Polo, Kushinagar (not Vijayawada)
## 3. Launch details (CA360)
- Launched by PM Modi on July 4, 2026 from Jodhpur, Rajasthan
- 100 airports: ₹12,159 crore; VGF: about ₹10,043 crore
- Civil Aviation Minister: Kinjarapu Ram Mohan Naidu; AAI est. 1995
### White Gold — India's cotton story
- ★ Egypt — grows the most long staple (ELS) cotton; long, fine, strong fibres
### Heat stress hits milk production — Haryana study
## 1. What happened
- Study: published in Scientific Reports — climate change cutting milk yield in Haryana (trans-Gangetic plains)
- Data: 2004–2019, 1,148 villages; 4.66 million cross-bred cattle, 2.86 million indigenous cattle, 35.56 million buffaloes
- Threshold: temperature above 38°C with humidity above 70% — worst in July–August; winter impact negligible
- Resilient breeds: Sahiwal, Hariana
- Heat stress can cut yield by 20–30%
- Current loss: about 3.2 million tonnes of milk, worth ₹2,661 crore; could reach 15 million tonnes by 2050s
- 80 million smallholder dairy farmers
## 2. Milk production — India
| Indicator | 2014-15 | 2024-25 | Growth |
| Milk production | 146.3 MMT | 247.87 MMT | 69.41% |
- ⚠ India is the world's largest milk producer
## 3. Breeds
| Breed | Type | Origin / note |
| Gir | Indigenous | Gir forests, Kathiawar, Gujarat; 1200–1800 kg/lactation |
| Red Sindhi | Indigenous | Karachi / Hyderabad (now Pakistan) |
| Sahiwal | Indigenous | Montgomery (now Pakistan); 1400–2500 kg |
| Jersey | Exotic | Jersey Island, UK — smallest dairy breed; about 4.5% fat |
| Holstein Friesian | Exotic | Friesland, Netherlands — largest dairy breed; 6000–7000 kg |
| Brown Swiss | Exotic | Switzerland; Karan Swiss = Brown Swiss × Sahiwal at NDRI Karnal |
| Red Dane | Exotic | Denmark |
| Ayrshire | Exotic | Scotland — Dunlop / Cunningham cattle |
| Guernsey | Exotic | Guernsey Island near France — golden milk (beta-carotene) |
## 4. Rashtriya Gokul Mission
- Launched: 2014 — Department of Animal Husbandry and Dairying
- Sex-sorted semen: female calves with up to 90% accuracy; indigenous technology cut cost ₹800 → ₹250/dose
- Accelerated Breed Improvement Programme: incentive up to 50% of cost on assured pregnancy
- IVF: for Gir, Kankrej; indigenous IVF media cut embryo cost ₹5000 → ₹2000
- Breeds covered: all registered by NBAGR — Gir, Sahiwal, Rathi, Tharparkar, Hariana, Kankrej, Ongole, Deoni, Nagauri, Red Sindhi
### Life insurers fund government borrowing
## 1. Key facts
- Life insurers hold nearly one-fourth of outstanding Central Government dated securities (RBI, IRDAI data)
- LIC: holds about 19% of Central G-Secs; ₹20.2 lakh crore in Central G-Secs; ₹32.3 lakh crore+ in Govt and Govt-guaranteed securities; 63% of non-linked policyholder funds in sovereign securities
- ⚠ LIC = single largest institutional holder of GoI debt; designated Domestic Systemically Important Insurer (D-SII) by IRDAI
- G-Sec maturities: 10 to 40 years — suit Asset-Liability Matching; insurers are patient, counter-cyclical capital
## 2. Insurance penetration
| Measure | Value |
| Pandemic peak | 3.2% of GDP |
| FY25 | 2.7% of GDP |
| Global average | about 3.0% |
- Initiatives: Insurance for All by 2047; Bima Sugam (digital marketplace); Bima Vahak (women distribution); Bima Vistaar (affordable product)
### India's toy ecosystem — net exporter
## 1. Key numbers
- Toy exports (HSN 9503, 9504, 9505): US$152.7 mn (2017–18) → US$384.7 mn (2025–26) — growth 151.9%
- Trade surplus: US$152 mn (2025–26) vs deficit US$213.01 mn (2017–18)
| HSN | Category | Exports 2017–18 → 2025–26 |
| 9503 | Electronic and non-electronic toys | US$77.35 mn → US$200.89 mn (~160%); USA largest (US$111.9 mn) |
- Employment (NIC Code 324): 8,685 (2018–19) → 17,693 (2023–24)
## 2. Government measures
- National Action Plan for Toys: 2020
- Quality Control Order for Toys, 2020: BIS certification compulsory for domestic and foreign makers; standards IS 9873 and IS 15644; by May 2026 — 1,786 domestic and 56 foreign licences; exempts registered artisans and GI users
- Tariff (MFN): 20% → 60% (2020) → 70% (2023); parts of electronic toys (HS 95030091) 20% in Budget 2025–26
- GST on toys: 12% → 5%
- Toycathon: 2021; first e-Toycathon: 2025
- e-Toys Lab: MeitY at C-DAC Noida — 1-year training (6 months lab + 6 months industry)
- Manak Manthan: April 2026, BIS, on IS 9873 Part 1:2025
- Toy Biz International B2B Exhibition: 4–7 July 2026, New Delhi
- Districts as Export Hubs: committees in 36 States/UTs; 10+ districts with toy potential
## 3. GI-tagged toys
| Toy | State |
| Channapatna Toys and Dolls | Karnataka |
| Leather Toys of Indore | Madhya Pradesh |
| Thanjavur Doll | Tamil Nadu |
- Toy tradition: about 5,000 years (Harappa, Mohenjo-Daro)
### BHAVYA Portal — 100 industrial parks
## 1. Scheme
- BHAVYA: Bharat Audyogik Vikas Yojana — 100 investment-ready industrial parks in six years
- Outlay: ₹33,660 crore (Union Cabinet)
- Portal launched by: Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal, New Delhi; developed by NICDC
- Model: challenge-based competitive selection; Centre-State — States give land, Centre infrastructure; NICDC–State 51:49
- Guidelines: released by DPIIT in May 2026
## 2. Park sizes
| Region | Size |
| Hilly / small UTs / NE States | 25 acres |
| Mid-sized States | 100–500 acres |
| Near cities | up to 1,000 acres |
- Testing partners: BIS, Export Inspection Agency, FSSAI
- International enclaves: Japan, Singapore, Korea, Switzerland
## 3. Background
- NICDC: under DPIIT, Ministry of Commerce and Industry; 20 projects in 13 States; plug-and-play greenfield industrial smart cities
### Mission for Cotton Productivity
## 1. Mission
- Approved by: Union Cabinet; outlay ₹5,659 crore; period 2026–27 to 2030–31
- Target: lint productivity 441 kg/ha (TE 2025–26) → 755 kg/ha by 2031
## 2. Bt cotton history
- 2002: commercial Bt cotton approved by GEAC — gene Cry1Ac against bollworm
- 2006: Bollgard II (two stacked genes); Bollgard III / RR Flex not commercialised
| Indicator | 2002–03 | 2013–14 |
| Production | 13.6 mn bales | 39.8 mn bales (+193%) |
| Year | Price control step |
| 2006 | Andhra Pradesh capped seed price at ₹750/packet |
| 2015 | Cotton Seed Price Control Order — trait fee cut 74% |
| 2020 | Trait fee eliminated |
## 3. Productivity gap (TE 2025–26)
| Country | Lint yield |
| India | 441 kg/ha |
- ⚠ India has the largest cotton area but low yield; production falling ~2%/yr since 2014–15; 2025–26 output ~29 mn bales; now imports ~4 mn bales
- PYQ: black cotton soil from fissure volcanic rock; cotton needs 210 frost-free days, 50–100 cm rain

### Remittances, FDI and insurance — May 2026
## 1. Remittances
- India received US$137 bn (2024) — ⚠ world's largest recipient and only country above US$100 bn; top since 2010 (was US$53.48 bn in 2010)
- Next: Mexico, Philippines, France; largest source USA
- India 2nd in number of international students (~6.2 lakh abroad); IOM: est. 1951, Geneva
## 2. FDI and insurance
- ⚠ 100% FDI in insurance companies; LIC foreign cap stays 20% — via DPIIT Press Note 1 (2026 Series), subject to IRDAI; aligned to Insurance Laws (Amendment) Act, 2025 ("Sabka Bima, Sabki Raksha")
- Sabka Bima Sabki Raksha Act, 2025: 100% FDI in insurance; inserted Sec 16A (IRDAI Policyholders' Education and Protection Fund, 1 Aug 2026)
- FEMA amendments from 1 May 2026 — firms with up to 10% shareholding from China/Hong Kong may invest via Automatic Route (eligible sectors only); amends Press Note 3 (2020)
- Press Note 3 eased (Mar 2026): firms with up to 10% Chinese stake can use the automatic route
- IRDAI: est. 1999, HQ Hyderabad
### Trade — exports target, CEPA, seafood, gold duty
## 1. Exports and partners
- Target US$1 trillion exports by 2026 and US$2 trillion in five years; exports ~US$863 bn (~5% growth); FTAs with 38 developed countries
- India–Oman CEPA: in force 1 June 2026 — signed December 2025, Oman Royal Decree Feb 2026; ⚠ duty-free access for ~98% of Indian exports; India cuts tariffs on Omani dates, marble, petrochemicals; trade US$10.61 bn (2024–25)
- EU = India's 3rd-largest seafood market (FY26, after US and China) — US$1.593 bn, 18.94%; shrimp leads; MPEDA est. 1972, Kochi
- Bangladesh: India overtook US as its 2nd-largest trading partner — 8.47% vs US 8.46% of trade (Feb 2026; Tk 123.28 bn vs Tk 123.17 bn), per Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics
## 2. Gold and silver import duty

- Gold imports FY26 record US$71.98 bn (+24%) — ⚠ 2nd-largest import after crude; ~9% of import bill
- Aim: curb imports, protect forex, support rupee, reduce CAD

### Rupee depreciation — to defend or not
## 1. Key points
- RBI approach: curb excess volatility, no specific exchange-rate target (BIS paper)
- Forex reserves: ~$701.4 bn on 16 January 2026 — ~11 months of goods imports (PIB)
- RBI defends rupee by selling dollars (absorbs rupees)
- Weak rupee → imported inflation; export gain limited due to import content and price-taker status
## 2. Background
- Forex reserves components: foreign currency assets, gold, SDRs, reserve tranche in IMF
- Expansionary monetary policy does not stop rupee slide; Masala Bonds, easier ECB help
### IBAM vs EU CBAM
## 1. News
- EU CBAM financially binding from January 2026; India got no exemption despite India–EU FTA
- India considering India Border Adjustment Mechanism (IBAM) — charge on exports to CBAM countries so carbon revenue stays in India
## 2. CBAM basics
- By: European Union — carbon cost on imports equal to EU ETS price; aims to stop carbon leakage
- Sectors: cement, iron and steel, aluminium, fertilisers, electricity, hydrogen
- Importers buy CBAM certificates; carbon price already paid at origin can be deducted
## 3. India's tools
- Carbon Credit Trading Scheme (CCTS) — domestic carbon market
- Annex 14-A of India–EU FTA — technical dialogue on carbon measures
### IBC (Amendment) Act, 2026
## 1. IBC performance (till March 2026)
- Realised: ~₹4.32 lakh crore — 116.85% of liquidation value, 94.56% of fair value
## 2. Key changes in 2026 Amendment
- Adjudicating Authority to decide admission within 14 days (record reasons if delayed)
- CoC role extended into liquidation — can replace liquidator
- Guarantor assets may be included; moratorium covers guarantee situations
- Dissenting creditors get at least lower of liquidation value or Section 53 waterfall amount
- Defines avoidance transaction, fraudulent trading, wrongful trading, service provider
- One-time restoration of resolution before liquidation; new creditor-led insolvency process
## 3. Evolution
| Year | Change |
| 2016 | IBC enacted; IBBI set up 1 October 2016 |
| 2018 | Section 29A eligibility, voting thresholds |
| 2020 | COVID suspension; immunity post-resolution |
| 2021 | Pre-packaged insolvency for MSMEs |
- CIRP: 180 days, extendable to 330; supervised by NCLT; appeals to NCLAT

### Energy storage — BESS and pumped hydro

## 3. More data

- Global battery storage: ~270 GW; ⚠ China ~60% of 2025 additions
- Other storage: concentrating solar-thermal (molten salt), compressed-air, flywheel, gravity
### Peak power demand, solar and coal vs El Nino
## 1. Key numbers
- Peak demand: 256.1 GW on 25 April (2026)
- Share at peak: coal 66.9%, solar 21.5%
- ⚠ Record solar addition: 44.61 GW in FY 2025–26 — more than double previous year
- Coal stock: ~200 million tonnes — over 83 days
- IMD: possible El Niño during monsoon (June–September)
## 2. Background
- El Niño: warming of central/eastern equatorial Pacific; part of ENSO; trade winds weaken; every 2–7 years, lasts 9–12 months; weakens Indian monsoon; opposite = La Niña
- Renewable curtailment: cutting RE output despite availability (grid/transmission limits)

### Cost of bringing down inflation — India, UK, US; RBI pause at 5.25%
## 1. News
- Crude above $120/barrel due to West Asia conflict — fresh inflation risk
- RBI paused rate cuts at repo 5.25% in April 2026 (after ~125 bps cuts from early 2025)
## 2. Country comparison
| Country | Response | Outcome |
| US (Fed) | 11 hikes 2022–July 2023 to 5.25–5.50% | No recession; sacrifice ratio ~zero |
| UK (BoE) | Aggressive hikes | Recession late 2023; CPI ~3% in Feb 2026 vs 2% target |
| India (RBI) | Repo 4% → 6.5% (May 2022–Feb 2023) | Held long; cuts from 2025 |
- Sacrifice ratio: GDP lost per 1 percentage point fall in inflation
## 3. India's framework
- Flexible Inflation Targeting since 2016 — CPI 4% ±2% (⚠ target is 4%, not 2%)
- Statutory basis: RBI Act, 1934 amended in 2016 (via Finance Act, 2016)
- Monetary Policy Framework Agreement: February 2015; ⚠ MPC constituted September 2016
- MPC: 6 members — 3 RBI (Governor chairs, Deputy Governor, one RBI official) + 3 Govt-appointed; Governor has casting vote
- India imports ~85% crude (this source)
- Repo kept at 5.25%; FY2026–27 inflation estimate 4.6%; 1 bps = 0.01%
- RBI Mission Saksham — capacity building for urban cooperative banks; ~1.4 lakh participants

### Fertilizer supply shock from Iran war and Hormuz closure
## 1. News
- US–Israel vs Iran conflict; Strait of Hormuz closed since February 2026 — urea, DAP, ammonia, sulphur, LNG imports hit before Kharif 2026
## 2. Key numbers
- India uses ~39–40 million tonnes urea/year
- Domestic urea output 2.5 → ~1.5 million tonnes/month (March 2026) due to LNG shortage
- GCC = ~40% of urea imports and 60%+ of LNG imports
- Kharif 2026 need ~19.4 MT; opening stock 5.5 MT
- Urea import price $510/t (Feb) → ~$950/t (Apr 2026); ammonia, sulphur > $900/t
- Hormuz: shipping lanes ~3 km each way; 17–20 million barrels/day
## 3. Response
- Indian Potash Limited (IPL) tender for 2.5 million tonnes urea (April 2026)
- Diversify ammonia/DAP sources — Morocco, Jordan, Indonesia, Malaysia
- Promote SSP, TSP instead of DAP; Nano Urea, Nano DAP (liquid, foliar); Phosphate Solubilizing Bacteria
- West Asia ~48–50% of proven oil reserves (⚠ not >60%); Suez Canal entirely in Egypt
### Ethanol blending and energy security
## 1. Status in India
- Programme: Ethanol Blended Petrol (EBP) Programme — since 2003; under Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
- E20 (20% blending) achieved in 2025 — ⚠ ahead of original 2030 target
- Forex savings: over ₹1 lakh crore; 700+ distilleries; capacity ~1,700 crore litres
- Ethanol: C₂H₅OH — fermentation of sugarcane, maize, wheat
## 2. Generations of ethanol
| Generation | Feedstock |
| 1G | Food crops — sugarcane, maize |
| 2G | Agricultural residues (crop stubble) |
| 3G | Algae |
| 4G | Genetically engineered biomass |
## 3. Brazil model
- Proálcool Programme — launched 1975 after oil crisis; mandatory blending ~30%; flex-fuel vehicles
## 4. Exam angle
- ⚠ Concerns: food vs fuel conflict; sugarcane is water-intensive; vehicle compatibility
- ⚠ One source's MCQ says E20 not yet fully achieved (target 2025–26); another says achieved in 2025
### Ethanol blending allowed in Aviation Turbine Fuel
## 1. News
- Govt allowed ethanol and synthetic hydrocarbons in ATF — amendment to ATF (Regulation of Marketing) Order, 2001 under Essential Commodities Act, 1955
- ⚠ No mandatory blending target fixed yet
## 2. Key facts
- Sustainable Aviation Fuel (SAF): cuts lifecycle emissions 60–80%; aviation ~2–3% of global CO₂
- ICAO carbon-neutral growth; CORSIA — Carbon Offsetting and Reduction Scheme for International Aviation
- Ethanol has ~27% less energy per litre than petrol; ⚠ not fully carbon-neutral; cuts CO, unburnt HC, PM
- Feedstocks: molasses, cane juice, maize, surplus FCI rice, 2G residues, sweet sorghum
- India exploring E85, E100 for flex-fuel vehicles
### Rupee as a barometer of credibility
## 1. Context (2026)
- Rupee under pressure — BoP stress; CAD financing hard due to negative net FDI inflows and volatile FPI
## 2. Theories
- Dornbusch Overshooting Model: exchange rate overreacts in short run (sticky prices)
- Mundell-Fleming / Impossible Trinity: cannot have fixed exchange rate + free capital mobility + independent monetary policy together; India — managed float
## 3. Exam angle
- 2013 Taper Tantrum (US Fed) — rupee fell; RBI tightened short-term rates, oil-dollar swap; govt raised gold import duty
- ⚠ RBI buying foreign currency → expands domestic money supply (sterilisation may follow)
- ⚠ Higher FDI inflows → rupee appreciates (not depreciates)
- Depreciation → imported inflation, higher external debt burden
- Fowler Committee (1898): Gold Exchange Standard — rupee pegged to sterling at 1s 4d (15 rupees = £1) — for Home Charges

- Union Budget 2026–27: region-specific push for coconut, cashew, cocoa, agarwood, walnuts, almonds, sandalwood, pine nuts (Chilgoza)

### Superior Kerosene Oil via PDS in 21 States/UTs
## 1. News
- Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas gazette notification — Superior Kerosene Oil (SKO) distribution through PDS in 21 States/UTs including Delhi and Gujarat
- Aim: energy access where LPG/electricity access is limited
## 2. Features of SKO
- Smoke point: 18–22 mm; flash point: above 35–40°C
- ⚠ PDS kerosene is dyed blue — prevents diversion/adulteration of diesel
- Uses: wick/pressure stoves, lamps; solvent; similar fractions refined into Aviation Turbine Fuel (ATF)
### Piped Natural Gas (PNG) push and gas-based economy
## 1. News
- Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas reviewed PNG network expansion amid Strait of Hormuz disruptions
- Gas allocation prioritised for households and essential services under Essential Commodities Act
## 2. Supply chain
- LNG imported → regasified at coastal terminals → trunk pipelines → City Gate Stations (CGS) → steel/polyethylene city network → metered households
- Planning integrated with PM Gati Shakti; part of City Gas Distribution (CGD)
## 3. Fuel comparison
| Fuel | Composition / form | Use |
| LPG | Propane + butane, liquid in cylinders; ⚠ heavier than air | Household cooking |
| CNG | Methane compressed at high pressure | Transport fuel |
| PNG | Methane via pipelines; ⚠ lighter than air, disperses fast | Homes, industry |
| LNG | Natural gas cooled to ~–160/–162°C | Shipping/storage form; feeds PNG, CNG |

### LPG shortage and storage gap
## 1. News
- LPG shortage due to West Asia conflict — 85%+ of India's LPG imports pass via Strait of Hormuz
- NRAI warned ~60% of restaurants could shut; IEA flagged lack of storage as a key weakness
## 2. Consumption and imports
- India — second-largest LPG consumer; ~3 million t/month (~80,000 t/day); 85%+ used by households
- Imports: ~20 million t/year (tripled 2011-12 to 2024-25); dependency 47% (2015) → ~60%
- 33 crore domestic connections; ~10 crore added after 2017 under PMUY
## 3. Underground LPG caverns
- Total: 1.4 lakh t — ⚠ less than two days of consumption; MoPNG: no more caverns planned
- Crude/products reserves: about two months; global norm 90 days; EU gas storage must be 90% full before winter
## 4. Storage options
- Depleted gas fields: KG Basin, Cambay, Mumbai Offshore
## 5. Response
- LPG and CNG on priority list under Essential Commodities Act
- IEA release of ~400 million barrels from strategic reserves
- US deal: 2.2 million t LPG/year — shipping ~45 days
- ⚠ Strait pairs: Hormuz–Persian Gulf, Bab-el-Mandeb–Red Sea, Malacca–Andaman Sea, Sunda–Java Sea
## 6. LPG vs LNG
| Feature | LPG | LNG |
| Composition | Propane + butane | Methane |
| Source | Crude refining, gas processing | Natural gas |
| Liquefaction | Moderate pressure — 1/260th volume | Cooled to ~–160°C — 1/600th volume |
| Storage | Cylinders | ⚠ Cryogenic tanks |
| Density | ⚠ Heavier than air | Lighter than air |
| Use | Cooking | PNG, CNG, industry |
## 7. Crisis response
- LPG: ~60% imported, ~90% of imports via Hormuz → ~54% of supply vulnerable; 33.3 crore households
- Gas: ~50% imported, 55–60% of it from West Asia → ~30% of LNG affected; PNG/CNG prioritised over industry
- Refineries maximised LPG — domestic output +40%; domestic share 40% → 55%
- Cylinder booking interval: urban 25 days, rural 45 days; ~60 lakh households can shift to PNG
### Strategic Petroleum Reserves only 64% full
## 1. News
- India's SPR: 3.37 MMT stored of 5.33 MMT capacity (~64%)
- At full capacity → ⚠ only ~9.5 days of crude need
- Total national storage incl. commercial: ~74 days; govt estimate ~8 weeks; ⚠ IEA norm 90 days; India is an associate member of IEA
- Hormuz: 2.5–2.7 mb/d (~50% of imports now; long-term ~40%); India imports 88%+ of crude
## 2. SPR sites (ISPRL, underground rock caverns)
| Location | Capacity |
| Mangalore (Karnataka) | 1.5 MMT |
| Padur (Karnataka) | 2.5 MMT |
- Phase II approved July 2021: Chandikhol (Odisha) 4 MMT, Padur expansion 2.5 MMT; proposed Bikaner, Rajkot
- SPR idea first mooted in 1973 (first oil crisis)
## 3. Background — PNGRB
- Statutory body under PNGRB Act, 2006; quasi-judicial (civil court powers)
- Regulates downstream — refining, storage, transport, distribution, marketing; ⚠ not crude oil/natural gas production
- Appeals lie with APTEL (Electricity Act, 2003)

### Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) Annual Report 2025
## 1. Background
- PLFS launched by NSO in 2017; quarterly urban estimates by CWS; annual by Usual Status (ps+ss) and CWS
- LFPR = labour force / population; WPR = employed / population; UR = unemployed / labour force
- CWS — last 7 days; Usual Status — last 365 days; subsidiary status — 30 days+
- 2025 survey uses a new sampling design and calendar-year format
## 2. Key data

| Urban UR | 4.8% (male 4.2%, female 6.4%) |
| Agriculture share | 44.8% → 43.0% (still largest) |
## 3. Challenges
- 44.4% of females out of labour force due to childcare/domestic work (69.8% of males due to education)
- NEET (15–29): ~25%; formal vocational training only 4.2% (15–59)
## 4. More facts
- Overall unemployment 3.2% → 3.1%; women (15+) 3.1%, men 3.3% → 3.1%; rural 2.5% → 2.4%; casual labour 20.2%
- Self-employment 52% (2017–18) → 58% (Labour Minister Mansukh Mandaviya in Lok Sabha); casual daily wage ₹295 → ₹418
- Four Labour Codes: Code on Wages (2019), IR Code (2020), Social Security Code (2020), OSH Code (2020)

### Electrifying India's kitchens
## 1. News
- LPG import bill: $26.4 billion; ~60% of LPG and 50% of natural gas imported
- 332 million connections but 37% households still use biomass; LPG subsidy ~₹40,000 crore/year
- Electric cooking now cheaper than non-subsidised LPG; share only ~5%
## 2. Efficiency
- Induction ~85% efficiency vs LPG burner ~40%; electric pressure cooker uses least energy
## 3. Peak demand
- Peak: 148 GW (2014) → record 242.5 GW (December 2025); IEA — each 1°C adds 7+ GW
- BSES Rajdhani (Delhi): ⚠ India's first commercial battery storage system
- OpenADR (Open Automated Demand Response) — ⚠ two-way standard; peak cut 7–24%; Tata Power Delhi pilot ~14%
## 4. Distributed energy
- Rooftop solar: 24 GW (2026) → 41+ GW by 2030; PM-Surya Ghar — 300 units free to 1 crore households
- Australia (2025) study: 50% peak cut, 75% lower grid reinforcement costs
- First blockchain P2P solar trading pilot: Lucknow — India Smart Grid Forum + Australia's Powerledger, UPERC sandbox; 43% lower buy price
- Feb 2026: P2P facility under India Energy Stack for Delhi and western UP
- Programmes: Go Electric, National Efficient Cooking Programme (2 million induction stoves), BEE star labels; New York All-Electric Buildings Act (from Jan 2026)
- LPG odorant: ethyl mercaptan (sulphur compound) for leak detection
### Stranded renewable power and grid congestion
## 1. News
- Highlighted at Bharat Climate Forum (BCF) 2026
- Targets: 500 GW non-fossil capacity by 2030; net-zero by 2070
## 2. Rajasthan example
- 4,000+ MW commissioned RE cannot evacuate at peak; installed RE ~23 GW vs evacuation capacity ~18.9 GW
- 765 kV double-circuit corridors designed for ~6,000 MW run at 600–1,000 MW (<20% utilisation); each costs ₹4,000–5,000 crore
## 3. Institutions and fixes
- CTU (Central Transmission Utility) plans corridors; developers get GNA (General Network Access); T-GNA projects shut down at peak solar hours
- Solutions: STATCOMs, dynamic grid management, adaptive line rating, transparent curtailment
- PM Surya Ghar Muft Bijli Yojana: rooftop solar for 1 crore households
### Rice production and exports — need for a rethink
## 1. India's rice leadership
- Largest rice exporter since 2011–12; exported 21.69 million tonnes in 2024–25
- ⚠ Surpassed China to become the world's largest rice producer (150 mt) in 2024–25
## 2. Water footprint
- System: conventional transplanting-cum-continuous flooding; field water level 5 cm; 20–30 irrigations
- One irrigation of 1 acre at 2.5 cm = 101,171.5 litres; total ≈ 5 million litres per acre
- Water per kg: paddy ≈ 2,000 litres; milled rice ≈ 3,000 litres — virtual water export; stress in Punjab–Haryana
## 3. Basmati vs non-basmati
- Non-basmati exports double in volume but earnings similar; basmati earns 2–2.5 times more per kg
- Transplanting: non-basmati in June (peak summer); basmati in July (monsoon) — less irrigation
- Aroma compound: 2-acetyl-1-pyrroline (2-AP) — forms when grain filling occurs in October at 30–31°C
- Basmati GI region: 6.2 million ha — Punjab 3.1, Western UP 1.5, Haryana 1.3, Uttarakhand 0.12, Jammu 0.1, HP 0.05 mh; current cultivation only 2.1 mh
## 4. GI aromatic varieties to promote
| Variety | State |
| Kalanamak, Adamchini | Uttar Pradesh |
| Katarni | Bihar |
| Gobindobhog | West Bengal |
| Badshah Bhog | Chhattisgarh |
| Koraput Kalajeera | Odisha |
| Wayanad Jeerakasala | Kerala |
| Seeraga Samba | Tamil Nadu |
## 5. Breeding (IARI)
- Blockbusters: Pusa Basmati-1509, 1121, 1401; PB-1509 yields 2.5 t/acre in 115–120 days (traditional 1 t/acre, 155–160 days)
- Marker-assisted selection for bacterial leaf blight and rice blast resistance: Pusa Basmati-1847, 1885, 1886
- Recommendations: floor price for basmati; shift non-basmati procurement to Eastern UP, Bihar, West Bengal, Assam

# MCQ NOTES OF APP TOPIC 6:34
### West Asia shock: key data
## Import dependence
- India imports about 89% of its oil
- GCC — about 40% of India's urea imports; 60% or more of LNG imports
- Strait of Hormuz–Persian Gulf — more than 85% of India's LPG imports pass through it
- Indian Potash Limited — tender for 2.5 million tonnes of urea (2026 fertilizer shock)
## Rupee, gold and reserves
- PM's appeal (Hyderabad rally) — avoid non-essential foreign travel and gold purchases for one year
- Revised gold and silver import duty — to curb imports, protect forex, support the rupee, reduce CAD
- Weak rupee — causes imported inflation; export gains cut by import content and India's price-taker status
- Forex reserves — foreign currency assets, gold, SDRs and reserve tranche in the IMF
- Dornbusch Overshooting Model — exchange rates overreact in the short run because prices are sticky
## Relief measures
- ECLGS 5.0 — approved by the Union Cabinet; guarantees through NCGTC; MSMEs 100% coverage, non-MSMEs and aviation 90%
- Proposed MSME credit guarantee (based on ECLGS) — 90% guarantee on loans up to ₹100 crore
- Bharat Maritime Insurance Pool — insures Indian ships on high-risk or war-affected routes, especially West Asia
## Other
- UDGAM — unclaimed deposits (unused 10 years) go to the Depositor Education and Awareness Fund
- Sugarcane 2024–25 — 444.9 MT; Uttar Pradesh 48.5%, Maharashtra 24.1%, Karnataka 10.5%
- Spices Board — HQ Kochi
- SKO — used in wick and pressure stoves, lamps and as a solvent (not a transport fuel)
- Gobindobhog — West Bengal; Kalanamak — Uttar Pradesh; Katarni — Bihar; Badshah Bhog — Chhattisgarh
- Public Key Infrastructure — digital security infrastructure
### Strategic Petroleum Reserves
## SPRs and oil storage
- SPRs (March 2026) — 3.37 MMT stored against 5.33 MMT capacity (~64%)
- Padur SPR — Karnataka (2.5 MMT)
- At full capacity SPRs meet only about 9.5 days of crude requirement
- IEA norm — 90 days; India's total national storage incl. commercial about 74 days
- Deccan Traps — not among used-up gas fields fit for easy storage
- PNGRB appeals — APTEL (Electricity Act, 2003)
## Energy and storage
- Renewable energy — about 53% of installed capacity
- Non-fossil capacity — 283 GW → 786 GW by 2035–36
- Other storage technologies include flywheel, molten salt, compressed-air, gravity
- Rajasthan — about 23 GW installed RE vs about 18.9 GW transmission capacity
- Ethanol generations — 2G agricultural residues; 3G algae; 4G genetically engineered biomass
- Ethanol Blended Petrol Programme — since 2003, under Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
- SKO via PDS in 21 States/UTs (incl. Delhi, Gujarat) — gazette notification by Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas
## Basmati and rice
- Basmati — earns 2–2.5 times more per kg than non-basmati; transplanted in July (monsoon), needs less irrigation
- Aroma compound — 2-acetyl-1-pyrroline (2-AP); forms when grain fills in October at 30–31°C
- Pusa Basmati-1847, 1885, 1886 (IARI) — marker-assisted selection; resistance to bacterial leaf blight and rice blast
- Wayanad Jeerakasala — Kerala
## Other
- El Niño — warming of the central and eastern equatorial Pacific; La Niña the opposite phase
- BMIP — run by GIC Re; US$1.5 billion pool; claims up to US$100 million
- 1% construction welfare cess — through BOCW Welfare Boards; Labour Minister Mansukh Mandaviya
- Noida International Airport, Jewar — developed by YIAPL; Phase 1 inaugurated 28 March 2026
### LPG vs LNG
| Fuel | What it is |
| LPG | propane and butane; from crude refining and gas processing; liquid in cylinders |
| PNG | piped methane |
| LNG | natural gas cooled to about –160/–162°C; cryogenic tanks |
| CNG | methane compressed at high pressure; transport fuel |
- PNG supply chain — regasification at coastal terminals → trunk pipelines → City Gate Stations → city network to metered households
- PNG network planning (City Gas Distribution) — integrated with PM Gati Shakti
- About 60% of LPG used in India is imported
- Ethyl mercaptan — added for LPG leak detection
- Mission Anveshan — satellite imagery for oil and gas exploration; associated with ONGC and Alpha Geo
- Salaya — tidal port on the Gulf of Kutch, Gujarat; boat-repair hub

### Rupee depreciation — to defend or not
## 1. Key points
- RBI approach: curb excess volatility, no specific exchange-rate target (BIS paper)
- Forex reserves: ~$701.4 bn on 16 January 2026 — ~11 months of goods imports (PIB)
- RBI defends rupee by selling dollars (absorbs rupees)
- Weak rupee → imported inflation; export gain limited due to import content and price-taker status
## 2. Background
- Forex reserves components: foreign currency assets, gold, SDRs, reserve tranche in IMF
- Expansionary monetary policy does not stop rupee slide; Masala Bonds, easier ECB help
### IBAM vs EU CBAM
## 1. News
- EU CBAM financially binding from January 2026; India got no exemption despite India–EU FTA
- India considering India Border Adjustment Mechanism (IBAM) — charge on exports to CBAM countries so carbon revenue stays in India
## 2. CBAM basics
- By: European Union — carbon cost on imports equal to EU ETS price; aims to stop carbon leakage
- Sectors: cement, iron and steel, aluminium, fertilisers, electricity, hydrogen
- Importers buy CBAM certificates; carbon price already paid at origin can be deducted
## 3. India's tools
- Carbon Credit Trading Scheme (CCTS) — domestic carbon market
- Annex 14-A of India–EU FTA — technical dialogue on carbon measures

### Cost of bringing down inflation — India, UK, US; RBI pause at 5.25%
## 1. News
- Crude above $120/barrel due to West Asia conflict — fresh inflation risk
- RBI paused rate cuts at repo 5.25% in April 2026 (after ~125 bps cuts from early 2025)
## 2. Country comparison
| Country | Response | Outcome |
| US (Fed) | 11 hikes 2022–July 2023 to 5.25–5.50% | No recession; sacrifice ratio ~zero |
| UK (BoE) | Aggressive hikes | Recession late 2023; CPI ~3% in Feb 2026 vs 2% target |
| India (RBI) | Repo 4% → 6.5% (May 2022–Feb 2023) | Held long; cuts from 2025 |
- Sacrifice ratio: GDP lost per 1 percentage point fall in inflation
## 3. India's framework
- Flexible Inflation Targeting since 2016 — CPI 4% ±2% (⚠ target is 4%, not 2%)
- Statutory basis: RBI Act, 1934 amended in 2016 (via Finance Act, 2016)
- Monetary Policy Framework Agreement: February 2015; ⚠ MPC constituted September 2016
- MPC: 6 members — 3 RBI (Governor chairs, Deputy Governor, one RBI official) + 3 Govt-appointed; Governor has casting vote
- India imports ~85% crude (this source)
- Repo kept at 5.25%; FY2026–27 inflation estimate 4.6%; 1 bps = 0.01%
- RBI Mission Saksham — capacity building for urban cooperative banks; ~1.4 lakh participants

### Ethanol blending allowed in Aviation Turbine Fuel
## 1. News
- Govt allowed ethanol and synthetic hydrocarbons in ATF — amendment to ATF (Regulation of Marketing) Order, 2001 under Essential Commodities Act, 1955
- ⚠ No mandatory blending target fixed yet
## 2. Key facts
- Sustainable Aviation Fuel (SAF): cuts lifecycle emissions 60–80%; aviation ~2–3% of global CO₂
- ICAO carbon-neutral growth; CORSIA — Carbon Offsetting and Reduction Scheme for International Aviation
- Ethanol has ~27% less energy per litre than petrol; ⚠ not fully carbon-neutral; cuts CO, unburnt HC, PM
- Feedstocks: molasses, cane juice, maize, surplus FCI rice, 2G residues, sweet sorghum
- India exploring E85, E100 for flex-fuel vehicles
### Rupee as a barometer of credibility
## 1. Context (2026)
- Rupee under pressure — BoP stress; CAD financing hard due to negative net FDI inflows and volatile FPI
## 2. Theories
- Dornbusch Overshooting Model: exchange rate overreacts in short run (sticky prices)
- Mundell-Fleming / Impossible Trinity: cannot have fixed exchange rate + free capital mobility + independent monetary policy together; India — managed float
## 3. Exam angle
- 2013 Taper Tantrum (US Fed) — rupee fell; RBI tightened short-term rates, oil-dollar swap; govt raised gold import duty
- ⚠ RBI buying foreign currency → expands domestic money supply (sterilisation may follow)
- ⚠ Higher FDI inflows → rupee appreciates (not depreciates)
- Depreciation → imported inflation, higher external debt burden
- Fowler Committee (1898): Gold Exchange Standard — rupee pegged to sterling at 1s 4d (15 rupees = £1) — for Home Charges

### Piped Natural Gas (PNG) push and gas-based economy
## 1. News
- Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas reviewed PNG network expansion amid Strait of Hormuz disruptions
- Gas allocation prioritised for households and essential services under Essential Commodities Act
## 2. Supply chain
- LNG imported → regasified at coastal terminals → trunk pipelines → City Gate Stations (CGS) → steel/polyethylene city network → metered households
- Planning integrated with PM Gati Shakti; part of City Gas Distribution (CGD)
## 3. Fuel comparison
| Fuel | Composition / form | Use |
| LPG | Propane + butane, liquid in cylinders; ⚠ heavier than air | Household cooking |
| CNG | Methane compressed at high pressure | Transport fuel |
| PNG | Methane via pipelines; ⚠ lighter than air, disperses fast | Homes, industry |
| LNG | Natural gas cooled to ~–160/–162°C | Shipping/storage form; feeds PNG, CNG |

### LPG shortage and storage gap
## 1. News
- LPG shortage due to West Asia conflict — 85%+ of India's LPG imports pass via Strait of Hormuz
- NRAI warned ~60% of restaurants could shut; IEA flagged lack of storage as a key weakness
## 2. Consumption and imports
- India — second-largest LPG consumer; ~3 million t/month (~80,000 t/day); 85%+ used by households
- Imports: ~20 million t/year (tripled 2011-12 to 2024-25); dependency 47% (2015) → ~60%
- 33 crore domestic connections; ~10 crore added after 2017 under PMUY
## 3. Underground LPG caverns
- Total: 1.4 lakh t — ⚠ less than two days of consumption; MoPNG: no more caverns planned
- Crude/products reserves: about two months; global norm 90 days; EU gas storage must be 90% full before winter
## 4. Storage options
- Depleted gas fields: KG Basin, Cambay, Mumbai Offshore
## 5. Response
- LPG and CNG on priority list under Essential Commodities Act
- IEA release of ~400 million barrels from strategic reserves
- US deal: 2.2 million t LPG/year — shipping ~45 days
- ⚠ Strait pairs: Hormuz–Persian Gulf, Bab-el-Mandeb–Red Sea, Malacca–Andaman Sea, Sunda–Java Sea
## 6. LPG vs LNG
| Feature | LPG | LNG |
| Composition | Propane + butane | Methane |
| Source | Crude refining, gas processing | Natural gas |
| Liquefaction | Moderate pressure — 1/260th volume | Cooled to ~–160°C — 1/600th volume |
| Storage | Cylinders | ⚠ Cryogenic tanks |
| Density | ⚠ Heavier than air | Lighter than air |
| Use | Cooking | PNG, CNG, industry |
## 7. Crisis response
- LPG: ~60% imported, ~90% of imports via Hormuz → ~54% of supply vulnerable; 33.3 crore households
- Gas: ~50% imported, 55–60% of it from West Asia → ~30% of LNG affected; PNG/CNG prioritised over industry
- Refineries maximised LPG — domestic output +40%; domestic share 40% → 55%
- Cylinder booking interval: urban 25 days, rural 45 days; ~60 lakh households can shift to PNG

### Periodic Labour Force Survey (PLFS) Annual Report 2025
## 1. Background
- PLFS launched by NSO in 2017; quarterly urban estimates by CWS; annual by Usual Status (ps+ss) and CWS
- LFPR = labour force / population; WPR = employed / population; UR = unemployed / labour force
- CWS — last 7 days; Usual Status — last 365 days; subsidiary status — 30 days+
- 2025 survey uses a new sampling design and calendar-year format
## 2. Key data
| Indicator | Value |
| Rural UR | 2.4% (male 2.6%, female 2.1%) |
| Urban UR | 4.8% (male 4.2%, female 6.4%) |
| Agriculture share | 44.8% → 43.0% (still largest) |
## 3. Challenges
- 44.4% of females out of labour force due to childcare/domestic work (69.8% of males due to education)
- NEET (15–29): ~25%; formal vocational training only 4.2% (15–59)
## 4. More facts
- Overall unemployment 3.2% → 3.1%; women (15+) 3.1%, men 3.3% → 3.1%; rural 2.5% → 2.4%; casual labour 20.2%
- Self-employment 52% (2017–18) → 58% (Labour Minister Mansukh Mandaviya in Lok Sabha); casual daily wage ₹295 → ₹418
- Four Labour Codes: Code on Wages (2019), IR Code (2020), Social Security Code (2020), OSH Code (2020)
- PLFS (revamped monthly): UR 5.4% in Apr-Jun 2026 (urban 6.7%); unemployment (15+) 5.1% in Jul 2026 (TH, 7 Sep 2026)
- Job data debate: youth unemployment 14.8% (PLFS 2025)

### Stranded renewable power and grid congestion
- Renewable grid congestion fixes — adaptive line rating, STATCOMs, dynamic grid management and transparent curtailment
### Rice production and exports — need for a rethink
## 1. India's rice leadership
- Largest rice exporter since 2011–12; exported 21.69 million tonnes in 2024–25
- ⚠ Surpassed China to become the world's largest rice producer (150 mt) in 2024–25
## 2. Water footprint
- System: conventional transplanting-cum-continuous flooding; field water level 5 cm; 20–30 irrigations
- One irrigation of 1 acre at 2.5 cm = 101,171.5 litres; total ≈ 5 million litres per acre
- Water per kg: paddy ≈ 2,000 litres; milled rice ≈ 3,000 litres — virtual water export; stress in Punjab–Haryana
## 3. Basmati vs non-basmati
- Non-basmati exports double in volume but earnings similar; basmati earns 2–2.5 times more per kg
- Transplanting: non-basmati in June (peak summer); basmati in July (monsoon) — less irrigation
- Aroma compound: 2-acetyl-1-pyrroline (2-AP) — forms when grain filling occurs in October at 30–31°C
- Basmati GI region: 6.2 million ha — Punjab 3.1, Western UP 1.5, Haryana 1.3, Uttarakhand 0.12, Jammu 0.1, HP 0.05 mh; current cultivation only 2.1 mh
## 4. GI aromatic varieties to promote
| Variety | State |
| Kalanamak, Adamchini | Uttar Pradesh |
| Katarni | Bihar |
| Gobindobhog | West Bengal |
| Badshah Bhog | Chhattisgarh |
| Koraput Kalajeera | Odisha |
| Wayanad Jeerakasala | Kerala |
| Seeraga Samba | Tamil Nadu |
## 5. Breeding (IARI)
- Blockbusters: Pusa Basmati-1509, 1121, 1401; PB-1509 yields 2.5 t/acre in 115–120 days (traditional 1 t/acre, 155–160 days)
- Marker-assisted selection for bacterial leaf blight and rice blast resistance: Pusa Basmati-1847, 1885, 1886
- Recommendations: floor price for basmati; shift non-basmati procurement to Eastern UP, Bihar, West Bengal, Assam
### Infrastructure financing — trends and institutions
## 1. Key data
- Public capex: ₹2 lakh crore (2014–15) → ₹12.2 lakh crore (2026–27)
- Need: ~USD 2.2 trillion; insurance/pension funds allocate only ~6% to infra
- Land acquisition causes ~35% of project delays; compensation up to 4 times market value
## 2. Institutions and instruments
| Institution / tool | Role |
| NIIF | Mobilises global and domestic capital |
| NaBFID | Long-term infra finance, ESG financing |
| IRFC | Est. 1986; leasing model; meets EBR needs — financed ~75% of rolling stock |
| InvITs | Monetise completed infra assets |
| REITs | Small investors in real estate |
| Infrastructure Risk Guarantee Fund | Partial guarantees to lenders |
| National Monetisation Pipeline | Lease operational assets |
- Other tools: Partial Credit Enhancement, green/blue bonds, municipal bonds, GIFT City
- Public Key Infrastructure = digital security infrastructure (UPSC PYQ)
### Mission Anveshan, Noida airport and CWC at 70

- Mission Anveshan: oil and gas exploration using satellite imagery — ONGC (1956) with Alpha Geo; India imports ~85% of crude
- Noida International Airport (Jewar) Phase 1 inaugurated March 28 — second international airport for Delhi-NCR; by YIAPL
- Central Warehousing Corporation — 70th foundation day on March 2, 2026; est. 1957
- Bureau of Energy Efficiency — 25 years (est. 1 March 2002, Energy Conservation Act 2001)

### Union Budget 2026–27 — key announcements
## 1. Basics
- Presented by: FM Nirmala Sitharaman — a "Yuva Shakti-driven" Budget
- Presented: 1 Feb 2026; Prepared by: DEA, Ministry of Finance
- ⚠ First Union Budget prepared in Kartavya Bhawan
- Three Kartavya (duties): (1) accelerate and sustain economic growth; (2) fulfil aspirations and build capacity; (3) Sabka Sath, Sabka Vikas — access for every family, community, region, sector
- Reforms since Independence Day 2025: 350+ reforms — GST simplification, Labour Codes notified, QCO rationalisation
- Six intervention areas (Kartavya 1): manufacturing in 7 strategic/frontier sectors; legacy industries; Champion MSMEs; infrastructure; energy security; City Economic Regions
## 2. Fiscal numbers
| Item | RE 2025–26 | BE 2026–27 |
| Fiscal deficit (% GDP) | 4.4% | 4.3% |
| Public capex | ₹11.2 lakh cr (BE 2025–26) | ₹12.2 lakh cr |
- Capex 2014–15: ₹2 lakh crore
- Fiscal deficit 2026-27: 4.3% of GDP = ₹16.96 lakh crore; Revenue deficit: 1.5%
- Debt-GDP target 50 ± 1% by 2030-31
- Capex ₹12.22 lakh crore (3.1% of GDP); effective capex ₹17.15 lakh crore; total spending ₹53.47 lakh crore
- Devolution: 16th Finance Commission's 41% vertical devolution retained
- ⚠ FD 4.3% (2026-27) vs 4.4% (FY26) vs 4.8% achieved in FY25
## 3. Manufacturing and industry
- Biopharma Shakti: outlay ₹10,000 crore over 5 years — biologics and biosimilars; 3 new NIPERs, 7 NIPERs upgraded; 1,000+ accredited clinical trial sites; strengthen CDSCO
- Integrated Textile Programme — 5 parts: National Fibre Scheme (silk, wool, jute, man-made, new-age fibres); Textile Expansion and Employment Scheme; National Handloom and Handicraft Programme; Textile Ecology Initiative; Samarth 2.0 (skilling)
- SME Growth Fund: ₹10,000 crore — to create future champion enterprises
## 4. Infrastructure
- Dedicated Freight Corridor: Dankuni (east) to Surat (west)
- 20 new National Waterways over 5 years — starting with NW-5 in Odisha (Talcher, Angul, Kalinga Nagar to Paradeep and Dhamra ports)
- City Economic Regions: ₹5,000 crore per CER over 5 years — challenge mode, reform-cum-results financing
- 7 high-speed rail corridors: Mumbai–Pune, Pune–Hyderabad, Hyderabad–Bengaluru, Hyderabad–Chennai, Chennai–Bengaluru, Delhi–Varanasi, Varanasi–Siliguri
## 5. Social and skilling (Kartavya 2)
- ~25 crore people exited multidimensional poverty
- 5 Regional Medical Hubs with private sector (incl. AYUSH, Medical Value Tourism Facilitation Centres)
- Creative economy: support to Indian Institute of Creative Technologies, Mumbai; AVGC Content Creator Labs in 15,000 secondary schools and 500 colleges
- Girls' hostel in every district (STEM higher education) — VGF and capital assistance
- National Institute of Hospitality — by upgrading NCHMCT; pilot to upskill 10,000 tourist guides at 20 sites, 12-week course with an IIM
## 6. Kartavya 3 — Sabka Sath, Sabka Vikas
- Bharat Vistaar: multilingual digital tool for farmers — agri portals + ICAR practices
- Self-Help Entrepreneur (SHE) Marts: community-owned retail outlets at cluster-level federations
- Mental health: second NIMHANS; institutes at Ranchi and Tezpur upgraded as regional apex institutions
- Purvodaya / NE: East Coast Industrial Corridor with node at Durgapur; 5 tourism destinations in Purvodaya states; 4,000 e-buses; Buddhist circuits in Arunachal, Sikkim, Assam, Manipur, Mizoram, Tripura
## 7. Taxation
- New Income Tax Act, 2025 effective April 2026
- In force from 1 Apr 2026
- BCD on monazite cut to nil
- TCS cut to 2%: overseas tour packages; education and medical remittances under LRS
- One-time 6-month foreign asset disclosure scheme; decriminalisation of minor tax offences
- Cooperatives: deduction extended to cattle feed and cotton seed; 3-year exemption for notified national cooperative federations
- IT services: single category; common safe harbour margin 15.5%; threshold ₹2,000 crore
- 5-year income-tax exemption for global experts under notified schemes; MAT exemption for non-residents on presumptive taxation
- Customs: duty exemption on lithium-ion battery capital goods, critical-mineral processing equipment, 17 medicines; tariff on personal imports 20% → 10%; value cap on courier exports removed
## 8. Background
- ⚠ Macro-Economic Framework Statement is mandated by the FRBM Act, 2003 (not a constitutional article)
- Vote-on-account deals only with expenditure; interim budget has both expenditure and receipts
## 9. Spending-share analysis (CMIE data)
- Union Budget size grew 10.4% a year on average since 2009
- Roads + Railways share: <4% (2009) → ~11% — largest post-pandemic increase
- MSME share only 0.24% — though MSMEs give ~30% of GDP, 45%+ of exports
- Interest payments: about 1 in every 4 rupees spent
- Skill Development Ministry share 0.06% → 0.05%; health share declined after peak in 2017–18; tourism share nearly vanished
## 10. Budget estimates (CA360)
- ⚠ 9th Budget by Nirmala Sitharaman — most by a woman FM; 6th digital Budget; first paperless Budget 2021–22
- Budget history: first Budget of independent India — R.K. Shanmukham Chetty, 26 November 1947; first of Republic — John Mathai; ⚠ most Budgets — Morarji Desai (10); Indira Gandhi first woman to present
- Article 112 — Annual Financial Statement
## 11. More industry and infrastructure measures
- India Semiconductor Mission 2.0; electronics components scheme raised to ₹40,000 crore
- Rare earth corridors in Odisha, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu; 3 chemical parks; 2 high-tech tool rooms via CPSEs
- Container manufacturing scheme — ₹10,000 crore+ over 5 years; revival of 200 legacy industrial clusters
- Atmanirbhar Bharat Fund + ₹2,000 crore; "Corporate Mitras" in Tier-2/3 towns
- Infrastructure Risk Guarantee Fund; REITs for CPSE real estate; river-vessel repair at Varanasi and Patna
- Coastal cargo share target 12% by 2047; CCUS — ₹20,000 crore over 5 years
- High-level committee on banking; restructuring of PFC and REC; ₹100 crore incentive for municipal bonds
## 12. More tax and customs measures
- MACT interest exempt from income tax (no TDS); TCS on overseas tours 2–20% → 2%; LRS education/medical 5% → 2%
- Revised return deadline 31 December → 31 March; updated returns allowed after reassessment with 10% extra tax
- Foreign non-immovable assets below ₹20 lakh — immunity from prosecution retrospectively from 1 October 2024
- IT safe harbour threshold ₹300 crore → ₹2,000 crore; data-centre related-party safe harbour 15%; bonded warehousing margin 2% (effective tax ~0.7%)
- Buybacks taxed as capital gains — effective 22% corporate promoters, 30% non-corporate
- MAT becomes final tax from 1 April 2026 — rate 15% → 14%; set-off capped at one-fourth
- TCS on tendu leaves 5% → 2%; liquor, scrap, minerals 2%
- Customs: seafood duty-free inputs 1% → 3% of FOB; nuclear project import exemption extended to 2035; BCD exemption on sodium antimonate (solar glass); 7 more rare-disease drugs for personal import
- AEO Tier-2/3 duty deferment 15 → 30 days; Advance Rulings validity 3 → 5 years; ₹10 lakh courier export cap removed; fish caught in EEZ/high seas duty-free

### Civil aviation sector stress and UDAN
## 1. Sector
- India — third-largest domestic aviation market; 840+ aircraft, 350+ million passengers/year; demand 715 million by 2030
- Events: June 2025 Ahmedabad crash; December 2025 IndiGo mass disruptions (revised FDTL norms)
- IndiGo: 360+ aircraft, ~5,038 pilots — ~14 pilots/aircraft vs global 18–20
- Pilot need: 7,000 (2024–26); 25,000–30,000 next decade; only ~5,700 CPLs issued 2020–24
- Market share: IndiGo ~63–65%, Air India group ~27–28% — together ~90%; IndiGo sole carrier on ~60% of routes
- DGCA: 19 safety violation notices by late 2025; nearly half technical posts vacant

- Launched: 21 October 2016; ⚠ first flight Shimla–Delhi (27 April 2017)
- Achievements: 625 routes; 90 airports, 15 heliports, 2 water aerodromes; 1.49+ crore passengers; 3 lakh+ flights
- Airports 74 (2014) → 159 (2024); VGF ₹4,023.37 crore; 2024: 102 new routes (20 in NE)
- Incentives: states cut VAT on ATF to 1% or less (10 years); Centre excise on ATF capped 2% (3 years); VGF via Regional Connectivity Fund

- DGCA: attached office of Ministry of Civil Aviation; HQ New Delhi; coordinates with ICAO

### Sustainability in rice production
## 1. News
- ⚠ 2025: India overtook China as world's largest rice producer
- India supplies ~40% of global rice exports — over 20 million metric tonnes
## 2. Facts
- Staple for ~65% of Indians; ~25% of total cropped area
- Kharif crop: temperature >25°C, rainfall >100 cm
- Transplanted paddy: 25–27 irrigations/season; 1 kg rice = 3,000–4,000 litres water
- Punjab–Haryana water table fell ~30 ft → 80–200 ft; extraction exceeds recharge by 35–57%
- Rice = 10–20% of India's agricultural GHG emissions (methane); Punjab spends ₹39,000/ha on power and fertiliser subsidies
- Climate change may cut yields 6–10%
## 3. Solutions
- DSR (Direct Seeded Rice), AWD (Alternate Wetting and Drying — cuts methane), SRI, micro-irrigation under PMKSY
- ICAR NICRA programme; fortified rice under NFSA and PM-POSHAN
- ⚠ Flooded transplanted paddy increases methane (anaerobic)
## 4. Official figures
- Announced by Shivraj Singh Chouhan: India 150.18 million tonnes vs China 145.28 million tonnes
- 184 improved varieties of 25 crops by ICAR released — 122 cereals, 6 pulses, 13 oilseeds, 11 fodder, 6 sugarcane, 24 cotton–jute, 1 tobacco
### Seafood exports amid US tariffs
## 1. News
- Apr–Oct 2025: marine exports up 16% in value, 12% in volume — $4.87 billion (from $4.19 bn); volume 9.62 → 10.73 lakh MT
- Apr–Nov 2025: up 16%+ to $5.75 billion
- US duties on Indian shrimp: nearly 60% (anti-dumping + countervailing + additional) — exports to US down 4% value, 11% quantity
- ⚠ US still India's single largest seafood market
- Diversification: China $845.67 million; Vietnam 100%+ growth; Belgium ~90%
- Main item: frozen Vannamei shrimp
## 2. Background
- India — 3rd largest fish producer, 2nd largest aquaculture producer; coastline 7,500 km+
- MPEDA — export promotion; PMMSY — infrastructure
- ReALCRaft: web-based platform for online registration and licensing of fishing vessels
- Richest fishing grounds — where warm and cold currents meet (Grand Banks; Kuroshio–Oyashio)
## 3. Fisheries decade (Ministry data)
- Fish production 95.79 lakh t (2013–14) → 197.75 lakh t (2024–25) — up 106%
- India 2nd largest fish producer (~8% of world); 1st in shrimp production and exports; 2nd in aquaculture and capture
- Aquaculture productivity 4.77 t/ha; seafood exports 2024–25 16.98 lakh t, record ₹62,408 crore (+3.11%)
- Value-added exports ₹4,863.40 → ₹7,589.93 crore (+56%, 5 yrs) — ~11% of fisheries exports

### Banking Laws (Amendment) Act, 2025
## 1. News
- Notified: in two stages — 1 August and 1 November 2025
- Amends five Acts: RBI Act 1934, Banking Regulation Act 1949, SBI Act 1955, Banking Companies (Acquisition and Transfer of Undertakings) Acts 1970 and 1980
## 2. Key reforms
| Reform | Detail |
| Nominees | Up to four per account; simultaneous (percentage, total 100%) or successive (lockers) |
| 'Substantial interest' | ₹5 lakh (1968) → ₹2 crore |
| Cooperative bank directors | Max tenure 8 → 10 years (excl. chairperson, WTDs) — aligns with 97th Amendment |
| PSB audit | PSBs fix auditor remuneration; unclaimed shares/interest to IEPF |
- ⚠ Director tenure is 10 years, not 15
## 3. Background
- SBI Act 1955: Imperial Bank → SBI
- Nationalisation: 14 banks in 1969; more in 1980
- Banking Regulation (Amendment) Act, 2020: RBI control over cooperative banks
- Urban Cooperative Banks: brought under BR Act by 1966 amendment

### Biostimulants for sustainable agriculture
## 1. News
- Fertiliser use: 139.81 kg/ha nationally; 247.61 kg/ha in Punjab; fertilisers give 19% of agriculture GHG emissions
- Biostimulants: stimulate plant processes independent of nutrient content — ⚠ not fertilisers, not insecticides (excluded from Insecticides Act, 1968)
## 2. Regulation
- Schedule VI of Fertiliser Control Order (FCO) 1985 — introduced February 2021
- Categories: nine (botanical extracts, humic acids, protein hydrolysates, vitamins, antioxidants, live microbes etc.)
- Provisional approvals: about 30,000 products → after expiry on 16 June 2025 only 132 approved
| Crop group | Share of approved products |
| Pulses & oilseeds | 15% |

- India: production 74,083 tonnes; potential 9.7 million tonnes
- Hydrocolloids: agar, alginate, carrageenan
### Domestic savings reshaping India's capital markets
## 1. News
- Shift: domestic household savings replacing FPI as main source of market liquidity
- FPI holding: 15-month low of 16.9%; 24.1% in NIFTY 50 companies
- Retail investors: about 19% of market ownership — highest in 20+ years
- IPOs 2025: 71 mainboard IPOs raised over ₹1 lakh crore
- Corporate investment announcements FY25: +39% YoY; about 70% private sector
- Benefit: greater RBI monetary policy autonomy
## 2. Measures suggested
- SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 2015 — move to suitability-based selling
- Mutual Fund Sahi Hai campaign; National Strategy for Financial Education (NSFE)
## 3. Background
- Capital market: both primary and secondary markets
- Rupee: full current account convertibility; capital account only partial
### Bank of India raises ₹10,000 crore via infrastructure bonds
## 1. News
- Bank of India (BoI): raised ₹10,000 crore through infrastructure bonds; bids ₹15,300 crore vs base issue ₹5,000 crore
## 2. Infrastructure bonds
- RBI rule: bank infra bonds — minimum maturity 7 years (typical 10–15 years)
- Bank-issued bonds: enjoy CRR and SLR exemptions; reduce asset–liability mismatch
- Issuers: govt (NHAI), banks (PSBs dominate), institutions (IREDA, PFC, REC, IRFC), green bonds
| Aspect | Infrastructure Bonds | InvITs |
| Nature | Debt | Trust-based vehicle |
| Returns | Fixed interest | Market-linked |
| Tenure | Fixed | No fixed maturity |
| Regulation | RBI & SEBI | SEBI (InvIT Regulations, 2014) |
- ⚠ InvITs are recognised as borrowers under SARFAESI Act, 2002 (UPSC 2023)
### Bitcoin's slide after October record and crypto regulation
## 1. News
- Bitcoin: record $126,000 (October 2025) → below $85,000; about $1 trillion crypto value wiped out; fears of "crypto winter"
## 2. Reasons
- US Fed: cut rates by 25 bps on December 10 but signalled no further cuts
- 10/10 crash: October 10 — Trump threatened 100% tariffs on Chinese goods; $19 billion leveraged trades liquidated; $500 billion wiped out in hours
- Long-term holders: 800,000 Bitcoins sold within a month
- ⚠ Bitcoin behaves like a risk asset, not "digital gold"
## 3. Why winter fears may be exaggerated
- Halving: four-year cycle; last April 2024
- Bitcoin ETFs: approved in US January 2024; hold 1.4 million BTC (6.8% of supply)
- US policy: US Bitcoin Reserve executive order (March 2025); GENIUS Act (June 2025) on stablecoins
## 4. Crypto basics and India's status
- Bitcoin: 2009, Satoshi Nakamoto; capped at 21 million; Bitcoin Cash — hard fork 2017
| Year | India |
| 2018 | RBI banking ban |
| 2020 | Supreme Court lifted RBI ban |
| 2022 | 30% tax on gains + 1% TDS; Virtual Digital Assets, not legal tender; e₹ (CBDC) pilots |
| 2023 | Exchanges under FIU-IND (KYC/AML) |
- Legal tender abroad: El Salvador, Central African Republic; bans/restrictions: China, Russia
- Digital Rupee: 1 e₹ = 1 ₹; issued by RBI
# MCQ NOTES OF APP TOPIC 6:35
### Budget 2026-27: fiscal numbers
## Fiscal and growth numbers
- Fiscal deficit BE 2026–27 — 4.3% of GDP (4.4% is the earlier figure)
- Real GDP growth Q3 (Oct–Dec) 2025–26 — 7.8% (7.4% a year earlier)
- Economic Survey 2025–26: GDP growth 2025–26 estimated at 7.4%; 2026–27 projection 6.8–7.2%
- World Bank projection for 2025–26 — 7.2%; NSO first advance estimate 7.4%
- Share of world GDP 1980–2023: Germany third (4.29%), ahead of Japan (4.0%); India fifth (3.45%)
- Weak tax buoyancy — tax grows slower than GDP
## GST collections
- December 2025 gross GST — ₹1.74 lakh crore (net ₹1.45 lakh crore; domestic ₹1.22 lakh crore; imports ₹51,977 crore)
- January 2026 GST — ₹1.93 lakh crore, up 6.2%
## Agriculture, dairy and sugar
- Bharat-VISTAAR — multilingual AI platform joining AgriStack with ICAR practices
- Foodgrain production 2024–25 — record 3,577.32 lakh tonnes
- Digital Crop Survey (AgriStack), Kharif 2025 — 604 districts, more than 28.5 crore plots
- Agriculture growth 2014–15 to 2024–25 — 4.45% per year, highest ever
- Rice — staple for about 65% of Indians; about 25% of total cropped area
- Kisan Credit Card coverage — 7.75 crore farmers
- Milk production 2023–24 — 239.3 MMT; India world's largest milk producer
- BAHS 2023: Andhra Pradesh fifth in milk production (6.70%), after UP, Rajasthan, MP and Gujarat
- Biostimulants — nine categories under Schedule VI, Fertiliser Control Order 1985 (added February 2021)
- Sugar, as of 15 January of 2025–26 season — total 15.9 million tonnes, 518 mills operating

- National Institute of Hospitality — by upgrading NCHMCT
- Second NIMHANS; Ranchi and Tezpur institutes upgraded as regional apex institutions

- PMUY ₹300 subsidy — 14.2 kg cylinder, up to nine refills a year
- Atal Innovation Mission — launched by NITI Aayog in 2016
- Ethanol blending 19.24% — relates to ESY 2024–25 (not 2025–26)
## MSMEs
- MSMEs — about 30.1% of GDP; 35.4% of manufacturing output, 45.73% of exports, 62% of employment

- Sonepur International Airport (4,200 m runway, A380) — Saran, Bihar

- Revised FDTL norms — weekly rest raised from 36 to 48 hours
- Aviation sector — 7.7 million jobs, about 1.5% of GDP
- Bharatiya Vayuyan Adhiniyam, 2024 replaces the Aircraft Act, 1934
- DGCA headquartered in New Delhi; aircraft noise and emissions under ICAO Annex 16
## Banking, currency and crypto
- Bank-issued infrastructure bonds get CRR and SLR exemptions; they are debt instruments (being debt is not the reason)
- Cooperative banks brought under RBI control — Banking Regulation (Amendment) Act, 2020; the 1966 amendment brought Urban Cooperative Banks under the BR Act
- Banking Laws (Amendment) Act, 2025 — unclaimed shares and interest go to the IEPF; PSBs may fix auditor remuneration
- Rupee — full convertibility on current account only; partial capital account convertibility
- Bitcoin Cash — hard fork of Bitcoin in 2017

- TCS on overseas tour packages and LRS education/medical remittances — cut to 2%
- Eligible startups — three-year income-tax exemption plus angel-tax exemption

### Exam corner
## Governance and Budget
- Seva Teerth — new PMO building; PMO moved from South Block
- Macro-Economic Framework Statement — required by FRBM Act, 2003; Article 112 = Annual Financial Statement
- National Household Income Survey and ASISSE (NSO) — through CAPI, from April 2026
- PAIMANA replaced the Online Monitoring System of 2006; "one data, one entry"; ~60% updates automated
- SAKSHAM 2026 (2–16 Feb 2026) — Ministry of Petroleum and Natural Gas; theme "Conserve Oil and Natural Gas – Towards a Greener Future"
## Agriculture, dairy, fisheries
- Bharat Vistaar — multilingual digital tool integrating agri portals and ICAR practices
- SHE Marts — community-owned, at cluster-level federations
- Transplanted paddy — 25–27 irrigations per season; flooding raises methane (anaerobic)
- Farmers' income growth 10.11% per year — period ends 2023–24; food surplus ~20% by 2035
- MPEDA — export promotion; PMMSY — infrastructure
- ReALCRaft — online registration and licensing of fishing vessels
- Banas Dairy — Asia's largest dairy cooperative; circular model can raise farmer incomes ~20% over five years
## Trade, startups, MSMEs
- Rupee depreciated ~5% to ~91.96/USD; India's share of global merchandise exports 2024 — 1.8%
- Export Promotion Mission – Niryat Protsahan — Department of Commerce; focus on MSMEs; pilot: interest subvention on pre- and post-shipment credit
- Stand Up India — SC/ST persons and women
- DPIIT startup turnover limit — below ₹100 crore
- 2020 MSME criteria, medium — investment ₹50 crore, turnover ₹250 crore
## Markets, aviation, regulation
- DGCA — attached office of Ministry of Civil Aviation, New Delhi, coordinates with ICAO; failed airlines: Kingfisher, Jet Airways, Go First, TruJet
- NPS Swasthya Pension Scheme pilot — voluntary; open to all citizens with an NPS Common Scheme Account
- PNGRB Act — 2006; PMUY launched at Ballia; TRAI (1997) first independent regulator; appeals to APTEL under Section 110, Electricity Act 2003
- 2025: 71 mainboard IPOs raised over ₹1 lakh crore; domestic participation gives RBI more policy freedom
- "Substantial interest" threshold — ₹5 lakh → ₹2 crore
- Mutual Fund Sahi Hai — suggested with National Strategy for Financial Education
- Bitcoin behaves like a risk asset, not digital gold
### Union Budget 2026–27 — key announcements
## 1. Basics
- Presented by: FM Nirmala Sitharaman — a "Yuva Shakti-driven" Budget
- Presented: 1 Feb 2026; Prepared by: DEA, Ministry of Finance
- ⚠ First Union Budget prepared in Kartavya Bhawan
- Three Kartavya (duties): (1) accelerate and sustain economic growth; (2) fulfil aspirations and build capacity; (3) Sabka Sath, Sabka Vikas — access for every family, community, region, sector
- Reforms since Independence Day 2025: 350+ reforms — GST simplification, Labour Codes notified, QCO rationalisation
- Six intervention areas (Kartavya 1): manufacturing in 7 strategic/frontier sectors; legacy industries; Champion MSMEs; infrastructure; energy security; City Economic Regions
## 2. Fiscal numbers
| Item | RE 2025–26 | BE 2026–27 |
| Fiscal deficit (% GDP) | 4.4% | 4.3% |
| Public capex | ₹11.2 lakh cr (BE 2025–26) | ₹12.2 lakh cr |
- Capex 2014–15: ₹2 lakh crore
- Fiscal deficit 2026-27: 4.3% of GDP = ₹16.96 lakh crore; Revenue deficit: 1.5%
- Debt-GDP target 50 ± 1% by 2030-31
- Capex ₹12.22 lakh crore (3.1% of GDP); effective capex ₹17.15 lakh crore; total spending ₹53.47 lakh crore
- Devolution: 16th Finance Commission's 41% vertical devolution retained
- ⚠ FD 4.3% (2026-27) vs 4.4% (FY26) vs 4.8% achieved in FY25
## 3. Manufacturing and industry
- Biopharma Shakti: outlay ₹10,000 crore over 5 years — biologics and biosimilars; 3 new NIPERs, 7 NIPERs upgraded; 1,000+ accredited clinical trial sites; strengthen CDSCO
- Integrated Textile Programme — 5 parts: National Fibre Scheme (silk, wool, jute, man-made, new-age fibres); Textile Expansion and Employment Scheme; National Handloom and Handicraft Programme; Textile Ecology Initiative; Samarth 2.0 (skilling)
- SME Growth Fund: ₹10,000 crore — to create future champion enterprises
## 4. Infrastructure
- Dedicated Freight Corridor: Dankuni (east) to Surat (west)
- 20 new National Waterways over 5 years — starting with NW-5 in Odisha (Talcher, Angul, Kalinga Nagar to Paradeep and Dhamra ports)
- City Economic Regions: ₹5,000 crore per CER over 5 years — challenge mode, reform-cum-results financing
- 7 high-speed rail corridors: Mumbai–Pune, Pune–Hyderabad, Hyderabad–Bengaluru, Hyderabad–Chennai, Chennai–Bengaluru, Delhi–Varanasi, Varanasi–Siliguri
## 5. Social and skilling (Kartavya 2)
- ~25 crore people exited multidimensional poverty
- 5 Regional Medical Hubs with private sector (incl. AYUSH, Medical Value Tourism Facilitation Centres)
- Creative economy: support to Indian Institute of Creative Technologies, Mumbai; AVGC Content Creator Labs in 15,000 secondary schools and 500 colleges
- Girls' hostel in every district (STEM higher education) — VGF and capital assistance
- National Institute of Hospitality — by upgrading NCHMCT; pilot to upskill 10,000 tourist guides at 20 sites, 12-week course with an IIM
## 6. Kartavya 3 — Sabka Sath, Sabka Vikas
- Bharat Vistaar: multilingual digital tool for farmers — agri portals + ICAR practices
- Self-Help Entrepreneur (SHE) Marts: community-owned retail outlets at cluster-level federations
- Mental health: second NIMHANS; institutes at Ranchi and Tezpur upgraded as regional apex institutions
- Purvodaya / NE: East Coast Industrial Corridor with node at Durgapur; 5 tourism destinations in Purvodaya states; 4,000 e-buses; Buddhist circuits in Arunachal, Sikkim, Assam, Manipur, Mizoram, Tripura
## 7. Taxation
- New Income Tax Act, 2025 effective April 2026
- In force from 1 Apr 2026
- BCD on monazite cut to nil
- TCS cut to 2%: overseas tour packages; education and medical remittances under LRS
- One-time 6-month foreign asset disclosure scheme; decriminalisation of minor tax offences
- Cooperatives: deduction extended to cattle feed and cotton seed; 3-year exemption for notified national cooperative federations
- IT services: single category; common safe harbour margin 15.5%; threshold ₹2,000 crore
- 5-year income-tax exemption for global experts under notified schemes; MAT exemption for non-residents on presumptive taxation
- Customs: duty exemption on lithium-ion battery capital goods, critical-mineral processing equipment, 17 medicines; tariff on personal imports 20% → 10%; value cap on courier exports removed
## 8. Background
- ⚠ Macro-Economic Framework Statement is mandated by the FRBM Act, 2003 (not a constitutional article)
- Vote-on-account deals only with expenditure; interim budget has both expenditure and receipts
## 9. Spending-share analysis (CMIE data)
- Union Budget size grew 10.4% a year on average since 2009
- Roads + Railways share: <4% (2009) → ~11% — largest post-pandemic increase
- MSME share only 0.24% — though MSMEs give ~30% of GDP, 45%+ of exports
- Interest payments: about 1 in every 4 rupees spent
- Skill Development Ministry share 0.06% → 0.05%; health share declined after peak in 2017–18; tourism share nearly vanished
## 10. Budget estimates (CA360)
- ⚠ 9th Budget by Nirmala Sitharaman — most by a woman FM; 6th digital Budget; first paperless Budget 2021–22
- Budget history: first Budget of independent India — R.K. Shanmukham Chetty, 26 November 1947; first of Republic — John Mathai; ⚠ most Budgets — Morarji Desai (10); Indira Gandhi first woman to present
- Article 112 — Annual Financial Statement
## 11. More industry and infrastructure measures
- India Semiconductor Mission 2.0; electronics components scheme raised to ₹40,000 crore
- Rare earth corridors in Odisha, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu; 3 chemical parks; 2 high-tech tool rooms via CPSEs
- Container manufacturing scheme — ₹10,000 crore+ over 5 years; revival of 200 legacy industrial clusters
- Atmanirbhar Bharat Fund + ₹2,000 crore; "Corporate Mitras" in Tier-2/3 towns
- Infrastructure Risk Guarantee Fund; REITs for CPSE real estate; river-vessel repair at Varanasi and Patna
- Coastal cargo share target 12% by 2047; CCUS — ₹20,000 crore over 5 years
- High-level committee on banking; restructuring of PFC and REC; ₹100 crore incentive for municipal bonds
## 12. More tax and customs measures
- MACT interest exempt from income tax (no TDS); TCS on overseas tours 2–20% → 2%; LRS education/medical 5% → 2%
- Revised return deadline 31 December → 31 March; updated returns allowed after reassessment with 10% extra tax
- Foreign non-immovable assets below ₹20 lakh — immunity from prosecution retrospectively from 1 October 2024
- IT safe harbour threshold ₹300 crore → ₹2,000 crore; data-centre related-party safe harbour 15%; bonded warehousing margin 2% (effective tax ~0.7%)
- Buybacks taxed as capital gains — effective 22% corporate promoters, 30% non-corporate
- MAT becomes final tax from 1 April 2026 — rate 15% → 14%; set-off capped at one-fourth
- TCS on tendu leaves 5% → 2%; liquor, scrap, minerals 2%
- Customs: seafood duty-free inputs 1% → 3% of FOB; nuclear project import exemption extended to 2035; BCD exemption on sodium antimonate (solar glass); 7 more rare-disease drugs for personal import
- AEO Tier-2/3 duty deferment 15 → 30 days; Advance Rulings validity 3 → 5 years; ₹10 lakh courier export cap removed; fish caught in EEZ/high seas duty-free

### Civil aviation sector stress and UDAN
## 1. Sector
- India — third-largest domestic aviation market; 840+ aircraft, 350+ million passengers/year; demand 715 million by 2030
- Events: June 2025 Ahmedabad crash; December 2025 IndiGo mass disruptions (revised FDTL norms)
- IndiGo: 360+ aircraft, ~5,038 pilots — ~14 pilots/aircraft vs global 18–20
- Pilot need: 7,000 (2024–26); 25,000–30,000 next decade; only ~5,700 CPLs issued 2020–24
- Market share: IndiGo ~63–65%, Air India group ~27–28% — together ~90%; IndiGo sole carrier on ~60% of routes
- DGCA: 19 safety violation notices by late 2025; nearly half technical posts vacant
## 2. New regional airlines (NOCs, December 2025)
| Airline | Base |
| Shankh Air | Noida region |
| Al Hind Air | Kochi |
| FlyExpress | Telangana low-cost |
## 3. UDAN (Ude Desh ka Aam Nagrik)

### Sustainability in rice production
## 1. News
- ⚠ 2025: India overtook China as world's largest rice producer
- India supplies ~40% of global rice exports — over 20 million metric tonnes
## 2. Facts
- Staple for ~65% of Indians; ~25% of total cropped area
- Kharif crop: temperature >25°C, rainfall >100 cm
- Transplanted paddy: 25–27 irrigations/season; 1 kg rice = 3,000–4,000 litres water
- Punjab–Haryana water table fell ~30 ft → 80–200 ft; extraction exceeds recharge by 35–57%
- Rice = 10–20% of India's agricultural GHG emissions (methane); Punjab spends ₹39,000/ha on power and fertiliser subsidies
- Climate change may cut yields 6–10%
## 3. Solutions
- DSR (Direct Seeded Rice), AWD (Alternate Wetting and Drying — cuts methane), SRI, micro-irrigation under PMKSY
- ICAR NICRA programme; fortified rice under NFSA and PM-POSHAN
- ⚠ Flooded transplanted paddy increases methane (anaerobic)
## 4. Official figures
- Announced by Shivraj Singh Chouhan: India 150.18 million tonnes vs China 145.28 million tonnes
- 184 improved varieties of 25 crops by ICAR released — 122 cereals, 6 pulses, 13 oilseeds, 11 fodder, 6 sugarcane, 24 cotton–jute, 1 tobacco

### Trump's "rice dumping" claim — India's rice trade
## 1. News
- Claim: US President Donald Trump said India (and Thailand) are "dumping" rice in the US; tariffs could fix it — at a White House farmers' roundtable with Brooke Rollins and Scott Bessent
- ⚠ Not dumping — US imports premium aromatic rice at high prices
## 2. India's rice numbers
- Production 2024–25: 150 million tonnes — 28% of world; #1 producer and exporter
- Exports: 198.65 lakh tonnes worth $12.95 billion; about 40% of global rice trade; to 172 countries (+26 planned incl. Philippines, Indonesia, UK, Mexico)
- Export projection: 22.5 MT (2024–25) → 25 MT (2025–26)
- USA: 7.05 MT, #13 globally; exports 3 MT, imports 1.6 MT
- India's share in US rice imports: 26.1% (2024)
## 3. Markets
- Basmati: mainly West Asia — Saudi Arabia, Iraq, Iran, UAE; to US 2.74 lakh tonnes ($337.10m) of 60.65 lt
- Non-basmati: mainly West Africa — Benin, Togo, Côte d'Ivoire, Liberia, Sierra Leone, Guinea, Senegal
- LT Foods' "Royal" brand: 55% of North American basmati market

### Domestic savings reshaping India's capital markets
## 1. News
- Shift: domestic household savings replacing FPI as main source of market liquidity
- FPI holding: 15-month low of 16.9%; 24.1% in NIFTY 50 companies
- Retail investors: about 19% of market ownership — highest in 20+ years
- IPOs 2025: 71 mainboard IPOs raised over ₹1 lakh crore
- Corporate investment announcements FY25: +39% YoY; about 70% private sector
- Benefit: greater RBI monetary policy autonomy
## 2. Measures suggested
- SEBI (LODR) Regulations, 2015 — move to suitability-based selling
- Mutual Fund Sahi Hai campaign; National Strategy for Financial Education (NSFE)
## 3. Background
- Capital market: both primary and secondary markets
- Rupee: full current account convertibility; capital account only partial
### Bank of India raises ₹10,000 crore via infrastructure bonds
## 1. News
- Bank of India (BoI): raised ₹10,000 crore through infrastructure bonds; bids ₹15,300 crore vs base issue ₹5,000 crore
## 2. Infrastructure bonds
- RBI rule: bank infra bonds — minimum maturity 7 years (typical 10–15 years)
- Bank-issued bonds: enjoy CRR and SLR exemptions; reduce asset–liability mismatch
- Issuers: govt (NHAI), banks (PSBs dominate), institutions (IREDA, PFC, REC, IRFC), green bonds
| Aspect | Infrastructure Bonds | InvITs |
| Nature | Debt | Trust-based vehicle |
| Returns | Fixed interest | Market-linked |
| Tenure | Fixed | No fixed maturity |
| Regulation | RBI & SEBI | SEBI (InvIT Regulations, 2014) |
- ⚠ InvITs are recognised as borrowers under SARFAESI Act, 2002 (UPSC 2023)
### Bitcoin's slide after October record and crypto regulation
## 1. News
- Bitcoin: record $126,000 (October 2025) → below $85,000; about $1 trillion crypto value wiped out; fears of "crypto winter"
## 2. Reasons
- US Fed: cut rates by 25 bps on December 10 but signalled no further cuts
- 10/10 crash: October 10 — Trump threatened 100% tariffs on Chinese goods; $19 billion leveraged trades liquidated; $500 billion wiped out in hours
- Long-term holders: 800,000 Bitcoins sold within a month
- ⚠ Bitcoin behaves like a risk asset, not "digital gold"
## 3. Why winter fears may be exaggerated
- Halving: four-year cycle; last April 2024
- Bitcoin ETFs: approved in US January 2024; hold 1.4 million BTC (6.8% of supply)
- US policy: US Bitcoin Reserve executive order (March 2025); GENIUS Act (June 2025) on stablecoins
## 4. Crypto basics and India's status
- Bitcoin: 2009, Satoshi Nakamoto; capped at 21 million; Bitcoin Cash — hard fork 2017
| Year | India |
| 2018 | RBI banking ban |
| 2020 | Supreme Court lifted RBI ban |
| 2022 | 30% tax on gains + 1% TDS; Virtual Digital Assets, not legal tender; e₹ (CBDC) pilots |
| 2023 | Exchanges under FIU-IND (KYC/AML) |
- Legal tender abroad: El Salvador, Central African Republic; bans/restrictions: China, Russia
- Digital Rupee: 1 e₹ = 1 ₹; issued by RBI

### Rupee hits record low, rebounds on RBI intervention
## 1. News
- ⚠ All-time low: ₹89.41 per USD on 21 Nov 2025; rebounded to ₹89.22–89.23 (24–25 Nov)
## 2. Causes
- Record goods trade deficit: ~USD 42 bn (October); exports fell ~12%; imports record ~USD 76 bn (gold)
- FPI selling; Japanese yen carry-trade unwind
- RBI extended export proceeds realisation from 9 to 15 months
## 3. RBI support
- Forex reserves: ~USD 690 bn
- Net spot sales (Jan–Sep 2025): ~USD 20.1 bn; forward sales USD 53.4 bn (Aug) → 59.4 bn (Sep)
- ⚠ RBI does not target a level — only curbs volatility (managed float)
## 4. Background
| Term | Meaning |
| NEER | Rupee vs basket, no inflation adjustment — rise = appreciation |
| REER | Inflation-adjusted — ⚠ rise = loss of competitiveness |
| Trade deficit | Goods imports – exports only |
| CAD | Goods + services + income + transfers |
- Forex reserve components: FCA (largest), Gold, SDRs, Reserve Tranche Position; held by RBI, owned by GoI
