| Life-saving cancer drugs | Nil |
- Inverted duty structure to be corrected
- Revenue impact: ~₹48,000 crore; Revenue Secretary Arvind Shrivastava
- Decision: unanimous; Bihar Dy CM Samrat Choudhary, UP FM Suresh Khanna
## 2. Economic implications
- Slabs dropped: 12% and 28%; retained 0, 5, 18% + 40% demerit rate (some special rates below 5%)
- ⚠ 28% → 40% for some goods reflects merger of compensation cess, not a real hike
- Q1 nominal GDP: 8.8% vs 10.1% projected; direct taxes -4.3%
- Revenue foregone (with income tax reforms): ~₹1 lakh crore
- Called: "GST Bachat Utsav" by PM Modi
### GST 2.0 health-care reforms
## 1. Changes (effective 22 September 2025)
- Insurance: zero GST on individual health and life insurance (term, ULIP, endowment, family floater, senior citizen) and reinsurance
- Insurance penetration: India 3.7% of GDP vs world 6.8%
| Item | GST |
| Hospital rooms below ₹5,000/day | Exempt |
| Non-ICU rooms above ₹5,000/day | 5% without ITC |
| Life-saving drugs | 0% |
| Most medicines, devices, diagnostics | 5% (earlier 12–18%); CT scan machine 18% → 5% |
| Gyms, yoga, salons, wellness | 18% → 5% |
| Personal care (soap, shampoo, toothpaste) | 12–18% → 5% |
| Sugary drinks | 40% sin slab |
| Cigarettes | 28% + cess (effective 52–88%) |
### RBI MPC (29 Sept–1 Oct 2025) — preview
## 1. Facts
- Repo rate: 5.5% after 100 bps cuts (Feb–June 2025); stance neutral
- GDP Q1 FY26: 7.8% — highest in five quarters (vs 6.5% a year ago)
- CPI: 2.7% (August 2025) from 1.61% (July)
- US Fed: cut 25 bps to 4–4.25% — first cut of 2025
- US tariff on Indian goods: 50%
- Projections cited: FY26 GDP 6.5%; CPI ~2.6%; Q3 3.1%, Q4 4.4%, Q1 FY27 4.9%
### PM E-DRIVE — up to 100% subsidy for EV charging infrastructure
## 1. News
- Guidelines: released 28 September 2025 — up to 100% subsidy for EV charging, battery swapping and battery charging stations (upstream infrastructure + EVSE)
- Scheme: PM E-DRIVE, ₹10,000 crore (announced September 2024); ₹2,000 crore earmarked for charging infrastructure
- Eligible: Central ministries via CPSEs; PSUs — IOCL, BPCL, HPCL, NHAI, AAI, SAIL, CONCOR, CESL, metro corporations; State/UT nodal agencies (⚠ not only private firms)
- Priority: cities over 1 million (Census 2011), smart cities, satellite towns of 7 metros (Delhi, Mumbai, Kolkata, Chennai, Hyderabad, Bengaluru, Ahmedabad), State capitals, NCAP cities, highways (with MoRTH)
- Nodal ministry: Ministry of Heavy Industries (MHI)
### SEBI proposes FPIs in non-cash-settled commodity derivatives (gold, silver)
## 1. News
- Proposal: allow FPIs in non-cash settled, non-agricultural commodity derivatives — gold, silver, zinc, lead, base metals
- Aim: liquidity in far-month contracts, price discovery, domestic hedging
- FPI outflow: ~₹60,679 crore equities sold since July 2025
- Also: single automatic window for foreign investors
### Quality Control Orders (QCOs)
## 1. Framework
- Law: BIS Act, 2016 — Section 16 lets govt make Standard Mark compulsory
- Standards: ~23,000 BIS standards, most voluntary; QCO makes them mandatory
- Schemes: 10 Conformity Assessment Schemes (7 products; 1 each management systems, services, processes); Scheme-I most common
- Process: draft hosted on WTO TBT website; usually 6 months for compliance
## 2. Impact and criticism
- Hermetic compressors: <2 million (2021-22) → 8 million (2023-24); room ACs ~1 million → 12 million
### India to add 97 GW coal capacity by 2035
## 1. News
- Announced by: CEA and CERC — 97 GW new coal capacity by 2035 for grid stability
- Split: 70 GW in renewable-rich states, 27 GW elsewhere; plants to run till 2050 (⚠ not phased out in 2035)
- Nuclear: 8.8 GW now → 100 GW by 2047
- Pumped hydro: 4.7 GW → ~60 GW by 2033; additions 2.8 GW (2025), 2.6 GW (2026), 13 GW (2027); potential estimate 94 → 214 GW
### Battery storage at NTPC coal plants
## 1. News
- CEA tasked NTPC to test battery storage at coal plants for grid balancing as solar grows
- Tender: 1.7 GW battery storage across 11 coal plants
- Targets: 500 GW non-fossil capacity by 2030; add 97 GW coal by 2035 → total ~307 GW; net zero 2070
### SC directions to DISCOMs on regulatory assets
## 1. News
- Directions to SERCs and DISCOMs: clear existing regulatory assets in 4 years; new ones in 3 years; cap at 3% of Annual Revenue Requirement (ARR); audits
- Regulatory asset: gap when Average Cost of Supply (ACS) > ARR, deferred for later recovery with interest
| DISCOM | Regulatory assets |
| BSES Rajdhani (2022-23) | ₹36,057 crore |
| BSES Yamuna | ₹22,040 crore |
| Tamil Nadu (2021-22) | ₹89,375 crore |
| Punjab (2003-04) | ₹487 crore — ⚠ first documented case |
- FPPCA: Fuel and Power Purchase Cost Adjustment
- UK models: Regulated Asset Base (RAB); RIIO = Revenue = Incentives + Innovation + Outputs

### Landscape approach to India's green economy
## 1. Facts
- Bioeconomy: $10 billion (2014) → $165.7 billion (2024), 4.25% of GDP; 10,000+ start-ups; industrial (biofuels, bioplastics) 47%, pharma 35%
- Ethanol blending: 20% achieved; India 3rd largest pharma producer
- Concentration: Maharashtra, Karnataka, Telangana, Gujarat, Andhra Pradesh = two-thirds of bioeconomy value; East & NE under 6%
- Women: only 11% of rooftop solar jobs
- Projected green jobs: 35 million by 2030; 2.5 lakh PRIs
### 'Involution' in China's EV sector
## 1. Concept
- Involution (nèijuǎn): excessive competition → price wars below cost → losses
- Coined by: anthropologist Clifford Geertz (1969), "Agricultural Involution" (Java wet-rice)
## 2. China EV facts
- Tariffs: US 100% (from 2024); EU countervailing — BYD 17%, Geely 18.8%, SAIC ~35%; Turkiye 40%; Mexico considered 50%
- Response: "war on price wars"; draft Pricing Law overhaul (24 July 2025); orderly exit of capacity; MIIT
- BYD plants: Hungary, Turkiye; Chinese EVs = 75% of EV sales growth in developing countries (2024)
### GST two-rate structure proposal and state compensation
## 1. News
- GoM on Rate Rationalisation: chaired by Bihar Deputy CM Samrat Choudhary — accepted Centre's proposal
- Keep: 5% and 18%; remove 12% and 28%
- 99% of 12% items → 5%; 90% of 28% items → 18%
- Sin goods (tobacco, cigarettes, online real-money gaming): new 40% slab
- Compensation cess: to be abolished
- Final say: GST Council (chaired by Union Finance Minister); meeting expected September 2025
## 2. Background
- GST introduced: 2017; current slabs 5, 12, 18, 28% + cess
## 3. Revenue impact and compensation debate
- Average effective rate: ~11.5% → ~10%
- Revenue loss: ₹60,000–1,00,000 crore a year (0.2–0.3% of GDP); FY2025-26 ~₹45,000 crore
- 18% slab: ~70% of GST revenue — kept unchanged
- More affected: manufacturing/service states (Maharashtra, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu); 2018 rate cut caused 3–4% monthly dip
- Less affected: agrarian states (Bihar, UP, North-East)
- Old compensation: 5 years from July 2017, via compensation cess — ended June 2022
- ⚠ GST Council works by consensus — voting rare
### US 50% tariffs on Indian exports — sector impact
## 1. Worst-hit sectors
| Sector | Exports to US | US share | Tariff before → now |
| Shrimp | $2.4 billion (2024-25) | 32.4% | 10% → 60% |
| Diamonds, gold, jewellery | $10 billion | 40% | 2.1% → 52.1% |
| Textiles & apparel | $10.8 billion (apparel $5.4 bn) | ~35% apparel | 13.9% → 63.9% |
| Carpets | $1.2 billion | 58.6% | 2.9% → 52.9% |
| Organic chemicals | $2.7 billion | 13.2% | 4% → 54% |
- AP angle: shrimp prices in Andhra Pradesh fell ~20% after the earlier 25% tariff
- Surat: diamond polishing — ~12 lakh workers
- Hubs hit: Tiruppur, Noida–Gurugram, Ludhiana, Bengaluru
- RBI Governor: Sanjay Malhotra — ready with liquidity support
- Response: diversify markets; FTAs with UAE, Australia, EFTA
- ⚠ Organic chemicals' US share is only 13.2%, not one-third
### Solar module manufacturing at 100 GW — oversupply risk
## 1. News
- Report: SBI Capital Markets (SBICAPS), August 2025
- Module capacity: ~100 GW (2025); target 190 GW cumulative solar by 2027
- Solar capacity growth: over 4,000% in a decade
- FY25 installations: 24 GW, up 60% y-o-y (needing ~50 GWdc modules)
- Q1 FY26: 11 GW — highest ever in a quarter
- Imports FY25: ₹322 billion modules and cells (mostly China), down 38%
- Exports: grew 23-fold FY22–FY24; 97% to the US — hit by end of Inflation Reduction Act subsidies and 'One Big Beautiful Bill'
- Needed: 40–50 GW a year to 2030; 500 GW non-fossil by 2030

### India's dairy model — competitiveness and challenges
## 1. Facts
- India: largest milk producer in the world — smallholder, cooperative-led
| Item | India | Others |

| Yield per cow | 1.64 tonnes/year | US 10.97, EU 7.33, NZ 4.6 |
| Farmer share of retail price | 55–57% | US 35% |
| Corn price | ₹22–23/kg | US ₹15.4/kg |
- Amul (GCMMF): pays ₹44–45/litre cow milk, ₹65–66/litre buffalo milk
- Competitiveness from: low labour costs, ⚠ not high yields
- Limit: India lacks land for New Zealand-style pasture dairying
### India–US trade deal — dairy and agriculture hurdles
## 1. News

- Earlier: US withdrew India's GSP status in 2019
- India "the only country" in such elaborate talks with the US
- Alternatives to Hormuz route: Chabahar, INSTC

# MCQ NOTES OF APP TOPIC 6:36
### GST 2.0 (56th Council)
## Council and structure
- 56th GST Council (3 September 2025) — chaired by Nirmala Sitharaman; Revenue Secretary Arvind Shrivastava; Samrat Choudhary (Bihar), Suresh Khanna (UP)
- GST Council decides by consensus; voting rare
- Final decision on rate rationalisation lies with the GST Council (GoM accepted Centre's proposal)
- Slabs: 0%, 5%, 18% plus 40% demerit rate; 12% and 28% dropped
- 40% slab — sin goods: tobacco, cigarettes, online real-money gaming
- 28% → 40% shift shows merger of compensation cess; not a real hike in every case
- Cigarettes — 28% GST plus cess, effective burden 52–88%
- Average effective GST rate expected to fall from ~11.5% to ~10%
- Economic effect — fixes inverted duty structure
## Rates from 22 September 2025
- Nil GST — individual health and life insurance, reinsurance, life-saving cancer drugs
- Non-ICU hospital rooms above ₹5,000/day — 5% without ITC
- Revenue lost (with income-tax reforms) — about ₹1 lakh crore; PM called it "GST Bachat Utsav"
- States more affected — Maharashtra, Karnataka, Tamil Nadu (earlier rate cut → 3–4% monthly dip); Bihar, UP, North-East less affected
## Other
- "Your Capital, Your Right" — launched by Nirmala Sitharaman, 4 October 2025, Gandhinagar; partners RBI, SEBI, PFRDA
- "Digital arrest" frauds — impersonating police or customs officials
- Sitharaman on stablecoins — India must be ready to engage with them
- Draft Quality Control Orders — on WTO TBT (Technical Barriers to Trade) website; usually six months for compliance
- SEBI (August 2025) — regulated venue for pre-listing trading to replace the grey market
- 11% cotton import duty — removed until 30 September 2025; introduced in Budget 2021 to protect farmers
- Cotton — about six million farmers; 2024-25 production 294, requirement 318 lakh bales; 2021: 350 and 335 lakh bales
- South Asian integration triggers 2025 — Pahalgam terror attack and US reciprocal tariffs; suggestions: revive SAARC summits, BBIN Motor Vehicle Agreement

- Farmers get 55–57% of retail milk price; corn ₹15.4/kg is US price, India ₹22–23/kg
### Rupee hits record low, rebounds on RBI intervention
## 1. News
- ⚠ All-time low: ₹89.41 per USD on 21 Nov 2025; rebounded to ₹89.22–89.23 (24–25 Nov)
## 2. Causes
- Record goods trade deficit: ~USD 42 bn (October); exports fell ~12%; imports record ~USD 76 bn (gold)
- FPI selling; Japanese yen carry-trade unwind
- RBI extended export proceeds realisation from 9 to 15 months
## 3. RBI support
- Forex reserves: ~USD 690 bn
- Net spot sales (Jan–Sep 2025): ~USD 20.1 bn; forward sales USD 53.4 bn (Aug) → 59.4 bn (Sep)
- ⚠ RBI does not target a level — only curbs volatility (managed float)
## 4. Background
| Term | Meaning |
| NEER | Rupee vs basket, no inflation adjustment — rise = appreciation |
| REER | Inflation-adjusted — ⚠ rise = loss of competitiveness |
| Trade deficit | Goods imports – exports only |
| CAD | Goods + services + income + transfers |
- Forex reserve components: FCA (largest), Gold, SDRs, Reserve Tranche Position; held by RBI, owned by GoI
### Cabinet approves four rail multi-tracking projects (₹24,634 crore)
## 1. Projects
- Approved by: Union Cabinet — 4 projects, 18 districts in Maharashtra, MP, Gujarat, Chhattisgarh; adds ~894 km
- Cost: ₹24,634 crore; completion by 2030-31; under PM Gati Shakti National Master Plan
| Section | States |
| Itarsi–Bhopal–Bina | MP |
| Gondia–Dongargarh | Maharashtra–Chhattisgarh |
- Tourism: Sanchi, Satpura Tiger Reserve, Bhimbetka, Hazara Falls, Nawegaon National Park
## 2. Background — PM Gati Shakti
- Brings together 16 infrastructure ministries; subsumes National Infrastructure Pipeline (₹110 lakh crore, 2019)
- 11 industrial corridors, 2 defence corridors (Tamil Nadu, Uttar Pradesh); 17,000 km gas pipeline addition
- Logistics cost: ~13% of GDP
### Stablecoins — FM calls for engagement
- DAI — crypto-backed (Ethereum); USDT, USDC fiat-backed; TerraUSD algorithmic
- Consensus mechanisms — Proof of Work and Proof of Stake; public/private/consortium are blockchain types
### Semiconductors — pragmatic approach for India
- Semiconductor materials — Si, Ge, GaAs
- N-type — electrons as carriers; P-type — holes
### India's solar industry — third-largest producer
## 1. Data
- 2024-25 generation: 1,08,494 GWh — surpassed Japan (96,459 GWh); ⚠ India third after China and USA
- Module manufacturing capacity: 2 GW (2014) → ~100 GW (2025) (MNRE)
- Installed solar (September 2025): 117 GW; effective ~85 GW
- Solar parity with coal: around 2017
- Target: 500 GW non-fossil by 2030 — 250–280 GW solar; needs ~30 GW/year vs actual 17–23 GW
- Indian modules 1.5–2× costlier than Chinese; exported ~4 GW to US in 2024 vs China's 236 GW
## 2. Background — ISA
- International Solar Alliance: launched by India and France in 2015 at COP21; HQ Gurugram; 120+ members; aims $1 trillion by 2030
### Bharat International Rice Conference (BIRC) 2025
## 1. Event
- Venue/dates: Bharat Mandapam, New Delhi, 30–31 October 2025
- Govt (Dept of Commerce, APEDA) — non-financial support only
- Rice exports FY 2024–25: US$12.95 billion — ⚠ India's largest agricultural export
## 2. Background — APEDA
- APEDA Act, 1985 (operative 13 February 1986); Ministry of Commerce; HQ New Delhi; runs NPOP
- Agriculture Export Policy (2018); ODOP
### 'Koyla Shakti' — Smart Coal Analytics Dashboard
## 1. News
- Ministry of Coal to launch Koyla Shakti – Smart Coal Analytics Dashboard (SCAD) on 29 October 2025
- Integrates CIL, SCCL, private miners, Railways, Power, Ports ministries, state E-khanij platforms
- Earlier tools: Uttam App (quality), Mines Surveillance System, Coal Allocation Monitoring System (CAMS)
- Coal: ~55–60% of energy supply; ~70% of electricity
### Employability gap — academia–industry partnership
## 1. Key points
- Only 42.6% graduates employable; 12 million+ graduates/year
- Models: German dual system; India's NAPS (National Apprenticeship Promotion Scheme)
### 56th GST Council — two-tier GST of 5% and 18% (GST 2.0)
## 1. News
- Meeting: 56th GST Council, 3 September 2025, chaired by Nirmala Sitharaman — 10.5-hour meeting
- New slabs: 5% and 18% (earlier 0, 5, 12, 18, 28%) — effective 22 September 2025
- Sin/demerit goods: 40% (cigarettes, paan masala) — highest slab
- Compensation cess: continues on tobacco until loans repaid
- GST 2.0 slabs: 0/5/18/40% (12% and 28% abolished); compensation cess largely ended (stays on tobacco)
| Item | New GST |
| All TVs | 18% |
| Individual health insurance | Nil |
| Life-saving cancer drugs | Nil |
- Inverted duty structure to be corrected
- Revenue impact: ~₹48,000 crore; Revenue Secretary Arvind Shrivastava
- Decision: unanimous; Bihar Dy CM Samrat Choudhary, UP FM Suresh Khanna
## 2. Economic implications
- Slabs dropped: 12% and 28%; retained 0, 5, 18% + 40% demerit rate (some special rates below 5%)
- ⚠ 28% → 40% for some goods reflects merger of compensation cess, not a real hike
- Q1 nominal GDP: 8.8% vs 10.1% projected; direct taxes -4.3%
- Revenue foregone (with income tax reforms): ~₹1 lakh crore
- Called: "GST Bachat Utsav" by PM Modi
### SEBI proposes FPIs in non-cash-settled commodity derivatives (gold, silver)

- Single automatic window for foreign investors
### India to add 97 GW coal capacity by 2035
## 1. News
- Announced by: CEA and CERC — 97 GW new coal capacity by 2035 for grid stability
- Split: 70 GW in renewable-rich states, 27 GW elsewhere; plants to run till 2050 (⚠ not phased out in 2035)
- Nuclear: 8.8 GW now → 100 GW by 2047
- Pumped hydro: 4.7 GW → ~60 GW by 2033; additions 2.8 GW (2025), 2.6 GW (2026), 13 GW (2027); potential estimate 94 → 214 GW

### Landscape approach to India's green economy
## 1. Facts
- Bioeconomy: $10 billion (2014) → $165.7 billion (2024), 4.25% of GDP; 10,000+ start-ups; industrial (biofuels, bioplastics) 47%, pharma 35%
- Ethanol blending: 20% achieved; India 3rd largest pharma producer
- Concentration: Maharashtra, Karnataka, Telangana, Gujarat, Andhra Pradesh = two-thirds of bioeconomy value; East & NE under 6%
- Women: only 11% of rooftop solar jobs
- Projected green jobs: 35 million by 2030; 2.5 lakh PRIs
### GST two-rate structure proposal and state compensation
- ★ Maximum Indian exports (2018) — European Union
### US 50% tariffs on Indian exports — sector impact
## 1. Worst-hit sectors
| Sector | Exports to US | US share | Tariff before → now |
| Shrimp | $2.4 billion (2024-25) | 32.4% | 10% → 60% |
| Diamonds, gold, jewellery | $10 billion | 40% | 2.1% → 52.1% |
| Textiles & apparel | $10.8 billion (apparel $5.4 bn) | ~35% apparel | 13.9% → 63.9% |
| Carpets | $1.2 billion | 58.6% | 2.9% → 52.9% |
| Organic chemicals | $2.7 billion | 13.2% | 4% → 54% |
- AP angle: shrimp prices in Andhra Pradesh fell ~20% after the earlier 25% tariff
- Surat: diamond polishing — ~12 lakh workers
- Hubs hit: Tiruppur, Noida–Gurugram, Ludhiana, Bengaluru
- RBI Governor: Sanjay Malhotra — ready with liquidity support
- Response: diversify markets; FTAs with UAE, Australia, EFTA
- ⚠ Organic chemicals' US share is only 13.2%, not one-third
### Solar module manufacturing at 100 GW — oversupply risk
## 1. News
- Report: SBI Capital Markets (SBICAPS), August 2025
- Module capacity: ~100 GW (2025); target 190 GW cumulative solar by 2027
- Solar capacity growth: over 4,000% in a decade
- FY25 installations: 24 GW, up 60% y-o-y (needing ~50 GWdc modules)
- Q1 FY26: 11 GW — highest ever in a quarter
- Imports FY25: ₹322 billion modules and cells (mostly China), down 38%
- Exports: grew 23-fold FY22–FY24; 97% to the US — hit by end of Inflation Reduction Act subsidies and 'One Big Beautiful Bill'
- Needed: 40–50 GW a year to 2030; 500 GW non-fossil by 2030
## 2. Support schemes
| Scheme | Detail |
| PLI for High Efficiency Solar PV Modules | ₹24,000 crore — Tranche I ₹4,500 crore (8,737 MW), Tranche II ₹19,500 crore (39,600 MW) |
- ⚠ Exports went to the US, not the EU
### SEBI proposes regulated venue for pre-listing trades
## 1. News
- Proposal: a "regulated venue" for trading shares before IPO listing — replace the grey market
- Announced: at FICCI event, 21 August 2025 — SEBI Chairperson Tuhin Kanta Pandey, WTM Kamlesh Varshney
- Grey market terms: Grey Market Premium (GMP); Kostak rate (premium for selling IPO application rights)
- Aims: price discovery, tax compliance, investor protection
- Also: longer tenure for equity derivatives (now weekly and monthly expiry); crackdown on misleading finfluencers
- ⚠ Derivatives expire weekly/monthly, not quarterly
### Cotton import duty of 11% withdrawn till 30 September
## 1. News
- Announced: 18 August 2025 — 11% cotton import duty withdrawn until 30 September 2025
- Duty introduced: Union Budget 2021 (FM Nirmala Sitharaman) to protect farmers
- Earlier exemption: April 2022 — till 30 Sept 2022, extended to 31 Oct 2022
## 2. Data
| Item | Detail |
| Cotton farmers | ~six million |
| 2024-25 production | 294 lakh bales — lowest in 15 years; need 318 lakh |
| 2021 production / need | 350 / 335 lakh bales |
| Imports | up 107.4% — $579.2 million (FY24) → $1.20 billion (FY25) (GTRI) |
| Import sources | Australia ($258.2 m), US ($234.1 m), Brazil ($180.8 m), Egypt ($116.3 m) |
| CCI MSP buying | 100 lakh bales, ₹37,500 crore; sold 73 lakh |
| MSP 2025-26 | up 8%; season starts 1 October |
- Industry asks: suspend duty April–September; 5% interest subvention for mills
### India's dairy model — competitiveness and challenges
## 1. Facts
- India: largest milk producer in the world — smallholder, cooperative-led
| Item | India | Others |
| Farmgate cow milk price | ₹34/litre (Maharashtra, 3.5% fat, 8.5% SNF) | US ₹36.7; Europe ₹55.6 |
| Yield per cow | 1.64 tonnes/year | US 10.97, EU 7.33, NZ 4.6 |
| Farmer share of retail price | 55–57% | US 35% |
| Corn price | ₹22–23/kg | US ₹15.4/kg |
- Amul (GCMMF): pays ₹44–45/litre cow milk, ₹65–66/litre buffalo milk
- Competitiveness from: low labour costs, ⚠ not high yields
- Limit: India lacks land for New Zealand-style pasture dairying
### India–US trade deal — dairy and agriculture hurdles
## 1. News
- Deadline: 9 July 2025 — US to reimpose reciprocal tariffs (back to 2 April levels) if no deal
- Sticking points: agriculture and dairy (small producers; hormones in US milk)
- Plan: a "mini trade deal" then first tranche of Bilateral Trade Agreement (BTA) by Fall 2025
- Earlier: US withdrew India's GSP status in 2019
- India "the only country" in such elaborate talks with the US
- Alternatives to Hormuz route: Chabahar, INSTC

### Union Budget 2026-27 — sector announcements
## 1. Manufacturing, pharma and strategic sectors
| Announcement | Detail |
| BioPharma Shakti | ₹10,000 crore over 5 years — make India a global BioPharma manufacturing hub |
| NIPERs | 3 new National Institutes of Pharmaceutical Education and Research; upgrade existing 7 |
| Clinical trials | Network of 1,000+ accredited clinical trial centres |
| India Semiconductor Mission 2.0 | Equipment, raw materials, indigenous IP; industry-led R&D and skill centres |
| Electronics components manufacturing scheme | Allocation raised to ₹40,000 crore |
| Rare Earth corridors | Odisha, Kerala, Andhra Pradesh, Tamil Nadu |
| Speciality chemical parks | Support states to set up 3 |
| Hi-tech tool rooms | 2, through CPSEs |
| Container manufacturing scheme | Over ₹10,000 crore over 5 years |
| Construction and infrastructure equipment | New manufacturing scheme |
- ⚠ Andhra Pradesh is one of the four Rare Earth corridor states
## 2. Textiles, MSMEs and local enterprise
- National Fibre Scheme: self-reliance in natural, synthetic and advanced fibres
- Textile expansion and employment scheme: modernise traditional clusters; mega textile parks for technical textiles
- Legacy industrial clusters: special scheme to rejuvenate 200
- SME Growth Fund: ₹10,000 crore — create "champion SMEs"
- Atmanirbhar Bharat Fund: additional ₹2,000 crore
- Corporate Mitras: with professional institutions in Tier-2 and Tier-3 cities
## 3. Infrastructure and connectivity
- Capital expenditure: raised to ₹12.2 lakh crore (2026-27)
- Infrastructure Risk Guarantee Fund: to be established
- Gives partial credit guarantees to lenders
- REITs: to recycle CPSE real estate assets
- Dedicated freight corridor: new — Dankuni to Surat
- National Waterways: 20 new over 5 years
- River vessel repair ecosystems: Varanasi and Patna
- Coastal cargo share target: 12% by 2047
- High-speed rail: 7 high-speed rail corridors to be developed
- CCUS: ₹20,000 crore over 5 years for Carbon Capture, Utilisation and Storage technologies
- City Economic Region: ₹5,000 crore over 5 years for each
## 4. Banking and financial sector
- Restructure: Power Finance Corporation (PFC) and Rural Electrification Corporation (REC)
### Union Budget 2026-27 — tax proposals
## 1. Direct tax and compliance
- New Income Tax Act: comes into force from April 2026; simplified rules and forms to be notified, redesigned for common citizens
- Motor Accident Claims Tribunal: interest awarded to a natural person exempt; TDS on it removed
- Return revision deadline: extended from 31 December to 31 March (nominal fee)
- Foreign Asset Disclosure Scheme: one-time, 6-month, for small taxpayers
- Updated returns: allowed even after reassessment begins — additional 10% tax
- Prosecution immunity: retrospective from 1 October 2024 for undisclosed non-immovable foreign assets below ₹20 lakh
- Form 15G/15H: single-window filing through depositories
- Lower/nil TDS certificate: rules-based automated process for small taxpayers
- Decriminalised: non-submission of books/documents; TDS where payment is in kind
## 2. TCS, STT and buyback changes
| Item | Old | New |
| TCS — foreign tour packages | 2%–20% | 2% |
| TCS — LRS for education and medical | 5% | 2% |
| TCS — tendu leaves | 5% | 2% |
| TCS — liquor, scrap, minerals sellers | — | 2% |
| STT — futures | 0.02% | 0.05% |
| STT — options premium and exercise | — | 0.15% |
- Buybacks: taxed as capital gains for all shareholders — effective tax 22% (corporate promoters), 30% (non-corporate promoters)
- MAT credit: old credit set-off only for companies moving to the new regime; capped at one-fourth
## 3. IT services and transfer pricing
- Safe harbour margin (IT services): 15.5%; threshold raised from ₹300 crore to ₹2,000 crore; approvals automated, valid up to 5 years
- Unilateral APAs (IT services): target to conclude within 2 years, extendable by 6 months; modified return facility extended to associated enterprises
## 4. Data centres, bonded zones and non-residents
- Data centres: tax holiday up to 2047 for foreign companies providing cloud services to global customers through Indian data centres
- Related-entity data centre services from India — 15% safe harbour on costs
- Component warehousing (bonded warehouses): safe harbour margin 2% of invoice value (tax about 0.7%)
- Toll manufacturing in bonded zones: 5-year income-tax exemption to non-residents supplying capital goods/equipment/tooling
- Non-resident experts: global income exempt for a 5-year stay under notified schemes
- MAT exemption: for non-residents paying tax on a presumptive basis
- ICDS: joint committee of Ministry of Corporate Affairs and CBDT to merge ICDS into Ind AS; separate ICDS accounting removed from 2027-28
- Safe harbour rules: definition of "accountant" simplified
## 5. Cooperatives
- Cooperatives: deduction extended to societies supplying cattle feed and cotton seeds; 3-year dividend exemption for notified National Cooperative Federations (investments up to 31 January 2026)
### Budget history and traditions
## 1. Firsts and records
| Fact | Answer |
| First Budget (British India) | 7 April 1860 — James Wilson |
| First Budget of independent India | 26 November 1947 — R.K. Shanmukham Chetty |
| First Budget of Republic | John Mathai |
| Most Budgets | Morarji Desai — 10 |
| First woman to present | Indira Gandhi (1970-71) — also PM |
| Most Budgets by a woman FM | Nirmala Sitharaman — 9 |
| Longest speech (time) | Nirmala Sitharaman, 1 February 2020 — 2 h 42 min |
| Longest speech (words) | Manmohan Singh, 1991 — 18,650 words |
| Shortest Budget | H.M. Patel, 1977 — about 800 words |
| First paperless Budget | 1 February 2021 (FY 2021-22) — Nirmala Sitharaman |
- 1947 Budget: covered 4 months to 31 March 1948; expenditure ₹171.15 crore; fiscal deficit ₹24.59 crore
- ⚠ Liaquat Ali Khan earlier presented the 1947-48 Budget for the Interim Government
- Other counts: P. Chidambaram 9, Pranab Mukherjee 8, Yashwant Sinha 8, Manmohan Singh 6
- Morarji Desai: presented on his birthday 29 February in 1964 and 1968
- PMs who presented: Jawaharlal Nehru, Indira Gandhi, Rajiv Gandhi
## 2. Changes over time
- Time: 5 PM until 1999; Yashwant Sinha moved it to 11 AM
- Date: last working day of February until 2016; 1 February from 2017 (Arun Jaitley)
- Railway Budget: separate from 1924; merged in 2017 (Arun Jaitley)
- Printing: Rashtrapati Bhavan until 1950 → after 1950 leak, Minto Road → North Block basement (press from 1980)
- Bahi-Khata: 2019 — Nirmala Sitharaman replaced the briefcase
- Halwa ceremony: North Block ground floor, marks start of printing about 10 days before; staff under 24-hour Intelligence Bureau watch

- MAT credit: old credit set-off only for companies moving to the new regime; capped at one-fourth
## 3. IT services and transfer pricing
- Safe harbour margin (IT services): 15.5%; threshold raised from ₹300 crore to ₹2,000 crore; approvals automated, valid up to 5 years
- Unilateral APAs (IT services): target to conclude within 2 years, extendable by 6 months; modified return facility extended to associated enterprises
## 4. Data centres, bonded zones and non-residents
- Data centres: tax holiday up to 2047 for foreign companies providing cloud services to global customers through Indian data centres
- Related-entity data centre services from India — 15% safe harbour on costs
- Component warehousing (bonded warehouses): safe harbour margin 2% of invoice value (tax about 0.7%)
- Toll manufacturing in bonded zones: 5-year income-tax exemption to non-residents supplying capital goods/equipment/tooling
- Non-resident experts: global income exempt for a 5-year stay under notified schemes
- MAT exemption: for non-residents paying tax on a presumptive basis
- ICDS: joint committee of Ministry of Corporate Affairs and CBDT to merge ICDS into Ind AS; separate ICDS accounting removed from 2027-28
- Safe harbour rules: definition of "accountant" simplified
## 5. Cooperatives
- Cooperatives: deduction extended to societies supplying cattle feed and cotton seeds; 3-year dividend exemption for notified National Cooperative Federations (investments up to 31 January 2026)
### Budget history and traditions
## 1. Firsts and records
| Fact | Answer |
| First Budget (British India) | 7 April 1860 — James Wilson |
| First Budget of independent India | 26 November 1947 — R.K. Shanmukham Chetty |
| First Budget of Republic | John Mathai |
| Most Budgets | Morarji Desai — 10 |
| First woman to present | Indira Gandhi (1970-71) — also PM |
| Most Budgets by a woman FM | Nirmala Sitharaman — 9 |
| Longest speech (time) | Nirmala Sitharaman, 1 February 2020 — 2 h 42 min |
| Longest speech (words) | Manmohan Singh, 1991 — 18,650 words |
| Shortest Budget | H.M. Patel, 1977 — about 800 words |
| First paperless Budget | 1 February 2021 (FY 2021-22) — Nirmala Sitharaman |
- 1947 Budget: covered 4 months to 31 March 1948; expenditure ₹171.15 crore; fiscal deficit ₹24.59 crore
- ⚠ Liaquat Ali Khan earlier presented the 1947-48 Budget for the Interim Government
- Other counts: P. Chidambaram 9, Pranab Mukherjee 8, Yashwant Sinha 8, Manmohan Singh 6
- Morarji Desai: presented on his birthday 29 February in 1964 and 1968
- PMs who presented: Jawaharlal Nehru, Indira Gandhi, Rajiv Gandhi
## 2. Changes over time
- Time: 5 PM until 1999; Yashwant Sinha moved it to 11 AM
- Date: last working day of February until 2016; 1 February from 2017 (Arun Jaitley)
- Railway Budget: separate from 1924; merged in 2017 (Arun Jaitley)
- Printing: Rashtrapati Bhavan until 1950 → after 1950 leak, Minto Road → North Block basement (press from 1980)
- Bahi-Khata: 2019 — Nirmala Sitharaman replaced the briefcase
- Halwa ceremony: North Block ground floor, marks start of printing about 10 days before; staff under 24-hour Intelligence Bureau watch
